DAWN OF DECAY — Changelog

Newest first. The Master Document holds current truth; this records what changed and when. (Earlier history lives in the retired Implementation Addendum.)

2026-08-07 — Batch AT: the Arcanist — escalation (and Shatter, re-specced)

Third of the Mage three, and the class is done. His tree was already purpose-designed — Resonance / Overload / Control, all 24 ids intact — so this batch is not a restructure. It is a SPINE: nothing early, everything late. Three things stopped him escalating, and all three are gone.

  • §1 ARCANE RESONANCE → RUNAWAY RESONANCE. Three clauses, and the middle one is the whole batch.
    • No ceiling. Resonance has no maximum, builds 1 per damaging cast and 2 on a crit, and nothing removes it — it persists to the end of the battle. second_max is a sentinel 99 nothing is meant to reach.
    • The curve compounds. The damage bonus is 1.5% × N(N+1)/2 and the damage-taken penalty is 0.75% × N(N+1)/2, both uncapped. Compounding rather than linear is the design: a linear per-stack term is a slope, a triangular one is a curve, and that is the mathematical difference between a ramp and an escalation. The worked numbers, so this can be checked rather than trusted — 5 stacks +22%/+11%, 8 +54%/+27%, 12 +117%/+59%, 16 +204%/+102%. Note what that says: at five stacks he is WEAKER than he was (+25% taken for less damage), and by twelve he has roughly doubled. That reshape is the point.
    • +1% critical chance per stack, LINEAR — one stable, readable term beside two that compound, so the tooltip has something a player can hold in their head. (It was +3%/stack with a talent deepening it; the talent's counter is rune-only now and still pays +1%/rank.)
    SHIPPED COMPOUNDING, NOT THE LINEAR FALLBACK. §1 offered a stated fallback (keep the cap removed, make both terms linear at 2%/1%) if the kit smoke came out absurd. It did not — see the smoke numbers below — so the fantasy ships as authored. THE CURVE HAS EXACTLY ONE IMPLEMENTATION, BattleUnit.resonance_curve(), and battle.gd reads it through two named sites (_resonance_dmg_mult / _resonance_taken_mult). The nameplate reads the same method directly, so the plate, the ability tooltip and the damage path cannot disagree.
  • THE READ SITES, one line each. _resonance_dmg_mult → the attacker block in _resolve and the ability tooltip's buff_mult. _resonance_taken_mult → the target block in _resolve. Crit → the crit-chance block, off the RAW stack count (linear, so it does not go through the curve at all). The nameplate → refresh_bars, which prints the stack count and both live percentages.
  • §2 THE PER-STACK TERMS COME OFF ARCANE CANNON AND MAGI'S WRATH, AND THIS IS THE TRAP THE BATCH EXISTS AROUND. Cannon lost +7.5% damage per stack and Wrath lost +4% per stack. Why: the passive compounds now, so an ability-side per-stack term multiplies a curve by a slope and SQUARES the escalation. At 12 stacks a Cannon would have been 2.17 × 1.90 = 4.1× its base instead of 2.17×. Both keep their Break terms (5 × stacks and 2.5 × stacks) because Break is a different axis, and Cannon keeps its 15% recoil. test_batch_at asserts both removals in the source AND live, and a negative control restoring Cannon's term trips 2 checks.
  • §2 STABILIZE LEAVES THE OPENING THREE; DEATH RAY COMES OUT OF THE VAULT. Stabilize was the escape hatch — it vents the very stacks the spec exists to build, and Master of Moments made venting free, which made it the most anti-escalation node in the game. It joins SPEC_POOLS["arcanist"] so a player who wants the safety valve can earn it: a choice, not a default. Its def moved into trimmed_kit_ability, so exactly ONE copy exists (the AK resolver rule), and it stays spec-only because it reads Resonance (AH's curation rule). Death Ray takes its slot: 40 Mana, 5.0 initiative, 3cd, 150% of Attack as arcane, single target, unusable below 5 Resonance, and it consumes no stacks (it builds one, like every damaging cast — "consumes nothing" is not "is not a cast"). A button dark for four turns and then the only thing he wants to press. Losing Stabilize costs him his Mana valve as well as his defence, and that is intended — his Mana comes from being hurt now: Conversion, Feedback Loop and Siphon in the Entropy lane. ONE INTERPRETATION STATED RATHER THAN GLOSSED: §1 says nothing removes Resonance and §2 keeps Stabilize earnable, which vents it. Those describe different things — the passive has no decay, no cap and no reset, and an earned Stabilize is the deliberate exception a player buys. Both ship as written.
  • §3 ALL 24 NODES RE-AUTHORED AT ROW PRICING. EVERY ID SURVIVES AND RE-SPECS IN PLACE, so saved picks migrate and NO SAVE VERSION MOVES (still v7). The Control lane is renamed ENTROPY — after Batch AS "Control" is the Cryomancer's identity word, a sibling lane wearing it reads as a collision, and the lane was never about control anyway. Resonance and Overload keep their names. THE FULL OLD → NEW MAPPING, by closest surviving intent:
    idwasis nowlane movenote

    ONE FORCED ASSIGNMENT AND ONE NEAR ONE, both reported rather than hidden (AR did this for Ashes of Al'ar, AS for Second Prison, and both reports were worth more than a tidy table): ar_still → Backlash outright, and ar_mindfulness → Terminal Velocity on the thin thread of "both are about cooldowns". Nothing needed a new subsystem: every design above is a stat counter plus one conditional at an existing read site, and Death Ray's gate is one line in _ability_usable beside Stabilize's.
  • ARCANE WARD AND STILL MIND ARE GONE AS DESIGNS — two mitigation nodes in a lane about converting danger into fuel, and Still Mind modified an ability that is no longer in the kit. MAGNITUDES ARE ADDITIVE, NOT RANKED: every counter writes its own magnitude in the units its read site sums (conversion_ranks 30 = percentage POINTS, stable_ranks 25 IS the cap, cannoneer_ranks 4 is the INCREASE on Cannon's 5 BD/stack). conduit_step is a FLOAT and is deliberately NOT named _ranks — Runes.STAT_INT_KEYS coerces anything ending that way, and 0.5 would have become 0: the node silently inert, nothing crashing. The test asserts the name as well as the type.
  • DELETED WITH THEIR PREMISE, not left unreachable: UNLIMITED POWER and BACKLASH WARD. Both existed only to answer "what happens when the ramp runs out of room", and the answer is now that it never does. The unlimited status went with them. UNREACHABLE BUT KEPT (the AR vault pattern, gated > 0, reported so a later batch can re-node or delete deliberately): mindfulness_ranks, arcane_mastery_ranks and critical_mass_ranks are RUNE-ONLY and their read sites are kept on purpose; mana_attune_ranks and still_mind_ranks have no writer at all — the first because Siphon replaced it, the second because Stabilize is earnable and still reads its floor.
  • §4 THE RUNE AUDIT. RE-POINTED:
    • Rune of the Resonant Core — conduit_ranks → conduit_step: 0.5, and its desc rewritten, because BOTH its clauses lost their meaning: there is no maximum to raise and no linear per-stack term to deepen. It pays the node's own 0.5 points of curve step now, and resonant_core_ranks still pays into the node's new meaning (+1 on the first cast of a turn). THE ARITHMETIC, REPORTED NOT BURIED — its old "+2% per stack" paid +10% at 5 stacks, +16% at 8, +24% at 12; 0.5 points of curve pays +7.5%, +18%, +39%. It crosses over around eight stacks: worse early, better late, which is this batch's whole shape. Rune magnitude has been closed since AF, so that is a report, not a retune.
    • Rune of the Unquiet Mind — feedback_ranks 2 → 20, because the counter is percentage POINTS now and 2 would have silently gutted it from "a fifth of Cannon's recoil" to a fiftieth. Its lane tag Control → Entropy, the AS Honed Lance lesson: a dead lane name leaves a rune homeless in the bot's build policy and the per-lane coverage test. Its mindfulness_ranks clause is now the ONLY thing that writes that counter, and its read site is kept for exactly that reason.
    • Rune of the Wide Current — on_edge_ranks → its own rune_on_edge_ranks term (the AR Cinder Trail pattern, because the node took the counter for new units). It reproduces the OLD formula exactly — threshold 20 + 5/rank, one stack per rank — so it still pays its advertised "surviving a blow under 25% health grants a stack". The threshold takes the MAX and the payout SUMS, so each half pays its number alone AND stacked (the AK/AL repair rule); summing thresholds would have produced a 60% window neither half asked for. Its other two clauses are untouched and still pay their advertised amounts, because their read sites were deliberately kept.
    • Rune of the Seventh Bolt — unchanged. Arcane Barrage is unchanged and still in the kit.
    THE THREE MAGE CLASS-WIDE RUNES TOUCH NO ARCANIST COUNTER (Wellspring, Reckless Channeling, Comet) — asserted in the test, not assumed. runes.json changed by three payload re-points and two descs; this is not the magnitude pass the designer closed in AF.
  • §5 THE ar_ward ↔ ar_still EXCLUSIVE PAIR DISSOLVES with the two designs, and the entry is removed from CLAUDE.md's prose with the reason recorded beside it rather than quietly vanishing. Note the correction AS already logged and this batch confirms: test_runes._exclusives was retired to a bare pass by Batch AI, so the pair list has only ever been prose since. The remaining four pairs are untouched.
  • §6 THE BOT: NEVER COME DOWN. The old mage rotation vented at 4+ stacks below 80% health — the exact opposite of the spec that shipped, and against an ability that is no longer in the opening three. Now: Death Ray the moment the gate opens (it consumes nothing, so there is no reason to save it) → Magi's Wrath at 3+ enemies → Overcharge once, at 8+ stacks, because it pays half of what he holds and spending it early throws the button away → Cannon → Barrage → Arcane Explosion as the free filler that keeps building. Stabilize is deliberately absent, and the code says so: if a player earns it, it is theirs to press, but the bot measuring the spec must never vent.
  • §8 SHATTER, RE-SPECCED — TIME HELD, NOT STACKS HELD (Cryomancer). AS reported it never firing and correctly called that a design tension rather than a bot bug: Shatter (Thaw) and Absolute Zero (Deep Freeze) are both capstones and only one is takeable, so a Shatter build holds at most two — and at a limit of one, Shatter and Ice Lance did the same job and the Lance was cheaper. The bot preferred the Lance correctly. Fixed by changing what it scales on. Shatter — 30 Mana, 4.0, 5cd, releases every hold, and each released enemy takes 10% of Attack per turn it spent held, capped at 12 (Perfect: cooldown 4, as before). This resolves the tension without touching either capstone: at three turns held it is worth less than Ice Lance's 35% plus its stack bonus, the crossover sits around turn four or five, and past that the Lance can never match it. Multiple holds still multiply it, so Absolute Zero and Second Prison builds still want it — they are no longer required for it to be worth a capstone. The hold stops being a binary state and becomes a CHARGE, which is right for a spec whose currency is time, and it gives the player a real question every turn: release now for a modest hit, or hold longer while the enemy sits off the board doing nothing.
  • §8 IMPLEMENTATION. The counter is BattleUnit.hold_turns, written in exactly one place — _hold_sync, at the top of every turn, walking _holds. It is deliberately NOT a dictionary parked beside _holds: that would be a second answer to "is this held" and the two could disagree. Because only the ledger walk writes it, a counter can never advance on an enemy that is not in a prison, and it advances once per turn, not once per unit per turn — a negative control nesting the increment inside a second walk over _holds trips the test. It zeroes at _hold_freeze, so an enemy released and re-frozen does not resume its old charge. The nameplate HELD chip shows the count ("HELD 7") and its tooltip says what the number buys, because a charge the player cannot see is a decision they cannot make. THE BOT: hold longer, and prefer Shatter over Ice Lance once the oldest hold has charged five turns — the Shatter check moved ABOVE the Lance release, and the _holds.size() >= 2 gate that made it never fire is gone.
  • §8 ONE CONSEQUENCE, INTENDED AND RECORDED: the hold is simultaneously a damage window for the party and a charging Shatter, so the party spending the window kills the target and the charge with it. That tension is the good kind — it makes "leave it held one more turn" an actual argument rather than a free choice. It is deliberately NOT resolved by preserving the charge on death; there is nothing to shatter.
  • ONE ITEM THE BRIEF DID NOT SPECIFY, FLAGGED RATHER THAN INVENTED: Death Ray carries no Break damage. §2 gives its Mana, initiative, cooldown, damage, target count and gate precisely and says nothing about BD, so it ships at pressure: 0 — the same shape Cannon and Wrath declare before their stack-based Break lands. A 40-Mana nuke contributing nothing to the party's Break is a real design question; it is the designer's, not this batch's.

VERIFIED: check_parse 0 failures, check_flow 0 failures (6 screens). NEW test_batch_at.gd 457 checks / 0 failures, STABLE 4/4. Regression: test_batch_an 6044/0, test_batch_ah 5494/0 (its master.html STAMP GATE bumped AS → AT, and its verbatim Arcanist pool assertion updated with the batch), ah_battle 65/0, ai 2036/0, aj 403/0, ak 524/0 (was 523 — the Arcanist's spec pool gained Stabilize, and every entry still resolves), al 557/0 (was 556 — same reason: it audits every spec pool entry too), ar 885/0, as 387/0, test_runes 2981/0 (was 2982 — the Resonant Core rune's payload went int → float, so one STAT_INT_KEYS-family check no longer applies, exactly AL's precedent), test_rune_battle 94/0, run-harness gates 1/2/3 PASS, 11 scenes 0 SCRIPT ERROR.

KNOWN-BAD, NOT OURS: test_batch_al's standing flake reproduced 2 runs of 4 here, and it is the same check AQ named and diagnosed — "Spite reflects damage at the attacker", a harness race in test_batch_al's own _spawn (it waits a fixed 20 process_frames while the battle's _run_battle loop is advancing on REAL timers, then drives _resolve by hand). Nothing in this batch touches the Warden tree or a block/taunt battle. Do not read a clean run as evidence it is gone.

TWO TEST RE-POINTS, IN PLACE AND WITH THE REASON IN THE FILE rather than deleted: test_runes' Batch AA call-site ordering alarm probed resonant_core_ranks as a CEILING field, and Runaway Resonance has no ceiling for it to raise — so the Resonance probe is inverted (it now asserts no Resonance ceiling is being derived at all, which is the thing a future batch could break) and the "runes actually write a ceiling" floor drops 4 → 3 with the three survivors NAMED so it cannot be lowered again by attrition. The RULE is untouched and still live for Mercy and Focus. test_batch_as's exclusive-pair prose assertion was already written to expect the AS dissolution note; the AT one is added the same way.

NEGATIVE CONTROLS RUN, because a test that cannot fail proves nothing: making the curve LINEAR instead of triangular trips 34 checks; restoring Arcane Cannon's per-stack damage term trips 2; putting Shatter back on Chilled stacks trips 1; capping the damage-taken curve at 5 stacks trips 1; and nesting _hold_sync's increment inside a second walk over _holds (once per unit per unit) trips 1.

KIT SMOKE — and §1's fallback decision rests on it. Fixed lineup, 40 battles/row, berserker,arcanist,inquisitor,beastmaster, DOD_SIM_TALENTS force-learning full 8-node builds. All four rows 40/40 wins, 0 SCRIPT ERROR, and every new piece appears in the rotation (Death Ray 0.7–1.1 casts/battle in every row, Overcharge 0.8, Magi's Wrath 1.0). Damage share and Break: ungeared 47%/72 BD; RESONANCE 60%/79; OVERLOAD 56%/54; ENTROPY 55%/85.

THE FINDING WORTH KEEPING, and it is a property of compounding itself: BUILD RATE BEATS PER-STACK VALUE, quadratically. The Resonance lane is the standout (526 damage/battle against Overload's 371) even though Overload is the lane whose entire pitch is "each stack worth more". The reason is the curve: damage is step × N(N+1)/2, so it is QUADRATIC in the stack count and only LINEAR in the step. Doubling the build rate roughly quadruples the payout; raising the step from 1.5% to 2% multiplies it by 1.33. Any future tuning of this spec should treat the Resonance lane's build-rate nodes as the expensive ones — that is not obvious from reading the node text, and it is the opposite of how the two lanes are pitched.

SHIPPED AS AUTHORED — COMPOUNDING, NOT THE LINEAR FALLBACK. §1 asked for the smoke numbers and a decision. 60% at the tallest lane is in the same band as content already shipped and flagged rather than nerfed (AR's Kindling row read 66%, AS's Thaw 51%), the spec loses no battles, and nothing came out absurd — so the fallback was not taken. MEASURED AND FLAGGED, NOT NERFED, per the standing rule. Kit-mechanics ratios ONLY: the smoke lineup carries NO difficulty signal (Batch R), and these magnitudes are this batch's own.

NO DIFFICULTY MEASUREMENT AND NO SIM ROW, deliberately — same reasoning as AJ, AK, AL, AR and AS: a single-spec re-author has no honest control row against a fixed four-hero party, and ./sim.sh carries no difficulty signal (Batch R).

2026-08-07 — Batch AS: the Cryomancer — control

Second of the Mage three. His structure was sound and his middle lane was a damage lane, which made a control spec a damage spec wearing a coat. The spine is now: you decide WHEN the enemy acts. Not how hard — when.

  • §0 FIRST, BECAUSE THE WHOLE BATCH RESTS ON IT — AND THE ANSWER IS MOSTLY GOOD NEWS. The scheduler HAS always called effective_speed(). Every line in battle.gd that advances a unit's place on the timeline — the turn loop, the stun and freeze branches, the debug turn lock, the enemy-skip toggle, the post-cast reschedule, delay_push, the turn bar's own preview — divides by effective_speed(), and always has. Chilled has been slowing enemies since it was written, and every doc claiming so was right. The one hole was exactly the one already confirmed: the OPENING ROLL. u.next_time = (100.0 / u.speed) * randf_range(0.0, 1.0) read the raw stat, so the comment above _apply_battle_modifier() — which promises modifiers are stamped before the roll specifically so Frenzied is on the board before next_time seeds off speed — has been wrong since Batch AN. Frenzied (mod_speed_mult) and Hoarfrost (Chilled) both live in effective_speed(), which that line never called, so neither did anything to the first turn order. Fixed: the seed reads effective_speed(), floored at 0.1 because a modifier is allowed to stack with a deep chill and 100/0 is not a turn order. The Hunter's next_time = -0.01 override is untouched.
  • PERMAFROST → GLACIAL HOLD. One mechanic in three clauses. Permafrost: his Chilled stacks never expire (unchanged, and the reason the accumulation feels like control). The hold: a Frozen enemy stays Frozen indefinitely — it leaves the initiative bar entirely and is released only when he chooses, by Ice Lance, by Shatter, or by freezing past his hold limit (which frees the oldest). Nothing else thaws it: not ally damage, not his own Blizzard, not time. He holds ONE enemy, two with Second Prison, any number under Absolute Zero; a released enemy comes back on 1 stack so the engine stays warm. The window: a held enemy takes +15% damage from all sources. Clauses 2 and 3 together are the design — the hold is simultaneously denial and a party-wide damage window he opens and closes, so his control is a team resource rather than a solo trick.
  • A named release with no accidental thaws is a deliberate choice over the alternative (any single-target hit from him breaks it). The alternative is more physical and much less legible, and it makes his own Blizzard a liability. Three things follow that the brief did not enumerate, and all three are decisions rather than side effects. (a) A CLEANSING RITE CANNOT REACH A HOLD. Frozen is in DEBUFF_IDS and the rite strips the longest-remaining debuff first — and a battle-long freeze reads as 999 turns, so a mender warband would have taken the hold every single time and the indefinite prison would be worth strictly LESS than the old one-turn freeze. That removes the enemy's only answer to a hold, which is what the hold limit and the boss carve-out are the price for. (b) A held enemy is taken OFF THE TIMELINE (next_time = INF) rather than losing a turn each round — otherwise the log would fill with "frozen solid and loses their turn" forever and its cooldowns would keep ticking while it sat helpless. (c) IF THE CRYOMANCER DIES, EVERY PRISON OPENS. A dead hero holding an enemy out of the fight forever is the softlock the boss carve-out exists to refuse, arriving through a different door.
  • Bosses keep their carve-out and get one more. They resist Frozen until Broken, as before — and a held boss keeps its place on the timeline and releases on its own after one turn. A boss removed from the fight indefinitely is not a control fantasy, it is a softlock.
  • The spine has no cost clause, unlike Overburn, and that is intentional. His cost is tempo: every turn spent building stacks is a turn not spent killing, and he can hold one enemy. Control should not be self-harm. If the hold proves too cheap the lever is stack-build rate, not a tax bolted on afterwards.
  • KIT: Ice Lance becomes the release. It keeps its damage, its Break and its always-crit against Frozen — which finally means something, because the release is now a deliberate act with a payoff attached rather than a spell that happens to like frozen targets. Frostbolt, Razor Ice, Blizzard and Rime are mechanically unchanged; Blizzard's description now says outright that it does not thaw a hold, because a player would otherwise assume it does and never cast it again. Two new abilities arrive as tree tools: Glacial Prison (25 Mana, 2.5, 4cd — freeze the target outright, whatever its stacks) and Cryoclasm (20 Mana, 2.0, 3cd — move the hold and its stacks onto a different enemy). Neither enters a pool: both read the hold, so both fail Batch AH's curation rule.
  • SHATTERPOINT IS RENAMED THAW. It was Piercing Ice, Empowered Frostbolt, Icy Veins, Crystal Edge — four crit dials in the payoff lane of a control spec. Deep Freeze and Winter keep their names; only the lane that was lying about its job changed. Each row asks one question: 1 the opening · 2 the spread · 3 what the slow is worth · 4 the tool · 5 the hold · 6 the price · 7 the release · 8 the capstone.
  • ALL 24 NODES RE-AUTHORED AT ROW PRICING, EVERY ID SURVIVES, NO SAVE VERSION MOVES (still v7). Ten ids changed lane — legal, and the reason test_batch_as repeats the shape audit test_batch_ai already does generically. THE FULL MAPPING, old node → new design:
    Winter: cr_hungering Hungering Cold → Hungering Cold (1% → 3% per stack) · cr_emp_frostbolt Empowered Frostbolt → Deep Chill (both are Frostbolt riders; the free pump doubles instead of hitting harder) · cr_grasp Winter's Grasp → Winter's Grasp (1 → 2 enemies, row 6 → 3) · cr_rime Rime → Rime, unchanged (row 3 → 4) · cr_icy_resolve Icy Resolve → Icy Resolve (+1 → +2 turns) · cr_whiteout Whiteout → Whiteout re-specced (a 15% Daze roll → a flat 3 stacks on every enemy; Deep Freeze row 3 → Winter row 6) · cr_splinter Splintering Shards → always strikes a fourth time (the roll is gone: a node that decides whether the spec's win condition happens this turn must not be a coin flip; Shatterpoint row 4 → Winter row 7).
    Deep Freeze: cr_frostbite Brittle Ice → Brittle Ice (2% → 6%, and it reads HELD rather than Frozen) · cr_bitter Bitter Cold → Bitter Cold (1 → 2 stacks, row 6 → 2) · cr_frigid Frigid Grip → Frigid Grip (3% → 10%, and PER STACK) · cr_numbing Numbing Veil → Glacial Prison (denial by attrition → denial by decision) · cr_frost_ward Frost Ward → Second Prison — A FORCED ASSIGNMENT, REPORTED NOT HIDDEN (Second Prison has no ancestor in this tree; Frost Ward's damage reduction against Chilled attackers was the closest thing to "fewer enemies get to swing at you", and a slot had to hold it) · cr_cold_snap Cold Snap → Cold Snap re-specced, not repriced (it extended Frozen's duration, which an indefinite hold makes meaningless; now a held enemy's Break meter fills 15 each of his turns, so denial converts into the party's Break) · cr_glacial Glacial Economy → Glacial Economy (5% → 15% Mana).
    Thaw: cr_hypothermia Hypothermia → Hypothermia (1% → 3%, Winter → Thaw) · cr_freezing Freezing Advance → Killing Frost (both are "more damage against the enemy he just froze"; the same question asked of the hold instead of the moment) · cr_crystal Crystal Edge → Crystal Edge (5% → 15% per stack) · cr_lance_focus Lance Focus → Cryoclasm — a FORCED-ISH ASSIGNMENT, also reported (a Mana discount on Ice Lance was simply the closest remaining Ice Lance node) · cr_piercing Piercing Ice → Piercing Ice (10% → 30% crit damage) · cr_razor_hone Honed Shards → re-specced onto the RELEASE (it rode Ice Lance crits; it applies 3 stacks to the thawed enemy now, and because it lives in _hold_release the Shatter path and an evicted prison inherit it with no second implementation) · cr_icy_veins Icy Veins → Shattered Tempo (the Lance's payoff paying forward, which is what this does too — the release is paid out in TIME, through the existing delay_push arithmetic rather than a new one).
    Capstones: cr_eternal Eternal Winter unchanged · cr_absolute Absolute Zero re-specced ("freezing no longer reduces stacks" is redundant under an indefinite hold → NO LIMIT on how many enemies he holds) · cr_shatter Shatter re-specced into the mass release (it detonated the Chilled; it breaks every prison at once now, paid on the pile each held enemy was carrying).
  • §4 — A HELD ENEMY LEAVES THE INITIATIVE BAR. That single line of UI is the whole spec made visible: an enemy disappearing from the turn order is the control fantasy in a way no damage number can be. Its nameplate chip reads HELD rather than "Fz", and the tooltip names every door out — and what does not open one. Worth knowing: an ordinary hold leaves the bar because its next_time is INF, so the bar's own filter is load-bearing only for the held BOSS, which keeps its clock. A negative control that stripped the filter passed cleanly against a raider; the assertion that catches it now lives in the boss check.
  • §5 — RANKED → ADDITIVE, AND THE RUNE AUDIT. Every Cryomancer counter now writes its own magnitude in the units its read site sums. Under the old 1 × step form a rune writing the same field silently inherited the node's multiplier. FOUR RUNES RE-POINTED: the Bitter Grip (frigid_ranks 1 → 3, frostbite_ranks 1 → 2), the Long Winter (frigid_ranks 1 → 3, crystal_edge_ranks 1 → 5; hungering_ranks stays 1 — its read site was already additive), the Killing Cold (numbing_ranks 1 → 5; hypothermia_ranks stays 2, same reason), and the Honed Lance, whose lane tag moved Shatterpoint → Thaw — a lane that stopped existing would have left it homeless in both the bot's build policy and the per-lane coverage test. THE THREE MAGE CLASS-WIDE RUNES (Wellspring, Reckless Channeling, Comet) TOUCH NO CRYOMANCER COUNTER, asserted so a future re-tune of one cannot silently re-tune the other. FLAGGED FOR RE-AUTHORING, NOT DELETED: numbing_ranks has no node any more (Glacial Prison took the id) — the read site is KEPT and live, the rune still pays its advertised 5%, and the test asserts both. Also flagged: the Killing Cold is tagged the Winter rune but now pays into Thaw's Hypothermia and a node-less counter. That is a re-authoring call, not a repair, and rune magnitude has been closed since Batch AF.
  • UNREACHABLE BUT KEPT (the AR vault pattern — no node writes these and no rune does either, each gated > 0, listed so a later batch can re-node or delete them deliberately rather than rediscovering them): icy_veins_ranks / icy_veins_charge, emp_frostbolt_ranks, freezing_ranks / freezing_adv_mark, frost_ward_ranks.
  • §6 — ONE NAMED EXCLUSIVE PAIR DISSOLVES, and the brief's premise was half stale. cold_snap ↔ bitter_cold sat in rows 6 and 2 of the same lane after Batch AI replaced named pairs with row exclusivity, so a player can hold both and a rune writing either counter is legal. It is removed from the list in CLAUDE.md, with the reason in place. But it was not, as the brief has it, an entry in a TESTED rule: Batch AI retired test_runes._exclusives to a bare pass — at row granularity the alarm would fire on nearly every spec rune in the game — so the list survives only as prose. The other five pairs are untouched.
  • §7 — THE BOT KNOWS WHAT A HOLD IS. Build stacks on the highest-Attack enemy, freeze it, leave it held, and release with Ice Lance only when a second freeze is ready or it is the last enemy standing; Glacial Prison on cooldown against the highest-Attack unheld enemy; Cryoclasm when a bigger threat appears than the one he is holding. Instrument honesty, not tuning — no difficulty measurement belongs in this batch.

VERIFIED: check_parse 0 failures, check_flow 0 failures (6 screens), 11 scenes 0 SCRIPT ERROR. NEW test_batch_as.gd 387 checks / 0 failures, stable 3/3. Regression: test_batch_an 6044/0, test_batch_ah 5410/0 (its master.html STAMP GATE bumped AR → AS), ah_battle 65/0, ai 2036/0, aj 403/0, ak 523/0, al 556/0, ar 885/0, test_runes 2982/0, test_rune_battle 94/0, run-harness gates 1/2/3 PASS.

NEGATIVE CONTROLS RUN, because a test that cannot fail proves nothing: putting the opening roll back on raw speed trips 3 checks, stopping Ice Lance releasing trips 2, letting a Cleansing Rite reach a hold trips 1, giving the hold a clock trips 2, and Frigid Grip back at 3 trips 2. A sixth control — stripping the turn bar's _is_held filter — passed, and that gap was closed rather than noted: the held-boss assertion described above is what catches it now.

KIT SMOKE, fixed lineup, 40 battles/row, berserker,cryomancer,inquisitor,beastmaster, DOD_SIM_TALENTS force-learning full 8-node builds (standalone sims spend no points, so an unloaded run never touches these nodes at all). All four rows 40/40 wins, 0 SCRIPT ERROR, and Glacial Prison appears in the rotation at 1.0 casts/battle. The three lanes separated cleanly, which is the result worth keeping: ungeared he is 39% damage share / 146 BD / 0.93 enemy Breaks / 1.00 Breaks taken. WINTER 45% / 130 BD / 0.68 / 0.38 — tempo, and the party stops getting Broken. DEEP FREEZE 39% (FLAT) / 300 BD / 2.67 enemy Breaks / 0.03 taken — its damage share does not move while the party's Break output nearly triples and the enemies barely get to act. That is "control is a team resource" landing as a number. THAW 51% / 346 damage/battle — the window lane is the damage standout. MEASURED AND FLAGGED, NOT NERFED (the AK/AL/AR discipline): Thaw at 51% is the tallest column, and these magnitudes are the batch's own, so it ships as written. Kit-mechanics ratios ONLY — the smoke lineup carries no difficulty signal (Batch R).

REPORTED, NOT ACTED ON — SHATTER NEVER FIRED IN THE SMOKE, AND THE REASON IS A DESIGN TENSION, NOT A BOT BUG. Shatter is the Thaw capstone and Absolute Zero is the Deep Freeze capstone, and only one capstone can be taken — so a player who takes Shatter holds at most TWO (Second Prison, Deep Freeze row 5, which costs him Piercing Ice). At a hold limit of one, Shatter and Ice Lance do the same job and the Lance is cheaper, so the bot correctly prefers it. The intended pairing is reachable — Shatter is also in SPEC_POOLS["cryomancer"], so a Cryomancer can EARN it and take Absolute Zero as the capstone — but no sim row exercised that. test_batch_as drives the mass release directly instead: three enemies held under Absolute Zero, one cast, all three released and all three struck.

NO DIFFICULTY MEASUREMENT AND NO SIM ROW, deliberately — same reasoning as AJ/AK/ AL/AR: a single-spec re-author has no honest control row against a fixed four-hero party, and ./sim.sh carries no difficulty signal (Batch R).

2026-08-07 — Batch AR: the Pyromancer — commitment

First of the Mage three. His lanes were purpose-designed and sound; what he needed was the repricing every node was missing, and a spine. Spend everything, and the payoff is behind the risk.

  • Inferno Master is gone. OVERBURN replaces it, and it is one mechanic in three clauses. Bonus: +2% damage per remaining turn of Burn on the enemy team, capped at +40%. Drain: at the start of each of his turns he loses 1 Mana for every one of those turns — and that has no cap. Refund: every turn of Burn he CONSUMES returns 1 Mana. Two things used to defuse his loop: his fire is free, so the bank filled itself, and the old passive paid him for HAVING lit enemies, so his damage arrived whether or not he ever pulled the trigger. Both are fixed by the same replacement.
  • The asymmetry is the design: the reward caps and the cost does not. One enemy at 3 turns is 3 Mana a turn for +6% — trivial. A full field at 20 burn-turns is 20 Mana a turn for +40%, against a Mage's regen of 22: treading water, and unable to bank Detonation's 25. At 24 he is going backwards. That squeeze — lit too much to afford the trigger — is the spec's characteristic failure, and it is reachable in ordinary play, not a corner case. The refund is what makes the whole thing an engine rather than a trap: consuming a six-turn Burn returns 6 Mana and drops the drain by 6 a turn, so cashing in is the answer to every problem he has. Fireball stays free, so he can always light more — including when that is the wrong move.
  • One rule, one implementation. The refund is a property of the passive, not of any ability, so Detonation, Wildfire, Cataclysm and anything the tree later adds all inherit it from a single helper. Same for the drain and the cap.
  • The spec chip shows both numbers live — the bonus he is being paid and the Mana his next turn will cost — because half of a trade is not a readout.
  • The 6% global Burn tick constant is NOT touched. A proposal to weaken tick damage so unspent fuel was wasted was dropped deliberately: Overburn's drain already makes unspent fire cost something, and that constant is shared with enemies, runes and every other burn source in the game.
  • Detonation becomes the win condition. Its Burn bonus rises 150% → 250% of the consumed Burn's remaining damage. Cost, cooldown and Break damage are unchanged. With the refund behind it, it is both his biggest hit and his release valve.
  • Wildfire is the WIDE release valve where Detonation is the narrow one — mechanically unchanged, and its description now says so.
  • Flame Shield is re-specced into IMMOLATE and stops being defensive. 15 Mana, 1.5 initiative, 2cd, self-cast: for 2 turns Overburn's damage cap is lifted entirely, its drain doubles, and anyone who strikes him is set Burning 3 turns. Every clause pushes the same direction, and the retaliation burn feeds his own engine.
  • This leaves the Pyromancer with no defensive option anywhere in his kit or tree. That is deliberate, and it is flagged here rather than softened. He is the commitment spec; an escape hatch is the one thing that would undo the batch. Molten Core, Scorched Earth and Ashes of Al'ar all went with it.
  • Pyroblast and Phoenix Rebirth come out of the vault as tree nodes. Pyroblast: 45 Mana, 55% of Attack, 6.0 initiative, +50% against a Burning target — no cooldown, because 45 Mana under a drain arriving 6.0 down the bar is the whole limiter. Phoenix Rebirth ships with one correction: the vaulted definition granted "Empower", which since the Holy Cleric's rework is a specific named mechanic belonging to her Mercy system. That clause is dropped. It reads: sacrifice 25% of current HP, Mana returns to full. Under Overburn a full pool is precisely the relief he needs, and paying health for it is a choice he makes rather than a clock he carries — which is the whole reason the drain sits on Mana.
  • All 24 nodes re-authored, and EVERY ID SURVIVES. Saved picks migrate and no save version moves (still v7). Where a node changed what it does it was re-specced in place — the precedent is Batch AJ turning bz_vitality from Vitality into First Blood. No ids added, none deleted.

THE FULL ID MAPPING — so a later batch reading a saved pick can see where it went. Mapped by closest surviving intent; four ids changed lane, which is legal and expected.

  • One old node had no home, and it is reported rather than forced. Ashes of Al'ar — the self-revive — has no successor anywhere in the new design, because §2 removes every defensive option deliberately. Its id carries Wildfire Spread now purely because a slot had to hold it; that is the batch's one assignment made on position rather than intent. Where it should go, if the designer wants it back: not into this tree. It is a Mage-wide or relic-shaped effect, and putting it back in a Pyromancer lane re-opens exactly the escape hatch the batch closed.
  • Cauterise is the health risk put back, once, as an opt-in. The drain sits on Mana so it would not be a clock he carries; one node that lets a player choose to carry it anyway is a decision rather than a tax, and it is the sharpest thing in the tree. Taking Kiln-Forged as well is legal, and the FLOOR WINS — a Pyromancer who paid for "the drain can never take me below 10" is not then billed in blood for the same drain.
  • The magnitudes are ADDITIVE, not ranked. Two fields are fed by a rune as well as by a node (Burn tick strength and Flamewave's turns), so each node writes its magnitude in the units its read site adds up. The node pays its number, the rune pays its number, and stacked they pay the sum. That is Batch AL's repair rule applied up front instead of after the fact.

THE SPEC RUNE AUDIT (and it found what it was sent to find). Most Pyromancer spec runes ride an existing talent counter rather than carrying their own machinery, and a rune pointing at a counter that no longer exists fails silently — no error, no log line. All four Pyromancer runes and all three Mage runes were checked against the new node set:

  • RE-POINTED — Rune of the Cinder Trail. The node took cinder_trail_ranks for a new meaning, so the rune was moved onto its own term and keeps paying exactly the effect it advertises (embers onto a second enemy). Its text is unchanged because its behaviour is.
  • STILL LIVE, now rune-only content — the Rune of the Blast Radius (Detonation consumes 25% more) and the Rune of the Long Burn (softer hits from burning enemies, Detonation crit). Their nodes are gone; their read sites were deliberately kept, because a rune whose node vanished wants re-authoring, not silent death.
  • FLAGGED, NOT GUESSED — the Rune of the White Flame's middle clause is INERT. It reads "Inferno Master grants +1% per burning enemy", and Overburn has no per-turn step to raise. Re-pointing it at the cap or the drain would be inventing a magnitude the design deliberately fixed, so the rune is left exactly as authored and this is the flag. Its other two clauses (resistance piercing, the health cost) are untouched and live. A test asserts the clause is inert, so a future re-authoring has to come and change that assertion rather than discover the problem.
  • The three Mage class runes (Wellspring, Reckless Channeling, Comet) touch nothing this batch moved.
  • runes.json changed by exactly one field name. This is a dead-counter re-point, not the rune-magnitude pass the designer closed in Batch AF.

THE BOT. The autoplay mage policy ignored Mana pressure entirely, and under Overburn a bot that never detonates to relieve the drain would measure a spec no player would recognise. It now knows the one rule that matters: consume Burn when the drain exceeds Mana regeneration — Detonation onto the largest stack, Wildfire when Detonation is cooling. This is instrument honesty, not tuning.

KIT SMOKE, AND ONE THING WORTH FLAGGING. Forty battles per row against the fixed lineup, with a full 8-node build force-learned (a standalone sim spends no talent points, so an unloaded run never touches these nodes at all). Every row 40/40 wins, no script errors, and every new ability shows up in the rotation. The Kindling lane is the standout by a distance — his damage share goes 46% ungeared to 66% on a full Kindling build, against 48% for Inferno and 50% for Detonation. Firestorm, Backdraft, Accelerant's tick and Conflagration's turns all compound, and the drain that is meant to price that does not bite inside a seven-round smoke fight. Measured and flagged, not nerfed: these are the batch's own magnitudes, so it ships as written. Kit-mechanics ratios only — the smoke lineup carries no difficulty signal.

NO DIFFICULTY MEASUREMENT WAS RUN, deliberately — the same reason Batches AJ, AK and AL recorded: a single-spec re-author has no honest control row against a fixed four-hero party, and ./sim.sh carries no difficulty signal.

TWO CLAIMS IN THE BATCH BRIEF WERE STALE, and are recorded rather than quietly actioned. (1) §6 asked for the Master Document's Wildfire text to be corrected, saying it described Wildfire spreading the target's Burn at half duration. It did not — Batch AG updated that passage and the doc already matched the code. (2) §4 warned that spec runes would break "quietly"; only one clause did, and it is the one flagged above. Both are the same lesson Batch AP recorded: a note in a brief is not evidence, and has to be re-read against the code before it is acted on.

2026-08-07 — Batch AQ: the modifier pool, 6 → 19, and upgrades you can see

Two things. The bargain's modifier pool goes from six placeholders to nineteen authored ones, weighted toward the mild end. And the ability upgrades Batch AP wired become visible outside a fight.

  • Thirteen new modifiers. Severity 1: Parched (both parties open at half resource), Slick Footing (+0.5 initiative on every ability), Dull Edge (crit chance halved), Muffled (all Break damage −25%), Hoarfrost (everyone opens Chilled). Severity 2: Fleeting (every status lasts one turn less, never below one), Feverish (everyone deals 25% more), Deadened (nobody can be Broken), Miasma (healing received halved). Severity 3: Thin Air (no resource returns at turn start), Encumbered (+2 turns on every cooldown that already had one). Severity 4: Bloodletting (every attack adds 15 Bleed), Mirrorbound (a quarter of the damage you deal comes back on you). The six existing modifiers are untouched — ids, severities and text — so a save holding a pending modifier still resolves.
  • The pool is weighted low on purpose. Six severity-1, six severity-2, four severity-3, three severity-4. Every offer's first card is drawn from the 1–2 pool, so that is the end a run actually sees over and over; the harsh end already rotates. Authoring the pool evenly would have deepened the half nobody repeats.
  • The other two cards now come from the severity 3–4 pool alone. They used to draw from everything left, low and high together. At six modifiers that was fine; at nineteen — twelve of them low — 86% of sampled offers came out holding two cheap options, three cheap rewards, and no real decision. Filling from the high pool makes every offer read the same way: one safe option, two gambles. The floor still guarantees the safe one, so a wounded party is never cornered. This is the only change in the batch that is not authoring.
  • Rot was authored, implemented, and dropped — and that is the finding worth keeping. It was to halve everyone's maximum health, and the risk flagged in advance was the percentage effects that read max HP. The real problem was somewhere else entirely: the end-of-battle sync writes the battle unit's max HP straight back onto the party member, so winning a Rot fight would have carried the halving out of the battle that charged for it — the map card would show the halved maximum, the party's health would be clamped under it, and a bargain sold as "one battle" would have taken half the party's HP for the rest of the run. Undoing that needs a seventh new field read at the save sync, which is past the field budget this batch set for itself, so it was dropped rather than patched around. Severity 4 ships with three modifiers instead of four; the pool is 19, not 20. Reinstating Rot is a small, separate decision, and it should be made deliberately.
  • Companions were missing every modifier — a pre-existing bug six modifiers made nearly invisible. The modifier is stamped on everyone at spawn, and a summoned beast exists at neither of those moments, so under Hoarfrost it would have been the only unit on the field not Chilled. Fixed at the summon site, beside the lines that already copy the hunter's armor, Stability and crit. Correction to the batch brief: it cited Feverish as an example of what the beast misses — it does not. The beast's Attack is copied from the hunter's, which is already modified, so Feverish (and Dull Edge) always reached it; stamping them again would have doubled them, and the fix skips exactly those two.
  • Ability upgrades are visible outside a fight now. A run awards three upgrades to every hero, twelve across the party, and until now every one of them was discoverable only by hovering ability buttons mid-battle. An upgraded ability wears a gold ◆ on its label — on the battle action button and on the hero sheet's chip — and each map hero card carries a ◆N count beside its rune slots, whose hover lists them one per line ("Overpower — Honed"). The badge takes no click of its own; the whole card is already the button that opens the tree. The hover line itself is unchanged from Batch AP: names only, because the magnitudes are already in the numbers above them. The hero sheet was capturing nothing from the call that applies upgrades; it reads that call's return now, so it can only ever mark an upgrade that really applied.
  • Four glossary entries: Ability Upgrades (under Progression, next to boss trophies), and The Bargain, Modifier and Severity under The Run, cross-linked to each other. Nineteen modifiers is the point at which the system needs one place explaining the system rather than nineteen descriptions each explaining one face.
  • Two shapes deliberately not authored, recorded so the rules stay visible: anything that touches only heroes ("no items this fight", "the skill-check window shrinks") breaks the binds-both-parties rule and is a difficulty slider wearing a bargain's clothes; and miss-chance modifiers, which would land cleanly on the hook Dazed already uses and play as tedium rather than tension. Rejected on feel, not on machinery.
  • Unchanged and stated as such: the reward table, the bot's severity-extreme bargain policy, the offer screen's layout, and the save version — nothing new persists.

2026-08-07 — Batch AP: the mini-boss reward becomes real

Ability upgrades were offered, picked, stored on the hero and saved — and nothing read them at battle spawn. Three mini-bosses a run handed out an inert prize. This batch wires the four placeholder upgrades and nothing else; the pool stays four, and no new upgrade is authored here.

  • The four upgrades now do something. Honed multiplies an ability's damage by 1.5. Quickened takes two turns off its cooldown, floored at 0. Effortless sets its resource cost to 0. Swift cuts its initiative delay by 25%, floored at 1.0.
  • Two of the four had their text corrected, because it was written before anything read it. Effortless zeroes the ordinary resource cost only — never Mercy. Mercy is the Holy Cleric's identity resource and a free Resurrection is a different game, so the description now says "costs no Rage / Mana / Focus (Mercy is unaffected)". Swift said "+2 initiative speed", but delays across the whole roster run 1.5–4.0, so subtracting 2 would have taken most abilities to near-zero — the authored number was never checked against the field it names. It is a 25% cut floored at 1.0 instead, described as "Arrives 25% sooner". That floor-and-percentage is the one balance judgement in this batch, and it is stated here rather than buried. Honed and Quickened keep their text as written.
  • Upgrades apply after talents, and the order is not interchangeable. One helper on Run, called from exactly the two places that assemble a hero's kit: the battle spawn and the hero sheet. Both call it after the talent tree and the equipped runes, because several talents set an ability's field rather than add to it — the Swordmaster node that upgrades Lunge sets its cost to 15 — so an Effortless applied first would be silently overwritten. Upgrades go last and always win. An upgrade naming an ability the hero no longer holds is skipped in silence; that can happen across a spec reroll and is not an error. The run simulator reloads the battle scene, so it inherits the same code with no separate change. Stacking and never-twice already worked and are untouched, and upgrades already persisted, so no save version moves.
  • The offer only pairs an upgrade with an ability it can change. It used to pair a random un-taken upgrade with a random owned ability, which could offer Honed on Heal (no damage), Effortless on Blood Price (no cost) or Quickened on a basic attack (no cooldown) — a pick that reads as a reward and does nothing, which is the exact bug this batch exists to close. Each candidate upgrade now keeps only the abilities it has something to touch, and an upgrade with nothing to land on is dropped from the offer rather than paired with a dud. An offer showing fewer than three was already normal and needs no new handling.
  • The player can see it. An upgraded ability's button in battle carries a trailing line naming its upgrades — "Honed · Swift". Names only: the magnitudes are already in the numbers above them, which now reflect the upgrade. The hero sheet assembles the kit the same way, so an Effortless ability loses its cost tag there too. Nothing else in the UI changes; the pick itself already resolved on the map hero card.
  • Talent tooltips stop reading "25.00%". Every tooltip in all twelve trees rendered its number to two decimals; the dead zeros and a trailing decimal point are trimmed, so a whole 25 reads "25" and a genuine 2.5 still reads "2.5". This had been reported and not acted on since Batch AJ. The Master Document's talent tables were regenerated to match, but only where the dead decimal was the only difference from the live text.
  • Housekeeping: one test check formatted an Array into a bare %s and spewed a "String formatting error" to stderr on every run — one character. The treasure/Loot node type needed no deleting: it was already gone, taken with the whole node-map generator when Batch AN replaced it with the line. It had been listed as outstanding since Batch AC; the listing was stale, not the code.

2026-08-07 — Batch AO: map direction, offer gating, and a kill switch

Three changes from playing Batch AN. No new systems, and no balance work beyond the one gold number in the second item.

  • The road now reads LEFT TO RIGHT. Encounter 1 sits on the left edge and the boss on the right. Batch AN flagged right-to-left as unconventional and shipped it anyway; it read wrong in play, so it is flipped. It was two constants, as AN said it would be (SLOT_X_START moves to the exact mirror of its old value across the 1280-wide screen, and the step now adds instead of subtracting — the line keeps its old margins, reversed). The compass label under the road is deleted: it existed only because the direction was unconventional, and left-to-right is what a reader already assumes. The slot numbers 1–12 under the dots stay — twelve near-identical dots want a ruler whichever way the road runs.
  • The offer moves to ELITES and MINI-BOSSES; plain fights pay gold instead. A bargain screen before all eight plain fights in a zone made it a toll booth. It is an event now: four encounters a zone instead of ten. Bosses still get no offer. A mini-boss is therefore fought under a modifier, which is intended — every enemy in a mini-boss warband already carries 1.5× max HP, so a severity-4 pick there is a real gamble, and the guaranteed low-severity option is what makes it a choice rather than a trap. The mini-boss's existing spoils (elite gold, 1 talent point, the ability-upgrade pick) are untouched and now stack with the bargain's reward. Plain-fight gold covers the hole: 25–35 → 45–60. Elite/mini-boss (80–100) and boss (110–130) are unchanged. It is deliberately below the old bargain payout, because a plain fight no longer charges a modifier for it. The severity-1-or-2 floor, the no-decline rule and reward-on-victory are all unchanged, and the run sim's harness walks the same gate so a simulated run walks the road the player walks.
  • New: battle DEBUG ▾ → Kill All Enemies. A tester can reach the victory screen on demand. It is a switch, not a hit: every living enemy is set to 0 HP and killed directly rather than through the damage path, so there is no damage event, no on-hit or overkill proc and no floating number. The deliberate consequence is that on-death talent procs which read a killing blow (Seeding Embers and friends) do not fire on a nuked kill — the accepted cost of a switch. Everything after the kills is the real victory path: gold, talent points, the 15% heal, the bargain's reward, elite spoils and the run summary, which is exactly what needs testing. It inherits the existing debug gate and the debug_used honesty flag from the menu's one dispatch, so a debug-touched run still declares itself in the summary.

2026-08-07 — Batch AN: the map scaffold

Rewards and modifiers in this batch are PLACEHOLDERS drawn from content that already exists. The point is to make a full 36-encounter run playable so the pacing can be felt before 66 pieces of new content are authored against it. Nothing here is final art or final content.

  • The branching node map is gone; a run is a LINE. 3 zones × 12 encounters = 36, and every zone has the identical shape: fight, fight, ELITE, fight, fight, MINI-BOSS, fight, fight, ELITE, fight, fight, BOSS. Zone 3's boss is the end boss. The road renders right to left — encounter 1 on the right edge, the boss on the left. (Horizontal run-maps conventionally read left-to-right, so an English-reading player may parse the boss end as the start; this was flagged, chosen anyway, and is two constants if it flips.)
  • Deleted rather than left unreachable: FLOORS, NODES_PER_TIER, the deck composition constants (FIGHT_NODES/REST_NODES/SHOP_NODES/EVENT_NODES), both DECK_FALLBACK tables, MINIBOSS_TIER, the rejection-sampling deal and its swap repair, the link graph, the inbound guarantee, and the forward-DP key-route check. All rest nodes and the rest-heal path go with them. So does the edge-column adjacency rule and its documented-70%/actual-53% bug — it needed deleting, not fixing. Batch AF's starting rune pick is deleted too: heroes now begin with no runes and empty slots. A test pins each of these as ABSENT, because a constant that still resolves is a constant a later batch reads by accident.
  • New: the offer screen. Before every fight and elite — not mini-bosses, not bosses — the player is shown three bargains and takes one. A bargain is one modifier (a condition binding BOTH parties for that battle) plus one reward, both visible before choosing, with no decline. Each modifier carries a severity of 1–4 and the reward table reads it, so authoring a modifier is authoring one number rather than a pairing. Severity is flat and deliberately ignores party composition — a modifier that happens to be cheap for your build is a good deal you earned by building that way. Every offer holds at least one option of severity 1 or 2, guaranteed by construction rather than by rejecting rolls, so a wounded party always has a survivable choice. The reward pays on victory: the modifier is the price, and the "a merchant follows the fight" reward settles that a reward cannot precede the fight it follows.
  • Six placeholder modifiers, to be replaced by the authored 20 — each built out of a hook battle.gd already had, each landing at exactly one read site: Overgrown (1, both parties open at 70% health), Tinderbox (2, all fire +25%), Frenzied (2, everyone acts 30% faster, cooldowns unchanged), Brittle (3, all attacks ignore armor), Warded (3, abilities cost 25% more, basics free), Bloodless (4, no healing for anyone; Bleed and damage-over-time still tick). NOTE on Warded: basic attacks are cost 0 already, so "basic attacks are free" describes the kit rather than changing it — the multiplier is written to never add to a zero, which is the line that keeps it true.
  • Placeholder rewards, existing content only. Severity 1: 40 gold. 2: 80 gold or a random potion. 3: 140 gold or a random rune. 4: 220 gold, a random rune, or a merchant follows the fight.
  • The map screen is the party screen now. All four hero stat cards live on it — portrait, spec name, HP bar with numbers, resource bar, three rune slots (filled or empty), unspent-talent badge. Clicking a card opens that hero's talent tree; clicking a rune slot opens their pouch. Owed ability, upgrade and rune picks resolve on the card. The Party button and its separate screen are gone; the old Party screen survives as the hero sheet, entered for one hero with a four-button switcher and its list view deleted.
  • Rewards re-cut. The mini-boss now awards a generic ability upgrade chosen from three, attached to an ability the hero owns — upgrades stack on the same ability, but the same upgrade never comes twice in a run. Placeholder pool of four until the dozen are authored: Honed (+50% damage), Quickened (−2 cooldown), Effortless (no resource cost), Swift (+2 initiative speed). Zone 1 and 2 bosses award an ability pick from three, drawn from the hero's spec pool only — the class-pool draw Batch AH added is dropped, abilities are spec-locked now. (Classes.CLASS_POOLS is left standing and still resolves; nothing reads it, and reopening the class draw is one line.) The end boss awards a relic unlock, big gold, and ends the run — no ability pick, because nothing follows it.
  • The shop is scheduled, not placed. 40% chance of a merchant after any cleared fight or elite, with a floor: four encounters without one and the next is guaranteed. Plus the severity-4 reward, which guarantees one on demand. Both the merchant and the 25% event roll are made once, on the victory screen, and queued — rolling them from the map would re-roll on every redraw and the floor would mean nothing.
  • Attrition: no rest nodes. Clearing any encounter heals the party 15% of max HP, riding the same victory_heal_pct hook the Chalice of Dawn already used, so the relic's 10% stacks on top for 25% and there is still exactly one read site for "how much does a victory heal". Potions are usable on the map screen, targeting a hero from the stat cards (the Bomb and the Defense Potion stay battle-only and say why on hover). Potion cap: 6 per item type — purchases and drops above it are refused with a message rather than silently lost, and the shop greys a full stack rather than taking the gold.
  • Talent points: 1 apiece from elites, mini-bosses and bosses — the end boss included, where it used to pay nothing. Regular fights award none. That is 4 a zone, 12 a run, against an 8-node tree. The two purses became one, and the arithmetic is why: Batch AI had 8 points against 8 nodes, so an elite point had to be BARRED from second nodes or the tree could be climbed faster than intended. At 12 against 8 the shape does the barring by itself — rows are mutually exclusive and there are only eight, so a purse can open at most 8 rows and the surplus has nowhere to go but second nodes. The flex purse survives in the data and is spent first when it holds anything; ordinary points cover these picks when it is empty.
  • Runes: three slots, flat, from run start. The 2 → 3 → 4 growth ladder is gone. Heroes start with none. Rune content is unchanged in this batch — the rebuild (deleting ability-granting and talent-rank runes, authoring ~36 double-edged ones) is its own batch.
  • SAVE v7, and pre-v7 saves are REFUSED and cleared on load. A pre-v7 save holds a [tier][column] board with links, rest nodes and a mini-boss row; there is no honest way to place a party standing at tier 7 column 2 onto a line with no columns. Same call Batch AI made about ranked talent purchases — a wipe that says so beats a migration that invents state.
  • Sim harness: the map walk follows the line, the bot takes bargains by a severity-extreme policy (harshest above 60% average party HP, mildest below), and the report gains merchants-met, events-fired, bargains-taken and average accepted severity while losing the rest lines. DOD_SIM_MAP, DOD_SIM_MINIBOSS, DOD_SIM_START_RUNE and DOD_SIM_SPEC_OPENING are retired with the features they controlled; a Matrix row now reads map=line and carries none of those fields. Rows from before the road are not comparable with rows after it — even depth changed units (36 encounters, not 33 tiers). "Route agency" is retained and reads 0% forever by design: a line has no route decision, and a missing figure would look like a broken instrument.
  • Verified: new test_batch_an.gd, 5,667 checks / 0 failures, stable 3/3 — the line's shape and its one-way-forward walk, every deleted constant and function pinned ABSENT, 2,000 sampled offers for the severity-1-or-2 floor (0 misses) and for severity-matched rewards, all six modifiers stamped on BOTH parties in a live battle and checked at the read site each one implements, a no-modifier battle proving the pre-AN path is untouched, the upgrade pool's stacking and never-twice rules, spec-only ability draws, the merchant floor driven rather than sampled (no drought exceeds four), the item cap refusing rather than swallowing, and the flat three rune slots. Regression: test_batch_ah 5,416/0, test_batch_ah_battle 65/0, test_batch_ai 2,036/0, test_batch_aj 389/0, test_batch_ak 524/0, test_batch_al 556/0, test_runes 2,982/0, run-harness gates 1/2/3 PASS, 11 scenes and every script compiling with 0 SCRIPT ERROR. Known-bad and not ours: test_rune_battle 91/1, the Inferno chip check Batch AK recorded as a standing defect. test_start_rune.gd and test_start_rune_ui.gd are deleted with the feature they pinned.

2026-08-06 — Batch AL: the Warden's 24 nodes

  • All 24 tree nodes re-authored for row exclusivity. The third of the four class batches Batch AI promised. Every id survives, so saved picks migrate and no save version moves (still v6). Since AI a node is not one of three ranks — it is the whole row, priced against the two doors it closes, which came out at roughly 3-4× the old rank-1 values: Unkillable 2% → 8% of the health he brought in, Richocet 5% → 35% stun chance, Toughness 5% → 25% of max HP as Constitution, Provoke +1 → +2 taunted foes, Rally +15%/2 turns → +30%/3 turns, Endurance 1% → 3% armor per unhealed turn, Iron Will 4% → 12% per debuff, Tenacity +5 → +15 max HP per block, Sundering 25% → 100% of the Break splash, Elemental Weakness 5% → 20% resist shred, Shield Mastery +1 → +2 turns of stance, Spite 8% → 30% reflected, Plate Discipline +3% → +12% on the plating climb, Bruising Guard 10 → 30 Break damage, Shared Vigil 3% → 12%, Battered Not Broken 8 → 30 Break shed, Grudge 6% → 25%, Steadfast 15% → 60% absorbed.
  • The rows are themed, not merely exclusive. 1 who your defence pays, 2 how much you hold, 3 attrition, 4 what compounds, 5 active defence, 6 the engine running, 7 the last stand, 8 the capstone. Row 1 is the model for the whole tree: one trigger, three beneficiaries — a block heals him, a block stuns them, a taunt empowers the party.
  • Two nodes were re-specced in place — same id, new effect — and for the same reason. Batch AH moved the ability each one modified into the earnable spec pool, so both were dead on a Warden who never drew it. Rallying Stomp ("War Stomp restores +5% more resource") became Rallying Cry: at the start of each of the Warden's turns every ally regains 4% of their maximum resource, and War Stomp — if he owns it — restores 20% more on top. Bulwark Line ("Interpose grants +1 charge") now keys to Shieldwall, which he has always had since Batch G promoted it into the base kit: the stance also grants every ally +10% Block chance for its duration, and Interpose — if he owns it — still hands each ally an extra charge. The refuel and the cover are Banner's real cargo; they happen on their own now, and the earnable abilities ride on top.
  • Tank and Spank is CERTAIN. It was a 15% chance to Empower an ally off Mocking Blow. Mocking Blow is free and on his rotation constantly, so a chance roll there read as noise rather than tension — nothing was ever planned around it. It always fires now.
  • Spite and Bruising Guard stopped being an exclusive fork. They sat in one tier as "reflect the damage you soak, or convert your blocks into Break". Split across rows 5 and 6 both are reachable, so taking the second welds them into one continuous Break engine instead of two unrelated numbers: with Spite taken, its reflected damage also builds Break equal to 50% of its value.
  • Hold the Line's capstone upgrades an earned copy. The ability also sits in the Warden spec pool (Batch AH), so drawing it early used to make the capstone the worst pick in its row — a second copy of a button you already have. Taken on an ability he already owns it now UPGRADES instead: the Break reduction rises from 50% to 80%, and the no-death window lasts two turns rather than one. Same pattern as the Swordmaster's Lunge and Execute and the Berserker's Battle Shout and Rampage.
  • Two Warden runes repaired in passing. The Rune of Grudges and the Rune of the Standard each added a RANK to a talent counter whose per-rank value this batch multiplies by four. Left alone, a rune advertising "+6% damage against his taunted mark" would quietly have started paying 25% — a magnitude change nobody authored, and exactly the rune-magnitude pass the designer closed in Batch AF. Both now carry their own smaller term alongside the node's, so each pays its advertised number whether it is worn alone or beside the talent. Same repair as Batch AK made to the Rune of the Still Wrist, for the same reason.
  • New status: Bulwark Line. The ally Block grant rides the same Heavy Plating slice of the block roll that Shieldwall's own stance does, so what it buys is real Block rather than a separate ward — and a block it buys is logged as "(Bulwark Line)". It does NOT feed the Warden's Tenacity or Rally, which key on his own Heavy Plating blocks.
  • Measured, flagged, not nerfed. Kit smoke test, fixed lineup, 40 battles per row, force-learned 8-node builds (a standalone sim spends no points, so an unloaded run never touches these nodes at all). All rows 40/40 wins, 0 SCRIPT ERROR. The THREAT lane is the standout by a distance: his Break damage per battle 104 → 320 and the party's Breaks per battle 1.02 → 2.35, with his damage share 17% → 30% — that is Sundering at a full 100% splash plus the newly-reachable Spite/Bruising Guard pair. The BANNER build shows up in statuses applied per battle (1.3 → 5.7) rather than damage, and the PLATE build barely moves against a lineup the party already survives at 85% health. Kit-mechanics ratios only — the fixed smoke lineup carries NO difficulty signal (Batch R). No difficulty measurement was run, deliberately: a content re-author of one spec has no honest control row, because the sim's damage-share instrument reads a fixed four-hero party.
  • Verified. New test_batch_al.gd, 556 checks / 0 failures, stable 3/3: tree shape, all 24 ids/rows/lanes/names, every magnitude in BOTH the payload and the tooltip that renders it, all three conditional halves firing / dark / inert on an empty ctx, the upgrade path in both acquisition orders, both repaired runes paying alone and stacked, and a live battle for the ally cover, the refuel, the taunt spread, the block payoffs and the upgraded capstone. Regression: test_batch_ai 2042/0, test_batch_ah 4284/0, test_batch_ah_battle 63/0, test_batch_aj 389/0, test_batch_ak 524/0, test_runes 3128/0, test_start_rune 239/0, test_start_rune_ui 31/0, run-harness gates 1/2/3 PASS. 10 scenes, 0 SCRIPT ERROR. Known-bad and not ours: test_rune_battle 91/1, the Inferno chip check Batch AK recorded as a standing defect.

2026-08-06 — Batch AJ: the Berserker's 24 nodes

  • All 24 tree nodes re-authored for row exclusivity. Every id survives, so saved picks migrate and no save version moves. The rows have been mutually exclusive since Batch AI, which means a node is no longer one of three ranks — it is the whole row, priced against the two doors it closes. Concretely: Savagery +5 → +15 Bleed, Unstoppable 2.5% → 3.5% per step, Hemorrhage's threshold 80 → 60 bloodloss, Reckless Fury 5%/5% → 20% dealt / 15% taken, Flurry +1 → +2 strikes, Crushing Blows 3% → 9% per step, Bloodied Momentum 15 → 40 Rage, Arterial Spray 25% → a full transfer, Deathwish 6% → 25%, Relentless −5 → −15 Rage, Scent of Blood 3% → 10% per bleedout, Scar Tissue's floor 60% → 85%, Second Wind 40 → 60 Rage, Bloodcraze 3% → 12% max HP, Measured Rage 8% → 20%, Unrelenting Assault +10 → +40 Constitution, Blood Tithe 15 → 45 Rage, Enraged 3% → 12% per stack.
  • The rows are themed now. Each row asks one question and each lane answers it its own way: 1 the opening, 2 the wound, 3 what the wound pays, 4 the edge, 5 what compounds, 6 refusal, 7 the finish, 8 the capstone. Batch AI cut the old tiers into rows mechanically, which left several rows with no question in them at all.
  • Two nodes were re-specced in place — same id, new effect. Vitality (+5% max HP) became First Blood: the Berserker begins every battle with 40 Rage. A flat health dial is not an opening. Deafening Cry (Battle Shout's cooldown) became Overkill: killing an enemy clears the cooldowns of Hack and Slash and Wildstrikes. AI's mechanical row assignment had put Deafening Cry in the same row as Battle Shout itself, and two exclusive nodes where one exists only to modify the other is a row with a dead option in it.
  • Three cross-row conditions. Crushing Blows counts every 15 points of bloodloss instead of 20 if Savagery was taken. Scar Tissue's Frenzy floor holds 100% of the peak — never falling at all — if Unstoppable was taken. Measured Rage with Reckless Fury cancels that node's +15% damage taken outright rather than subtracting from it, so the pair buys consequence-free damage: two nodes for a clean +20%, or one node and eat the risk.
  • Two upgrade paths. Battle Shout and Rampage sit in the Berserker's spec pool as well as his tree. A node on an ability he does not own grants it; on one he earned from a pick it upgrades instead — Battle Shout to +18% for 4 turns (from +12% for 3), Rampage's kill-recast to twice a turn.
  • Rampage's recast is capped for the first time. It used to chain for as long as the kills kept coming, which is a capstone with no ceiling and nothing left for an upgrade path to buy. It is once per turn now, twice with the upgrade.
  • Fixed: Measured Rage has been doing nothing since Batch AI. The damage-taken read site was guarded > 0.0, which silently ate every negative value — and Measured Rage's whole payload is a negative. The node has been inert for two batches. The guard now reads any non-zero value, and the batch's own test spawns a live Berserker to prove the reduction lands.
  • Second Wind clears every cooldown as well as granting its Rage, which is what makes the near-death moment a whole turn rather than a resource top-up.
  • Relentless buys reliability permanently. Hack and Slash's bleed roll always lands with it — the Batch AG rule that a perfect on a swingy ability pays in reliability rather than magnitude, bought once instead of earned per cast.
  • Naming collision, reported not resolved: the Sharpshooter's Precision lane already has a talent called Overkill (a kill's overflow damage carries to another enemy). Different trees and different ids, so nothing breaks — but two nodes now share a name in tooltips. The engine field was named overkill_reset to keep them apart.
  • Verified: new test_batch_aj.gd, 389 checks / 0 failures, stable 3/3 — the tree's shape, all 24 ids/rows/lanes/names, every magnitude in both the payload and the tooltip it renders, all three conditional halves firing/dark/inert-on-empty-ctx, both upgrade paths in both acquisition orders, and a live battle for First Blood's opening Rage, the Overkill reset, Second Wind's cleared cooldowns, the Measured/Reckless cancellation and the Scar Tissue floor. test_batch_ah_battle's ordering probe was re-pointed IN PLACE (it rode Deafening Cry, which no longer exists) onto Battle Shout's own upgrade path — the same question asked of the mechanism that is load-bearing today. Regression: test_batch_ai 2042/0, test_batch_ah 4284/0, test_batch_ah_battle 63/0, test_batch_ak 524/0, test_runes 3130/0, test_start_rune 239/0, test_start_rune_ui 31/0, run-harness gates 1/2/3 PASS. Known-bad and not ours: test_rune_battle 91/1, the standing Inferno-chip defect.
  • No difficulty measurement was run, deliberately. This is a content re-author of ONE spec, and the sim's damage-share instrument reads a fixed four-hero party, so a single-spec re-tune has no honest control row (the same reasoning Batch AK recorded). Several values are 2–3× their old rank-1 numbers by design: a hero now gets 8 nodes a run instead of a third of a 24-node tree, so per-node impact has to rise to match. Whether the whole curve needs enemy scaling is a question for after all twelve trees are authored.

2026-08-06 — Batch AK: the Swordmaster kit correction and his 24 nodes

  • Guard Change is back in the opening three; Shatterpoint is earnable in its place. Batch AH's trim named the Swordmaster's three keepers and made Guard Change earnable — and Guard Change is the only stance swap in the game. Until he earned it back he fought every battle Aggressive, half of Seasoned Fighter was inert and four talent nodes sold him nothing. AH shipped it as written and flagged it rather than reinterpreting the instruction; this is the flag being answered. Shatterpoint only accelerates a Break he reaches by other means, so it is the safe piece to move the other way. The count AH actually cared about is unchanged: three abilities at the start, six earned over a run, five trims across the twelve specs.
  • Exactly one definition of each, still. Guard Change's def moved out of trimmed_kit_ability() and into the live kit; Shatterpoint's went the other way. Neither name now exists in two places, which is the property the whole resolver arrangement is for. Guard Change is in no pool at all — the class pool has excluded it since AH (a sibling has no stance to swap), and it no longer needs a spec-pool entry.
  • All 24 tree nodes re-authored for row exclusivity. Every id survives, so saved picks migrate and no save version moves. The rows have been mutually exclusive since Batch AI, which means a node is no longer one of three ranks — it is the whole row, priced against the two doors it closes. Concretely: the stance nodes 3% → 12%, Sword Mastery 3% → 12% parry, Killing Edge 4% → 15% crit, Bracing +8 → +30 Constitution, Dominant Presence 5% → 15% armor per debuff, Precision Strikes 5% → 20% crit, Seasoned Fighter 3% → 15%, No Quarter 15 → 45 Rage, Overwhelm 3% → 8% per debuff, Punishment 15% → 60%, Tempo 10% → 30%, Off Balance 5% → 20%, Pressure Point +8 → +30 BD, High Guard 25% for 1 turn → 40% for 2.
  • Sunder Guard now points at an ability he is guaranteed. It read "Shatterpoint +8 Break damage" — an ability he may never draw, and after the correction above, one he starts without. It now re-points Guard Change: 40 Break damage to EVERY enemy instead of 15 to the single mark. The old target did not disappear so much as become a bonus — if he owns Shatterpoint anyway, that gains +40 BD too. A node aimed at a guaranteed button that pays extra on a lucky draw is strictly better than one aimed only at the draw.
  • Swordsmanship became a spike he earns rather than a number he owns. A perfect Guard Change now grants +25% parry for 2 turns (the ability's own 10% plus the node's 15). It rides the existing Parry Up chip with a live counter, and the whole value now lives in the granted status power rather than being added again at the roll — so what the chip says is exactly what the parry roll reads.
  • Riposte answers with a free Overpower instead of a basic Strike, reusing the recast machinery Opportunist already had. Opportunist now answers a parry as well as a whiff. They are the same answer bought from two different lanes and both are reachable (rows 6 and 4), so a parry fires one counter, not two; the log names the node that paid for it. The Untouchable capstone's free Pommel Strike still takes precedence in the Defensive stance.
  • Punishment and Off Balance stopped being an exclusive pair. Split across rows 6 and 7 they are both reachable, and two "+% versus Broken" numbers stacking would be redundant rather than interesting. So with Punishment also taken, Off Balance widens what counts as a window — Exposed and Crippled targets pay out too — instead of adding to the same number.
  • Lunge and Execute sit in the spec pool AND the tree, so the node upgrades a copy he already earned. Lunge drops to 15 Rage and applies both Exposed and Crippled whatever the guard; Execute's threshold rises from 20% to 35% health and it costs no Rage against a Broken target. Neither is ever granted twice, in either order of acquisition.
  • New payload machinery, two keys. also holds extra payloads applied alongside the main one, each carrying its own condition — that is how one node has two halves under different conditions (Sunder Guard's Shatterpoint hook, owns_ability; Off Balance's widening, has_node) without a second node id. upgrade holds payloads applied instead of granting when the ability is already in the kit. The batch asked for owns_ability on all three checks and it is the wrong instrument for two of them: a learned node's own grant is itself in ability_names(), so after taking the Lunge node owns_ability(member, "Lunge") is true no matter where Lunge came from. The only honest question is "was it in the kit when the tree ran", and the abilities list is what knows that. Shatterpoint's hook does use owns_ability, because no node grants Shatterpoint.
  • The Rune of the Still Wrist was repaired in passing. It wrote pommel_parry_bonus and advertised "a perfect Pommel Strike's parry blessing" — a blessing that stopped existing in Batch AH, when Pommel's perfect became the boss Stun. It has been half-dead since, and this batch's field rename would have killed it outright. It now pays into the live field and names Guard Change. That field is additive rather than a replacement for exactly this reason: a max() against the talent's value would have left the rune silently inert on its own. Rune alone = a 15% grant, node alone = 25%, both = 30%.
  • Verified: new test_batch_ak.gd, 524 checks / 0 failures, stable across three runs — the kit swap and its single-definition property, every pool entry of all twelve specs and four classes still resolving, all 24 nodes' id/row/lane/magnitude, both tooltip and payload (they are two hand-written places), both conditional halves firing and staying dark including on an empty ctx, both upgrade paths in both acquisition orders, and a live battle for the BD spread, the parry spike, the parry counter, Execute's pricing and the upgraded Lunge.
