Regression: test_batch_ai 2,042/0, test_batch_ah 4,284/0 (its Guard Change trim assertion re-pointed, with the reason in the file), test_batch_ah_battle 62/0, test_runes 3,130/0, test_start_rune 239/0, test_start_rune_ui 31/0, run-harness gates 1/2/3 PASS. Headless parse: all 10 scenes, 0 SCRIPT ERROR. One pre-existing failure, not caused by this batch and not fixed by it: test_rune_battle 91/1, the Inferno chip check — it reproduces 3 of 3 on unmodified HEAD in a clean worktree, so it is a standing defect in that check rather than a regression.
  • Measured, and flagged rather than second-guessed: Sunder Guard is the largest single node in the tree by a distance. On the fixed smoke-test lineup (60 battles, one node forced, everything else at default) it takes the Swordmaster's Break damage from 158 to 649 per battle and the party's Breaks from 1.33 to 5.43 per battle — 4.1x on both. His own damage share falls, 29% to 23%, because he spends those turns pivoting instead of swinging; what he is really doing is opening windows for the other three. That is the node as the batch specified it (40 BD to every enemy, on a 0-Rage 1-cooldown button), so it ships as written and this is the note rather than a quiet nerf. The smoke-test lineup carries no difficulty signal (Batch R) — these are kit-mechanics ratios, not a balance verdict.
  • Not in this batch: the other ten trees and enemy tuning, both untouched as instructed. No difficulty measurement was run — this is a content re-author of one spec, and the sim's damage-share instrument reads a fixed four-hero party, so a single-spec re-tune has no honest control row.

2026-08-06 — Fix: a rune triple could offer the same rune twice

  • The pick-of-3 rune offer could contain a duplicate — roughly one triple in a hundred, which surfaced as an intermittent test failure on the Warden and, in play, as an awakening or elite cache that presented the same choice twice. The roller drew each candidate independently and, on a collision, rerolled up to four times — then appended the result unchecked. Two ways to lose: every reroll could collide, and the last reroll was never tested at all (the check sat at the top of the retry loop, so four checks covered five draws). On a small per-rarity pool both were reachable.
  • Fixed by drawing without replacement instead of rolling and hoping. The names already in the triple now ride the same exclusion channel the owned pouch already used — a rune sitting in the offer is exactly as unavailable as one already worn — so the pool a candidate is picked from cannot contain its siblings. A duplicate is not merely unlikely, it is unreachable. Measured 0 duplicates in 4,000 triples on both the guaranteed and the ordinary path, where the old roller produced them on both.
  • The default path changed too, deliberately. It had the same defect (it just needed three unlucky draws rather than a seeded one), so the fix reaches it, and its roll sequence moves: fewer random numbers, because the wasted retries are gone, and a smaller pool for draws two and three. The Batch AF test that pinned this path against a verbatim copy of the old loop was re-transcribed to the corrected one — keeping the old copy frozen would have asserted the bug had to stay forever. Its distinctness assertion is new: the default path never had one, which is why only the guaranteed path ever caught this, and only sometimes.

2026-08-06 — Batch AI: talent tree structure — seven rows, eight decisions

  • Every tree is now seven mutually exclusive ROWS plus a capstone row. A row holds one node per lane and the player picks exactly one of the three; that pick shuts the other two permanently. They stay visible and greyed with the reason in the tooltip — the player must see the door they closed — and a node not yet taken names the two doors taking it would shut. Rows were assigned mechanically from the existing data: within each lane, nodes ordered by their old tier then by definition order, numbered 1–7. Every lane had exactly seven non-capstone nodes, so the mapping was one-to-one and nothing moved on screen. exclusive_with is gone — sixteen authored pairs across nine specs, all superseded by row exclusivity.
  • Row 1 is open from the start; row N opens when row N−1 has been picked. The capstones become row 8: open at 7/7, take one, from any lane — the lane-purity requirement is gone. Every node costs 1 point and holds a single rank, no exceptions. A complete tree is 8 nodes.
  • THE POINT SUPPLY NOW MATCHES THE TREE EXACTLY. 1 for confirming a spec (paid at the awakening, from both the spec screen and the sim's start_run — the sync_spec_hp pattern), 1 per mini-boss, 2 per zone boss, 0 for regular fights, 0 for the final boss. A three-zone run guarantees 1 + 3 + 4 = 8 points, exactly one complete tree. The question stops being "how many nodes can I afford" and becomes "which eight".
  • ELITE POINTS ARE A SEPARATE PURSE (§6 of the brief, taken rather than cut). An elite kill pays 1 — 0–3 a run. They cannot open a new row. The only thing they buy is a second node in a row already picked, forcing that one row's door back open; the third node in that row stays shut forever. This is what makes hunting elites read as "widen a choice I already made" rather than "climb the tree faster", and it keeps the guaranteed 8 honest. Normal points can never buy a second node in a row, and the sim's bot spends normal points before flex (flex can never open a row, so banking a normal point to spend flex is strictly worse).
  • Retired machinery, deleted rather than left unreachable: LANE_TIER_REQ, NODE_TIER_REQ, CAPSTONE_REQ, NODE_CAPSTONE_REQ, ROW_REQ, FIXED_TREES, LANE_CONVERSIONS, next_node_cost, node_cost, lane_nodes_bought, lane_points, points_below_row, is_node_gated, is_lane_tree, the node_gated flag, the per-node tier field, and member["talent_order"] (which existed only to replay the ceil(N/3) price curve). The party screen's classic-tree renderer went with FIXED_TREES. desc_for() stays — it already rendered {v} at rank 1 for unlearned previews, which is now the only case there is.
  • HOOKS FOR THE FOUR CLASS BATCHES (§5), built but not yet used by any node. Talents.has_node(learned, id) — was this node taken. Talents.owns_ability(member, name) — does the hero have this ability from ANY source: starting kit, kit override, mini-boss/boss pick, or talent grant. A payload may now carry a condition ({"has_node": id} / {"owns_ability": name}, or both), evaluated at ONE read site inside apply_payload; a conditional payload with no context is inert rather than silently unconditional, because an effect that fails to appear is a bug you can see. Both statics are callable from battle.gd at resolve time.
  • owned_ability_names moved from Run to Talents, and this is load-bearing. §5 asked owns_ability to read the same list the action bar reads, and it could not: GDScript does not resolve autoload identifiers inside a class_name script (the same constraint that makes RunSim take Run injected). So the implementation now lives as Talents.ability_names(member) and Run.owned_ability_names forwards to it — one list, not two copies that could drift. The Talents → Runes → Classes → Talents cycle this creates resolves fine (verified headless, not assumed).
  • Save migration: save version 6. A save from before this batch holds learned talents under the old ranked, point-priced scheme — ranks that no longer exist, bought at prices that no longer exist — so there is no honest translation. The tree is WIPED and the purse re-issued on the new schedule: 1 for the spec choice, 2 per zone boss cleared, 1 per mini-boss cleared (zone bosses counted by zone_idx, since a zone is only left by killing its boss; the mini-boss sits at a fixed tier, so "cleared" is "past that tier"). The player re-picks their eight rows with a full purse. Same-version saves keep the old behaviour.
  • Test coverage: new test_batch_ai.gd (2042 checks) pins the shape of all twelve trees, the row gating, the elite crack including that no row ever holds three, the hooks, payload conditions, the 8-point economy and the migration. The run harness's gate 2 (talent spend conservation) was rewritten for flat pricing and now also asserts a fed hero lands on a complete tree with a capstone. test_runes.gd's exclusive-pair alarm was RETIRED, with its reasoning left in the file: it watched authored pairs, and at row granularity every spec rune in the game would trip it — that is the alarm asking a question the design no longer answers the same way. Whether a rune should grant a counter whose node sits in a row the player passed on is a real question for the class batches to answer with the nodes, and then re-arm.
  • EXPECTED STATE, stated plainly: the tree is structurally correct and numerically weak. Rows are lopsided, because the 21 existing nodes per spec were authored to stack within a lane rather than compete across a row — Savagery and Hemorrhage were built to be taken together and now sit as alternatives. Several rows have one obviously correct pick. Single-rank values are the old rank-1 values, so roughly a third of their intended power. None of this was tuned or re-homed to compensate: the four class batches re-author all 252 nodes.
    Measured, 30 runs each side, same flags (route=default, map=new, standard, all shipped content on): depth 17.10±1.55 → 13.40±0.86 (bands do not overlap — runs now die a full zone earlier); wipe median z2 t7.5 → z1 t11.0; talent nodes owned entering a boss 5.0 → 2.6; points earned per hero per run 17.4 → 3.6 (partly the new schedule, partly that shorter runs collect less — a run that went the distance would bank exactly 8). ratio@z1t8 is flat, 0.97 → 0.98, and completions are 0% both sides: the party's raw stat ratio barely moved, so what was lost is the conditional and proc power the extra ranks were paying for. These are readings of placeholder content, not a difficulty signal — do not tune against them until the nodes are written.

2026-08-05 — Batch AH: ability progression — three to start, six to earn

  • Every hero now opens a run with its core attack plus exactly THREE spec abilities, and earns six more over the run. Nothing was written for this batch: every earnable ability already existed as a kit piece, a talent grant, or a vaulted one whose machinery never left battle.gd. Trimmed out of their opening kits and made earnable: the Berserker's Blood Price; the Warden's War Stomp and Interpose; the Swordmaster's Sweeping Strikes and Guard Change. Pyromancer, Cryomancer, Arcanist, Holy, Devout, Occultist, Sharpshooter and Survivalist already held exactly three and were left alone — and so was the Beastmaster, which is already at three under the batch's own counting rule: the Summon Companion picker is ONE slot however many beasts it offers (the action bar has always grouped them that way), plus Hunter's Instinct and Kill Command.
  • WARDEN AND SWORDMASTER — the judgment calls, and their reasons. The Warden keeps Mocking Blow (his only 0-cost Rage generator: without it he cannot afford his own opening), Crushing Blow (his damage and his Sunder) and Shieldwall (the Block identity Batch G deliberately promoted out of the tree and into the base kit). War Stomp is a mid-fight AoE, and Batch AB already found that Interpose and Shieldwall are the same verb pointed two ways — so the redundant half is the one that becomes earnable. GUARD CHANGE IS FLAGGED, NOT ARGUED WITH. The batch named the Swordmaster's three keepers explicitly, which leaves Guard Change earnable — and Guard Change is the ONLY way to swap stance, so until a Swordmaster earns it back he fights every battle Aggressive and half of Seasoned Fighter is inert. His tree still sells four stance nodes that would sit dead beside it. That is a real consequence of an explicit instruction, so it shipped as written and is recorded here rather than quietly reinterpreted.
  • Two pools, and a curation rule with teeth. SPEC_POOLS now covers all twelve specs: each spec's own trims, its talent-granted abilities, and its own vaulted ones. CLASS_POOLS is new — four entries holding the sibling specs' abilities plus the class's vaulted ones. An entry only reaches a class pool if it FUNCTIONS for a sibling. Excluded and why: Hymn of Hope, Resurrection, Divine Plea (faith_cost — Mercy is Holy-only, Faith is Devout-only, and a sibling has no stacks to pay with); Detonation and Wildfire (Burn consumers, and Wildfire is hard-gated with nothing alight); Shatter (hard-gated with nobody Chilled); Arcane Cannon, Magi's Wrath, Stabilize, Overcharge (Resonance — the first two scale their damage AND their entire Break contribution off stacks a sibling never builds, so they land as strictly-worse basics; the last two are outright no-ops); Bestial Wrath, Spirit Bond, Primal Surge (a living companion or Loyalty); Coup de Grâce (consumes all Focus); Lunge and Guard Change (the Swordmaster's stance — stance defaults to "aggressive" on every unit, so a sibling's Lunge could only ever Expose and never Cripple, which is half an ability); and the three named identity pieces, the summons, Kill Command and Hex of Ruin. Kept with their inert riders noted rather than excluded: Hold Breath and Aimed Shot (the Focus they grant is a rider; a guaranteed armour-ignoring crit and a 45% hit are the abilities), Arcane Surge and Reality Fracture (the +20% attack and the six-point initiative shove are the abilities; the Resonance line is a rider), Mark of the Hunt (its +25% keys on the caster's index, not on the Pack passive — only the Mana drip is Beastmaster-only), Hunter's Instinct (it empowers Quick Shot, which every Hunter spec has).
  • The vault, unsealed — and the five that could not come back. Returning as earnable picks, all on machinery that never left battle.gd: Rallying Shout (warrior), Retaliation (Warden), Mana Shield, Arcane Surge, Reality Fracture, Phoenix Rebirth (mage), Dawnbreak, Sanctuary, Divine Wrath (cleric), Umbral Sigil (Occultist). Pyroblast, Flame Surge, Frost Bolt, Death Ray and Mend Wounds did NOT: their special handlers went with them, so they exist only as prose in a comment and reviving them would mean AUTHORING them — which this batch's own opening line forbids. The three restored abilities whose full dicts survived (Mana Shield, Arcane Surge, Reality Fracture) came back verbatim; the other seven needed a cost/cooldown/initiative wrapper around effects the code already defines exactly, and those numbers are new and are the one place this batch made a balance judgment.
  • NOT DONE, and it is a contradiction in the batch, not an omission. §2 asks for six UNSHIPPED abilities to be added — Disengage, Suppressing Fire and Piercing Arrow for the Sharpshooter, Blight, Smoke Bomb and Field Dressing for the Survivalist. The batch's own first paragraph says "No new abilities are written in this batch", and no costs, damage, mechanics or descriptions were given for any of the six. Authoring them would have been six abilities' worth of undirected design, so the header rule won and the two Hunter pools ship at five entries each. Nothing is blocked by this — the cross-fill rule keeps every offer at three regardless.
  • Each award offers 3 abilities the hero does not own: 1 from its spec pool, 2 from its class pool. Either side fills for the other when its own runs dry, so an offer only shrinks below three once BOTH are nearly exhausted; measured over all twelve specs, six awards a run, every offer stays at three. THE SPEC DRAW LEADS THE OFFER and wears the trophy gold on the Party screen. That is a deliberate departure from the rune picker, which shuffles its triple: there the blindness IS the question, but the two ability pools OVERLAP by design (a spec's own talent grants sit in both), so nothing in the NAME says which pool an entry came from and hiding it would just withhold something the player has earned the right to know. The triple is rolled the moment the award drops and stored on the member (the rune-cache pattern), so re-opening the screen never rerolls it. Storage stays bm_abilities; the new bm_candidates queue is another key on the party dict, so the save stays v5 with no migration, and a run saved before this batch that owes a pick rolls its triple on the spot rather than dead-ending.
  • A SPAWN-ORDERING BUG, found by making abilities earnable. Earned abilities used to be appended AFTER Talents.apply_from_tree. Several talents MODIFY an ability rather than grant one — and Battle Shout is both talent-granted and talent-modified, so a Berserker who bought Deafening Cry and then EARNED Battle Shout from the pool would have had the -1 cooldown silently miss it. Earned abilities now go on BEFORE the tree; apply_from_tree already refuses to double-grant a name it can see, so the talent-bought path is unchanged.
  • ONE GUARANTEED MINI-BOSS PER ZONE, on the middle row. Tier 6 is now the zone's mini-boss and it occupies all three columns, so no route goes around it. It is a combat node between elite and boss: an elite warband carrying a boss-tier health pool — every enemy at ×1.5 max HP, attack untouched, which puts a chief's 250 at 375, in among the two authored bosses at 370 and 500. It pays elite gold (80–100) and 2 talent points, and its own spoil is an ability pick — no consumable, no rune cache, so the rune economy AE and AF measured is left alone. The zone boss now awards an ability pick too, on top of its existing 3 talent points. Two picks a zone, six a run.
  • The map deal absorbs it without touching the run economy. The mini-boss row is AUTHORED, not dealt, so the deck now deals 27 cards into nine tiers — and it is paid for out of the FIGHT cards alone (17 → 14). Rest 5, shop 5 and event 3 are byte-identical, which is the point: a zone trades three ordinary fight OFFERS for one guaranteed hard fight, and the economy dials that drive gold, healing and rune access do not move. The structural constraints are now stated in TIER terms and converted to card slices, so lifting a row out of the deal cannot silently shift a window; a second 27-card authored fallback (DECK_FALLBACK_MB) matches the new composition. Elites still roll at 25% on tiers 6+, which in practice means tiers 7–10 now, with the existing one-elite-per-zone floor unchanged.
  • FLAG: DOD_SIM_MINIBOSS=off reproduces the pre-AH map — ten dealt tiers, the full 17-fight deck, no mini-boss and so no mini-boss award. Shipped content, so it defaults ON and, like the AE and AF flags and unlike AD's experiment arms, it is NOT gated on sim_run. It is an honest control for the MAP half of this batch only: kit sizes still open at three with it off, because the trim is structural and has no switch. Post-AH Matrix rows carry mb=; pre-AH rows do not, so never compare across.
  • The action bar has no cap and no swap step. A hero holds every ability it earns. Abilities 1–9 keep their hotkeys — with G added as the ninth, which the batch's own worked example requires ("ability 10 = Shift+Q" only lands if nine plain keys exist) — and ability 10 onward binds to Shift + the same key in the same order, up to 18 at Shift+G. The button carries the modifier: "[⇧Q] Blood Price". Shift hotkeys route through the same _ability_usable gate and the same two fenced call sites, so they are ignored when the ability is unaffordable or gated and inactive during targeting or a skill check. A real run tops out at core + 3 + 6 = 10 abilities, so exactly one Shift slot is reachable in play; the harness stresses it to eighteen anyway.
  • Eight Perfect skill checks became RELIABILITY checks. In each the Perfect removes a chance roll instead of adding magnitude, and the old bonus is gone: Hack and Slash lands its Bleed on all three strikes (loses the 4th strike); Blizzard puts 2 stacks of Chilled on every enemy instead of 1–2 (loses the 5-Mana refund); Pommel Strike lands its Stun on an unbroken boss (loses the parry buff); Snare Trap the same (loses the extra Poison turns — Poison is a flat 4 either way now); Firestorm spreads its bolts evenly across the living instead of scattering them (loses the extra bolts); Arcane Barrage never strikes the same enemy twice while an unstruck one stands (loses the 7th bolt); Triple Shot pays out one guaranteed critical; and Deadfall gained a skill check it never had so it could have a Perfect at all — a perfect rig lets the player NAME the victim. Wildstrikes was already converted in Batch AG and was left alone.
  • Three implementation notes from that conversion. (1) The two scatter perfects — Firestorm and Arcane Barrage — are ONE mechanic under two framings: deal the target pool round-robin and refill it only once it empties. "Every enemy takes an even share" and "no two bolts strike the same enemy" are the same rule described from different ends. (2) The boss Stun immunity now has exactly one escape, an explicit force argument on _apply_status rather than a name check inside it — so the exception is visible at the call site that bought it, and Freeze, Psychosis, Bewitch and Hysteria are untouched. (3) An AIMED Deadfall waits for its mark, and releases if the mark dies first, so a perfect rig can never be worth less than an ordinary one.
  • A LIVE BUG, exposed by the rule. Triple Shot fired TWO arrows. Its multi_hits was 2 while its name, its description ("Three arrows at one target") and the master document all said three — and because perfect_extra_hit defaults to true and its perfect_text was empty, a Perfect silently added the third with no tooltip anywhere admitting it. Removing the undocumented magnitude perfect is what this batch's rule demands, and doing it would have left an ability called Triple Shot firing two arrows forever. multi_hits is now 3 and the extra hit is off.
  • Two knock-ons the conversion left behind, cleaned up. (1) Swordsmanship read "a perfect Pommel Strike grants +5%/rank more parry chance" — and Pommel Strike's perfect is now the boss Stun. The mechanic was ALREADY shared with Guard Change's perfect, so the node is re-pointed in place: same id (saved ranks migrate), same stat, wording moved to the source that still pays it. (2) The parry_up and mana5 perfect_id handlers had no callers left — Pommel Strike and Blizzard were the only ones — so they are gone. Guard Change applies its own parry buff inline and is unaffected. Nothing in runes.json or the talent trees writes a perfect_id except rage5, so the two removals are provably unreachable rather than probably.
  • Visibility. Owed ability picks badge the map's Party button in the trophy gold and pulse it (rune purple still outranks them), and the Party screen's hero list marks the owed row — the same affordance chain Batch AE built for runes, because a pick left unchosen is a hero walking into the next tier a third weaker than intended. The picker's heading is now "NEW ABILITY — choose one" with the subtitle "one from your spec, two from your class"; the map gains a WARDEN node label in a colour between the elite's orange and the boss's red, and a "Mini-boss" entry in the debug summon menu.
  • VERIFIED. New test_batch_ah.gd: 4,263 checks, 0 failures — every spec's kit counted at three, all 100 pool entries resolved to an Ability, the curation rule asserted rather than trusted (no faith_cost, no summon/Kill Command/Hex of Ruin, no passive-gated special in any class pool), pool copies proven identical to the kit and talent copies they mirror, the offer shape and the six-award sequence checked for all twelve specs, cross-fill and both-pools-dry forced, a talent-learned ability proven un-offerable, and the mini-boss row proven to be exactly one row, on the middle tier, full width, with a forward DP showing no route reaches the boss without crossing it. New test_batch_ah_battle.gd: 41 checks, 0 failures in a spawned battle — an earned class-pool ability reaching the kit, Deafening Cry finding a POOL-bought Battle Shout (the ordering fix), the action bar handing slot 10 its ⇧Q, the boss Stun immunity holding for an ordinary cast and yielding to a forced one through both routes (_apply_status and _spring_trap), Freeze immunity proven untouched by the new argument's default, and mini-boss enemies measured against their own elite selves. Regression: test_runes.gd 3,248/0, test_start_rune.gd 233/0, test_start_rune_ui.gd 31/0, test_run_harness.gd gates 1/2/3 PASS, test_rune_battle.gd 91/1 — the same single failure the unmodified code gives 6 runs out of 6 (a Batch AG Inferno-chip check that reads the chip before any Burn is on the field). That harness needed one fix, and the fix is worth recording: its White Flame check reads a LOG LINE, which made it a race against how long a fight lasts — and trimming the Berserker's kit shortened fights enough that the Pyromancer sometimes never took a turn (0 fire casts in ~50% of runs; restoring Blood Price made it 6/6 again, which is how the cause was pinned). A check that depends on the bot happening to get a turn cannot fail honestly, so the pass now FORCES one fire hit onto a resistant enemy instead of hoping for one: 6/6 stable, and the flake is gone rather than tolerated. Headless parse: all 10 scenes, 0 SCRIPT ERROR.
  • MEASURED (n=100 runs each, route=default, control = mb=off). THE ACQUISITION HALF LANDED: abilities earned per hero per run 1.18 → 3.02 (offers 3.54 → 9.06), and the ceiling of 6 is not the target — the average run only reaches 2.0 mini-bosses and 1.3 zone bosses, so 3.02 is very nearly every award a run actually survives to collect. Talent points 19.1 → 21.0 and gold 711 → 808, both exactly the mini-boss's own payout. Mini-boss nodes taken 2.0 of 2.0 offered: unroutable in practice, not just on paper.
    THE DIFFICULTY HALF DID NOT MOVE, on the metric built to see it: depth 20.15 → 20.49 = +0.34 tiers against a resolvable difference of 3.57 — nowhere near clearing, and remarkable in itself, since the treatment fights two extra hard battles a run to reach the same place. ratio@z1t8 1.00 → 0.95, flat. Wipe median moved z2 t8 → z2 t10, deeper.
    REPORTED, NOT ACTED ON — the one row that disagrees. Completions read 9% → 0%, which nominally clears its own 8.2-point threshold (z=3.07). It is recorded rather than believed, for two reasons stated together: (a) Batch AE's noise calibration measured 7% and 14% from two identical halves of one arm, so a 9-point completion swing is inside this instrument's known noise, and (b) an earlier n=100 pass over the same code — discarded because two background jobs wrote the same file and corrupted it — produced completion rows of 12% and 4%. Depth is the low-variance metric and depth says nothing moved.
    ROUTE AGENCY IS THE ONE REAL COST, and it is by design: steps offering a genuine choice 77% → 71%, and rests taken 2.3 → 1.9. A row with one node type in all three columns is a row with no decision in it — that is what "cannot be routed around" buys, and one tier in eleven is the price. Fights taken fall 14.4 → 12.7 and elites 1.1 → 0.9, both the arithmetic of three fight cards leaving the deck.
    PURITY: profile.json and relics.json byte-identical across both arms; run_save.bin never created.

2026-08-05 — Batch AG: combat tuning — reliability, Burn turns, and the tempo of self-buffs

  • Wildstrikes trades magnitude for reliability. The sweep had no bleed_chance, so it defaulted to 1.0 and put a guaranteed 35 Bleed on every enemy — 140 buildup across a four-wide field, every cast, with no roll to lose. It now carries bleed_chance 0.5 like Hack and Slash, and the Perfect changed what it buys: instead of +50% buildup on every target it now guarantees the roll on all of them (four × 35, no bonus). The skill check is a reliability check now, not a magnitude one — which is what a Perfect on a wide, swingy ability should be. The per-target roll already lived in the strike loop; the Perfect simply treats the chance as 1.0 for the cast.
  • Inferno Master counts Burn TURNS, not burning heads. The passive read the number of enemies alight, which meant a single enemy burning for six turns was worth exactly as much as one burning for one, and the Pyromancer's own build-the-fire loop (Flamewave extensions, Conflagration, Accelerant) fed it nothing. It now reads the total Burn turns standing on the enemy team: step = 1.0 + 0.2 × Pyromaniac ranks percent per turn, cap = 25 × step + 10 × Heat Haze ranks percent. Ceilings: +25% base, +40% at Pyromaniac 3, +70% with Heat Haze 3 on top (was 64%). Pyromaniac now raises the cap along with the step — the old node bought a deeper step against a fixed ceiling. Avatar of Flame still removes the cap and still burns through fire resistance.
  • Consumption ordering, which was a live bug. Detonation removed its target's Burn before the passive block ran, so the cast that spent the field was paid on the field it had already emptied. Both Detonation and the reworked Wildfire now measure the total before their own consumption: the consuming cast gets full credit, and only subsequent turns see the thinner field.
  • Wildfire is the wide payoff now. The old version copied one target's Burn to everyone at half duration — a setup tool that made the Pyromancer's own Detonation worse by diluting the deep fire he was building. It is now a special with no target selection: 20 Mana, 3cd, 2.5 initiative, 10 BD, consuming one turn of Burn from every burning enemy, each taking 18% of Attack in fire for it. An enemy on its last turn simply loses its Burn; enemies that are not burning are untouched. Perfect takes two turns from each, dealing the damage twice to anyone holding 2+. Detonation is unchanged — the two now ask different questions of the same field (one deep fire, or a wide one).
  • Wildfire cannot be cast into an empty field, on the same rule as Shatter: with nothing alight the ability does literally nothing, so it is gated out of the menu rather than eating 20 Mana and a turn. The autoplay/sim pyromancer loop was retargeted with it — Flamewave builds, Wildfire harvests at 3+ burning, Detonation still cashes the deepest single fire.
  • Battle Shout is a party-wide warcry. It was a self-buff scaling off the enemy party's bloodloss, which meant it read as a warcry and behaved as a solo cooldown — and at low buildup it was worth nearly nothing. Power is now 8 + (total enemy bleed buildup / 20) and it lands on every living hero (companions excluded — the beast's damage runs through its own path). Duration 2 turns and the +5 Rage Perfect are unchanged; Deafening Cry still takes a turn off its cooldown. The damage clause already read the status per unit, so it works party-wide untouched.
  • Razor Ice: damage 20 → 15, cost 20 → 25. Three shards, 3 Chilled stacks, 3cd and the 25%-of-Attack Perfect all unchanged. This is the first cut against the Cryomancer overtune Batch S identified and Batch U confirmed at the honest floor (38–40% damage share across every budget and every routing policy) — his numbers, not his AoE.
  • Self-buff initiative costs cut 0.5 (floor 1.5). Buffing yourself or the party costs a turn and the tempo of a long initiative delay, and the double charge was pushing whole categories of ability out of real rotations. Applied to the fifteen named: Blood Price, Battle Shout, Shieldwall, Flame Shield, Stabilize and Spirit Bond → 1.5; Interpose and Blessing of Zeal → 2.0; Divine Shield, Sacred Resolve and Hunter's Instinct → 2.5; Consecrated Ground, Bulwark of Fortitude and Hold the Line → 3.0; Bestial Wrath → 3.5.
  • The sweep for unlisted abilities matching the rule (a special handler, damage: 0, no healing, no enemy target) found five: Quick Draw and Overcharge (both named in advance) and Hold Breath → 1.5, Tripwire → 2.0, Hold the Line → 3.0. Judged out, and why: Deadfall, Snare Trap and Mass Hysteria are pointed at the enemy team even though they need no target pick; Dark Pact heals; the three Summons field a unit rather than buff anyone, and Aguila takes an enemy target, so including them would have split the trio; Guard Change already sits at the 1.5 floor. Heals were excluded by the rule — Heal, Renewal, Hymn of Hope, Divine Plea and Resurrection keep their delays.
  • Bug: Call of the Wild permanently locked a Lone Bond hunter out of summoning. Call of the Wild writes kinds_summoned[kind] = true for all three beasts as bookkeeping for Feral Momentum and Menagerie. The Lone Bond gate in _ability_usable read not kinds_summoned.is_empty() as "you have spent your one beast" — so a single Call of the Wild marked all three kinds and closed every summon for the rest of the fight. Fix: a separate beast_committed flag on BattleUnit, set only in _do_summon (a real summon), and the gate reads that. kinds_summoned is untouched — Feral Momentum and Menagerie keep reading it and Call of the Wild keeps writing it. The AI path goes through _ability_usable, so autoplay and the sims inherit the fix.
  • Verified. Headless parse clean on all four touched scripts; 40-battle sims on the standard party and on a pyromancer party — 0 SCRIPT ERRORs, Wildfire firing 0.8×/battle through the new handler. Autoplay log checks confirm the Wildstrikes Perfect landing 35 (not 52) on all four targets, and Wildfire tearing 1 turn from 6 enemies for 101 and 2 turns from 5 for 96 on a Perfect.

2026-08-04 — Batch AF: the opening pick offers a rune for your spec

  • Why — the number that prompted it. Batch AE shipped the starting rune and measured it delivering 0.49 spec runes worn per hero against the 1.18 projected. AE's own diagnosis found the cause and it was not the lever: the ordinary roller put a spec-scoped rune among the three only 36–42% of the time, and the sim's policy — which already prefers a spec candidate — took one at exactly that rate, i.e. whenever one was there. The ceiling was the roller's hit rate, not the policy's. A player sits under the same ceiling, and the opening pick is the most visible gear decision in a run. This batch raises the offer rate and changes nothing else.
  • Guarantee, not weighting — and that was the design choice. The opening triple always contains one spec-scoped candidate where the hero's spec pool has an eligible entry; the other two roll normally. A weighting nudges a probability and leaves the outcome mushy. A guarantee makes the opening pick a reliable question — do I take the rune written for my spec, or the generic one that is stronger right now? — and that question can only be asked if it is always on the table. One candidate, not two: a triple of spec runes is not a question, it is a menu.
  • Only the opening pick. Run.roll_rune_candidates is shared with the elite cache, so the guarantee is an opt-in parameter defaulted off and exactly one caller arms it. The elite cache and every other caller produce identical rolls. This is asserted, not assumed — the test seeds the global RNG and replays a verbatim copy of the pre-AF loop against the default path, because "it looks the same" proves nothing about a random roll, and it re-checks the call sites so a future batch cannot quietly arm it.
  • What the guaranteed candidate is. A normal roll within the spec-scoped subset — same rarity roll on the zone slot, same widen-on-exhaustion — not a forced Epic. Measured over 600 rolls per spec, the four specs that own an Epic draw it ~25% of the time, which is what an unconstrained draw over their four gives. The three candidates stay distinct, and the guaranteed one is shuffled into a random slot so its position is not a tell. Fallback, silent and safe: if the spec subset is empty — an unmet requires_ability, an exhausted set — the pick falls back to an ordinary roll of three. No error, no empty slot, no half-filled triple.
  • What this batch is not. Not a magnitude pass — runes.json is byte-unchanged for the third batch running, and that is the intent, not an accident. The designer has closed the rune-magnitude question; the six magnitude-proof entries Batch AE catalogued are left exactly as they are. No change to the elite cache, the shop roll or any other rune source. No second acquisition lever. No sim policy change — _pick_rune_candidate already preferred a spec candidate, which is precisely why raising the offer rate is the whole intervention.
  • Measured — the acquisition half, and it is near-deterministic. Fixed party, n=200 per row, control is specopen=off (AE's opening with the starting rune still on, so the pair isolates this change rather than re-measuring AE).
    Offer rate 41% → 100%, with ZERO fallbacks across 800 opening triples — no spec hit the fallback path, which is expected: all twelve specs have four entries and the only gates on them are core kit abilities.
    Spec runes worn per hero at run end: 0.56 → 1.15 (AE measured 0.49; the control reproduces it). The pick was spec-scoped 100% of the time against the control's 41% — the policy's rate tracks the offer rate exactly, which is AE's diagnosis confirmed from the other side.
    The finding worth keeping: this is a COMPOSITION change, not an acquisition-volume one. Runes acquired per hero barely moved (1.62 → 1.67) and slots filled barely moved (1.58 → 1.64 of 2.7–2.8 available, 59% → 58%). What changed is what fills them — spec 0.56 → 1.15 while class 0.16 → 0.10, universal 0.32 → 0.16 and stat sticks 0.54 → 0.23. The party does not wear more runes; it wears authored spec content where it used to wear filler.
  • Measured — the difficulty half, reported as information. There is no gate to fail here and no target to hit; nothing in this batch is conditional on the number. Depth 15.89 → 17.14 = +1.25 tiers against a resolvable difference of 2.72 (n=200, 80% power) — it does not clear, and is reported as movement inside noise, not as an effect. Completions 7% → 6% (resolvable 6.9 pts), ratio@z1t8 0.97 → 0.98, wipe median identical at z1 t11.0. Consistent with Batch AD's and AE's central claim that acquisition alone moves nothing detectable — and the composition finding above explains why: the party wears about the same number of runes, just better-targeted ones. Nothing was compensated, because there is nothing measurable to compensate.
  • Reported, not acted on. No spec rune in the pool is Common — all 44 are Rare plus 4 Epics. So inside the spec subset the zone-slot-1 Common lean has almost nothing to bind on, and the guaranteed slot is always Rare-or-better where it used to be ~60% Common. "The opening gift stays modest" is delivered by the pool's own composition rather than by the rarity weighting; a designer who wants the opening gift smaller would author Common spec entries, not re-weight the roll. Also still outstanding: treasure /Loot, untouched since Batch AC, six batches.
  • Flag and Matrix rows. DOD_SIM_SPEC_OPENING=off reproduces AE's opening while leaving the starting rune itself ON. Like the AE flag and unlike Batch AD's two arms it is shipped content, so it is on by default and not gated on sim_run — a control a real build cannot reach is a control nobody can reproduce. Post-AF Matrix rows carry specopen=; pre-AF rows do not and are not comparable.
  • Verified. test_start_rune.gd extended 131 → 233 checks, 0 failures — AE's 131 pass unchanged (the run prints the AE and AF subtotals separately) and AF adds 102: the guarantee holding across 250 rolls for each of all twelve specs, not the four a fixed party draws; the seeded-RNG replay proving the default path unchanged; the fallback forced two ways (a hero who already owns all four of their spec's runes, and a member with no spec) and asserted to yield three valid distinct candidates and no error; the Epic share bounded so rarity weighting cannot be bypassed; the guaranteed rune proven never to be a stat stick and never pinned to one slot; the flag's default, its off state, and its independence from sim_run. test_start_rune_ui.gd 31/0 unchanged, test_runes.gd 3,248/0, test_rune_battle.gd 91/0. test_run_harness.gd gates 1/2/3 PASS, rerun before either --run row was trusted. Purity: profile.json and relics.json byte-identical across a real DOD_SIM_RUN, run_save.bin never created. Headless parse: all ten scenes, 0 SCRIPT ERROR.

2026-08-04 — Batch AE: the runes turn on, and the magnitude pass does not

  • Why. Batch AD established that rune power and rune dilution were never alternatives — they are two factors of one product, both near zero, each masking the other — and it deliberately shipped no content change, putting the acquisition lever in front of the designer instead. The designer chose: a starting rune at the awakening. On AD's own reading that is +1.00 rune per hero, landing in zone 1 where runs actually die, and visible to a player inside the first ten minutes. This batch ships that lever. It was also meant to ship the magnitude that lever requires, and it does not — the evidence did not survive its own replication, and that refusal is the batch's second deliverable.
  • The rule that changed, stated plainly. Difficulty has been closed since 08-02 pending human playtesters. This batch knowingly moves it, because turning runes on is the largest single difficulty lever in the project and there is no version of fixing the rune system that does not move it. The designer accepted this explicitly. What it did not license: no target (there is no completion rate this batch aimed at), no compensation (nothing was nerfed to offset the gain), and no second lever.
  • Stage 1 — the starting rune, and where it is granted. Not at the draft: at spec confirmation. Specs are chosen after the draft, and spec-scoped runes are the authored content that currently reaches nobody — a rune rolled before a hero has a spec cannot be one of them. The hook is the run-start beat in spec_choice where Profile.note_run_started was already booked: specs locked, party built, before the first map exists.
    What is granted is a pick of three, per hero — not a random grant. Batch X's own reasoning for making the elite cache a pick-of-3 was that "a random rune on a random hero was the least agency in the game", and the opening pick is the most visible gear decision in a run. It rolls through the existing Run.roll_rune_candidates with its normal scope eligibility and rarity weights (zone slot 1 leans Common — an appropriately modest opening gift), queues on the member exactly as an elite cache does, and resolves on the Party screen through the same trophy-picker. No new gear machinery exists in this batch. Two slots are already open at run start, so the pick is worn immediately.
    Exactly once per hero per run, and it is the saved data that says so. A start_rune_granted marker rides inside the party dict, so a v5 save carries it with no version bump and no migration, and neither a resumed run nor the debug spec swap (which re-opens the spec screen) can deal a second one. Save version stays v5.
  • Stage 1 — the player cannot walk past it. Four owed picks sitting silently on the Party screen would defeat the entire point of choosing a front-loaded lever, so the existing owed-work affordance carries it rather than a new modal. The map's Party button badges (4 runes!), takes the rune purple (which outranks the talent gold), and pulses until the picks are spent. The Party screen opens on the hero list, one click short of the picker, so each owed hero's row reads ◆ 1 RUNE TO CHOOSE in the same purple — a gap found by testing the real scenes rather than reasoning about them. A one-shot first-map toast points at the Party screen, and it chains off the Batch Z framing card's dismissal so the two never talk over each other.
    The sim gets it too, resolved through the existing _pick_rune_candidate policy — otherwise the harness would measure a different game than the one shipping. DOD_SIM_START_RUNE=off reproduces the pre-AE economy. Default on, and unlike AD's two arms it is not gated on sim_run, because it is shipped content rather than an experiment. Post-AE Matrix rows carry a start= field; pre-AE rows do not, so never compare across.
  • Stage 2 — the correction that made the sweep necessary. AD's "detectable at ×1.5, unambiguous by ×2" was measured with rich acquisition held on — all four slots open from tier 1 plus a granted rune at every zone half-mark, which delivered 2.93–3.92 runes acquired per hero. One starting rune delivers about a third of that. A dose-response curve cannot be read across acquisition levels, so authoring ×1.5 on the strength of AD's number would have been authoring against conditions that will not exist in the shipped game. The whole sweep was re-run at the acquisition level this batch actually ships: starting rune on, DOD_SIM_RUNE_ECON off, DOD_SIM_RUNE_POWER across ×1 / ×1.5 / ×2 / ×2.5 / ×3.
  • Stage 2 — the sweep is three dose levels, not five, and that is a property of the data. Runes.scale_payload rounds integer terms. 82 of the pool's 107 scalable benefit terms are integers and 52 of those are a bare 1 — which rounds to 2 at both ×1.5 and ×2, and to 3 at both ×2.5 and ×3. Between ×1.5 and ×2, 62 of 82 integer terms are frozen and 23 of 65 entries are byte-identical. So the five swept points are really ×1, {×1.5, ×2}, {×2.5, ×3}, and the near-equality measured inside each pair is the expected result rather than a surprise.
    The corollary is worth more than the caveat: between ×1.5 and ×2 the floats grow by a third while the integers hold, and depth does not move; between ×2 and ×2.5 the integers step 2→3 and depth does. The pool's power lives in its talent-counter grants, not in its stat percentages.
  • Stage 2 — the rows. Fixed party, n=200 per row, route=default, map=new, diff=standard, start=on. Primary metric is depth reached; the resolvable difference for each pair is the report's own 80%-power figure.
    ×1 15.12 | ×1.5 17.71 | ×2 16.86 | ×2.5 18.98 | ×3 18.98 (SD ≈ 9.4)
    Against the ×1 baseline: ×1.5 +2.59 (resolvable 2.64 — misses by five hundredths of a tier), ×2 +1.74 (2.63), ×2.5 +3.86 (2.74 — clears), ×3 +3.86 (2.64 — clears). Completions ran 4% / 12% / 9% / 14% / 16% with a band of ±5.5 points. ×1.5 is an underpowered result and is recorded as one, not as "noise" — pooling the {×1.5, ×2} level to n=400 still lands at +2.17 against a resolvable 2.25.
  • Stage 2 — and then the replication did not. The rotated arm exists because a fixed party can only ever roll 16 of the 48 spec-scoped runes, and AD's finding only earned its confidence because it reproduced across both. At n=100 it read ×1 15.48 / ×1.5 17.48 / ×2 15.84 / ×2.5 15.79 / ×3 15.65 — an ordering with no dose-response in it at all. That was not treated as a refutation, because at n=100 the rotated arm's resolvable difference is ±4.28 tiers, larger than the +3.86 it was being asked to replicate: it could not have seen the fixed-party result even if that result were perfectly real. Reading it as "no effect" would have been precisely the AA/AB failure Batch AD diagnosed.
    So the two decisive rotated rows were re-run at n=300 each (2×150, pooled), bringing the resolvable difference to 2.52 — comfortably smaller than the effect it was hunting.
    rotated ×1 15.21 (SD 10.64, n=300) → ×2.5 17.15 (SD 11.40, n=300) = +1.94, resolvable 2.52 — does NOT clear.
    The instrument could have seen it. It did not. That is a failure to replicate rather than an underpowered null, and the distinction is the entire reason the rows were re-run.
  • Stage 2 — the decision rule, applied as committed. The rule was written down before any row was readable: the smallest multiplier at or below ×2.5 that clears the resolvable difference gets authored; if nothing at or below ×2.5 clears, stop and author nothing, because the finding is then that one starting rune is not enough acquisition at any sane magnitude and the designer needs a second lever before a magnitude pass means anything. ×2.5 cleared on one arm and not on the other, so nothing cleared on the evidence as a whole. The batch stopped. data/runes.json is byte-unchanged.
  • Reported, and deliberately not acted on. Both arms are individually significant at the conventional five per cent level (t = 3.94 fixed, t = 2.15 rotated); they are not statistically distinguishable from each other (Q = 2.08 on 1 df); and an inverse-variance combination of the two gives +2.82 tiers, SE 0.663, which would clear. All of that is true. It is also an analysis chosen after seeing the rows — the project's "resolvable difference" is an 80%-power design threshold, deliberately stricter than a significance test, and it is the threshold this batch's rule named in advance. Relaxing it afterwards is the exact flexibility that made AA's and AB's verdicts unfalsifiable. The designer now has both numbers and can overrule the threshold deliberately; that call is theirs and it is not this batch's.
    Noise calibration worth keeping: the two n=150 halves of the same rotated ×2.5 arm measured 16.39 and 17.91 — 1.52 tiers apart with nothing whatsoever changed.
  • Stage 3 — not run, and what it would have been. The pass is fully worked out and recorded rather than applied, so the next batch does not repeat the analysis: benefits and costs scaled in step (AD's arm scaled upside only and held costs, which would have buffed every scarred rune twice), 59 of 65 entries moving, and all 59 descriptions rewritten against their read sites rather than their payload values, because most rank grants state their effect and not their rank count. Three findings came out of preparing it and they outlive it:
    (a) Five rune fields are read as booleans — coated_blades, deep_focus, perfect_form, opening_volley and vulture are all if x > 0 gates in front of hard-coded constants. Scaling them is a silent no-op. This is a correction to Batch AD: its power arm scaled them like any other integer, so its ×3 / ×6 / ×10 rows never actually multiplied those terms. It also means the Rune of the Deep Sight carries nothing but boolean fields and cannot move under any magnitude pass at all, joining Comet, Binding Souls, Last Rites, Flayed Mind and the Quick Spring on the unscalable list — six entries, not five.
    (b) A proportional cost on healing_received_mult would have crossed a clamp. The field is a running sum onto a 1.0 base and unit.heal_amount floors it at zero. Vampiric (universal) plus the Occultist's Hollow Chalice sums to −1.50 at ×2.5, so the clamp would have turned two runes into "you cannot be healed at all" — a rule change wearing a magnitude change's clothes, with nothing crashing to announce it. Batch AA's guard comment says "no reachable loadout gets there today"; that was a claim about the data, and nothing had been checking it. Now something does.
    (c) Two effects are non-linear in their own field and cannot take a proportional scale: on_edge_ranks is a threshold with a base (0.20 + 0.05 × ranks, so 1→3 ranks moves it 25%→35%, not 75%) and mindfulness_ranks is an interval (counter >= 7 − ranks, where the talent's own 3 ranks plus a rune's 3 would make every Arcanist cooldown tick double every turn).
  • Stage 4 — what the acquisition change actually did. Control is start=off at the batch's own HEAD, measured before stage 3 was considered, so "control" means the game as it stood and not "the new numbers minus the starting rune". Matched n, matched flags.
    The lever landed exactly as designed, and this half is near-deterministic: runes acquired per hero per run 0.56 → 1.47; slots filled per hero 0.56 → 1.46 (20% → 55%); and the number the whole rune arc has been about — authored SPEC runes worn per hero — 0.16 → 0.49, a tripling. A hero now visibly wears an authored spec rune in roughly half of runs instead of one in six.
    The difficulty half did not move. Fixed party n=200: depth 16.07 → 15.12 (−0.95, resolvable 2.54), completions 3% → 4%. Rotated n=300: depth 14.89 → 15.21 (+0.32, resolvable 2.39), completions 10% → 16% against a band of ±7.7 points. Opposite signs, both far inside the noise. The per-tier power ratio curve is unchanged in shape (z1t8 0.97→0.95, z1t10 0.88→0.86, z1 boss 0.74→0.75) and the wipe distribution keeps its cluster.
    This independently reproduces Batch AD's central claim at a different acquisition magnitude. AD measured its rich arm — acquisition raised, power untouched — at −0.85 tiers. AE raised acquisition by a smaller, shippable amount and measured −0.95. Acquisition alone still moves nothing, which is exactly why the magnitude half mattered and exactly why its failure to replicate leaves the product where AD found it.
  • Stage 4 — the sentence the designer asked for, unhedged. The game did not get measurably easier: depth reached moved −0.95 tiers on the fixed party and +0.32 on the rotated one, both well inside the ±2.5 tiers this instrument can resolve, so the starting rune tripled how much authored rune content a hero actually wears and bought no detectable difficulty change. Nothing was nerfed to offset it, because there was nothing to offset.
  • Stage 4 — the twelve-spec contribution table, rotated, at per-spec n≈100. AD flagged Pyromancer 35→42% and Sharpshooter 25→19% as the only moves over 5 points under its combined arm, at per-spec n≈33. Neither reproduces here. Pyromancer 36→34, Sharpshooter 25→26, and no spec moves more than 3 points: Survivalist 30→32, Holy 28→27, Warden 26→26, Cryomancer 27→26, Swordmaster 22→24, Arcanist 24→21, Beastmaster 19→20, Occultist 19→20, Berserker 18→18, Devout 14→14. Consistent with AD's own note that those two moves appeared only under the combined arm.
  • Verified. test_runes.gd 3,085 → 3,248 checks, 0 failures — extended, not replaced, with three new alarms that exist because of what stage 3's preparation turned up: every boolean-read field is asserted to still be a boolean gate in battle.gd (so the day one becomes arithmetic, the test says so and the pool may start using it) and to carry no rune value above 1; the reachable healing_received_mult sum is asserted to leave the multiplier above the clamp; and the opening triple is asserted to contain a spec-scoped rune within a wide 20–70% band, so a future spec batch or a RARITY_WEIGHTS change reports its effect on the opening pick. That test independently measured the rate at 42%, matching the sim's 36–42%.
    New: test_start_rune.gd 131 checks (dealt exactly once per hero; survives a save/load round-trip without rerolling; never dealt twice on a resumed run nor after the debug spec swap; a marker-less pre-AE save loads clean and is not retro-fitted; the off flag works with sim_run false; DOD_SIM_RUNES=off leaves no owed pick pointing at an empty queue; the sim's policy resolves it and resolving twice is a no-op; a sim_run party never reaches user://run_save.bin) and test_start_rune_ui.gd 31 checks against the real scenes (the map badge, its colour, that it is pulsing, the hero-list rows, the picker offering the three saved candidates, spending one clearing panel and badge together, the control showing no badge and no nudge, and the nudge waiting behind the framing card and arriving after it). test_rune_battle.gd 91 checks, 0 failures — including the known-flaky White Flame check, which passed this run. test_run_harness.gd gates 1/2/3 all PASS, rerun before any --run row in this batch was trusted. Purity: profile.json and relics.json byte-identical across a real DOD_SIM_RUN with the AE start rune on and both AD arms armed, and run_save.bin never created. Headless parse: all ten scenes, 0 SCRIPT ERROR.
  • Report back, not decided.
    (a) Should the opening pick be spec-weighted? Yes, on the numbers. The ordinary roller puts a spec-scoped rune among the three only 36–42% of the time, and the sim's policy — which prefers a spec candidate — took one at exactly that rate, i.e. it takes one whenever one is there. The ceiling is the roller's hit rate, not the policy's. This is why spec-worn landed at 0.49 rather than the 1.18 the brief projected, and a spec-weighted opening is a one-line change that would roughly double it.
    (b) Six entries now cannot move under any magnitude pass, not five: Comet, Binding Souls, Last Rites and Flayed Mind (ability grants), the Quick Spring (inverted cost/cooldown), and the Rune of the Deep Sight, whose only two fields are boolean-read. If a magnitude pass ever lands they are all relatively weaker by the whole multiplier, and the Deep Sight is the one nobody had noticed.
    (c) Whether difficulty needs its own batch: on this evidence, no — this batch spent difficulty budget and the meter did not move. Wanderer (×0.7) is untouched and still earning its place, since Standard has not moved.
    (d) The treasure/Loot node type remains outstanding since Batch AC — five batches now.
    (e) The magnitude pass is prepared and recorded, not applied: scaling with costs in step, four hand-held costs with their reasons, and 59 rewritten descriptions. If the designer adds the second acquisition lever (a rune offer at rest nodes, touched 2.3×/run against the shop's 0.6, or a second elite cache), the sweep should be re-run at that acquisition level — for the same reason this batch could not reuse AD's.

2026-08-04 — Batch AD: the rune economy — power and dilution are one finding

  • Why. Three batches authored the rune pool — X built the machinery and the Warrior's set, AA did Mage and Cleric, AB did the Hunter — and twice, independently, the measurement came back the same: the authored pool at current power does not move completions beyond noise. A whole system, the shop's entire stock and the elite cache's entire payoff, measured as inert. Two explanations had been named and never separated: the entries are too weak, or they never arrive (acquisition measured at 0.55–0.7 runes per hero per run, out of the four written for that spec). Every prior measurement confounded them, and that mattered practically: raise power while dilution is the real cause and the entries are over-tuned the moment acquisition is fixed — an error a human playtester would find instead of us. This batch separates them. It is measure-first in the Batch U/W/Y mould and it ships no content change, because that is what the data says to do.
  • Stage 0b first, because the instrument was the problem. Before any arm ran, the report was made to state what it can actually resolve. Completions is close to useless as a primary metric at the sample sizes this project has been using. Recent rows sit at 2–12% on n=50 — one to six runs — and the resolvable difference between two 50-run rows, at 80% power, is about 15 percentage points. Batches AA and AB read 4%→6%, 6%→2% and 8%→13% as "noise". They were noise; but that instrument would have called a genuine ten-point improvement noise too. Two batches concluded from a measurement that could not have seen the effect either way.
    The proof arrived by accident. The same commit (7ec7f97), the same flags, measured three times, returned 4% (Batch AC), 10%, and 8% completions. Six points of spread with nothing changed at all.
  • What replaced it. Depth reached — the absolute tier a run ends on, 1–33, as a mean over per-run samples — is now the PRIMARY metric. Every run contributes one sample, so it carries far more information per run than a binary win, and unlike the wipe median it is not a knife-edge statistic. The run report now prints an INSTRUMENT RESOLUTION block above the numbers it qualifies, giving mean/SD/SE for each metric and the smallest difference two rows could resolve at n=50/100/150. Completions and the wipe median are demoted to SECONDARY and always quoted with their band.
    At n=100, measured: depth resolves ±3.9 tiers, ratio@z1t8 ±0.09, completions ±10.7 points. All rows in this batch were run at n=100 for that reason.
  • A metric caveat found by running the arms, worth more than the metric. ratio@z1t8 is conditioned on survival — only runs that reach zone 1 tier 8 contribute one. Across this batch's rows that sample was 49, 59, 62 and 76 runs of 100, because the arms changed how many runs get there. An intervention that helps weak runs survive to t8 lowers the mean ratio at t8 by adding weak runs to the sample. Depth reached has no such bias. Read ratio@z1t8 with its n attached, and never as a lone primary.
  • Stage 0a — the rune economy is now visible. Nothing about it had ever been in a run report. The new block gives, per run: runes offered/bought/equipped split by source (shop vs elite cache); shop offers refused and why (no free slot / unaffordable / duplicate); elite runes won with no slot to wear them in; slots available vs filled at run end; and what each hero is actually wearing, split spec / class / universal / stat stick. The header states the known confounder rather than burying it in a comment: since Batch Y the bot's fight-first preference binds, it takes 0.6 of 5.4 shops offered, and sim rune acquisition is therefore a floor on the bot's routing, not an estimate of a human's. No route policy in the harness ranks a shop above a fight (greedy, default, cautious and elites all put combat first), so the acquisition ceiling is not reachable by changing route. That gap is reported rather than papered over with a policy invented mid-batch — the rich arm is what supplies the ceiling instead.
  • Stage 1 — two flags, one variable each. Both are sim-only, off by default and never shipped.
    • DOD_SIM_RUNE_ECON=rich — acquisition raised, authored content untouched: every slot open from tier 1, plus a spec-eligible authored rune granted at each zone half-mark, so the four runes written for a spec reach their hero by the middle of zone 2 instead of never. Crude on purpose; it is a probe, not an economy.
    • DOD_SIM_RUNE_POWER=<mult> — a blanket multiplier on authored payload magnitudes, acquisition untouched. It scales the upside only and holds every cost at its authored value, deliberately: a multiplier that grew the drawbacks in step would answer a different question. Generated stat sticks are not authored content and do not move. Named exclusions, stated rather than hidden: grant_ability/new_ability have no magnitude (four entries — Comet, Binding Souls, Last Rites, Flayed Mind — are identical in every arm, and the power arm cannot speak for them); set fields are absolute assignments; and ability cost/cooldown reductions are inverted fields, where scaling −5 Rage to −15 would measure "what if abilities were free" (one rune, the Quick Spring, is wholly unscaled as a result).
  • The purity gate is the load-bearing half. Every other harness flag reads the environment unconditionally, so a stale export in a shell changes a real game. These two are gated on sim_run as well as on the variable, so a player's run can never be silently in an experiment arm. Asserted, not intended: the test sets both variables, leaves sim_run false, and checks that nothing happens — then sets sim_run and checks that the arms actually arm, because a gate that is always closed proves nothing. Junk values (banana, -2, 0) fall back to ×1.0 rather than zeroing every payload in the pool.
  • The interpretation rule, committed in writing BEFORE the first arm ran. Deciding what counts as an answer after seeing the rows is how a measure-first batch turns into a rationalisation, so the rule was written down first: "moves the needle" means a difference larger than the resolvable effect size the row itself prints. rich moves it alone → dilution, stop and bring levers to the designer. power moves it alone → the entries are underpowered, do a magnitude pass. Both move it → report the sizes, the designer picks. Neither at any reachable magnitude → runes are decorative, and say so plainly. And explicitly: "if neither single arm moves the metric but the pair does, the two are jointly necessary — report it as such rather than attributing the movement to either flag alone." That last clause is the one the data landed on.
  • The four rows — fixed party, n=100 each (route=default, map=new, diff=standard). Depth reached is the primary; the resolvable difference is ±3.9 tiers.
    rowacquired /herospec runes worn /heroDEPTH (primary) vs controlcompletionswipe median
    control0.580.1816.97 ±0.99—8% ±5.3z1 t11.0
    rich2.932.5416.12 ±0.94−0.85 (no)6% ±4.7z1 t11.0
    power ×30.500.1416.49 ±0.95−0.48 (no)12% ±6.4z1 t11.0
    rich+power3.923.1527.13 ±0.92+10.16 MOVED61% ±9.6z2 t7.0
  • Replicated across all twelve specs. A fixed party can only ever roll 16 of the 48 spec-scoped runes, so it is structurally blind to two-thirds of the authored pool. The whole table was re-run at DOD_SIM_ROTATE=1, n=100 each, and reproduces: control depth 14.78 ±1.06 / 12% — rich 14.71 ±1.01 / 12% (a change of −0.07 tiers and exactly zero completion points while delivering 2.27 spec runes per hero against the control's 0.17) — power×3 14.94 ±1.06 / 15% — rich+power 22.83 ±1.20 / 43%. Two independent parties, same shape: neither alone, both together.
  • THE FINDING: the two hypotheses were never alternatives. They are two factors of one product, both near zero, and each perfectly masks the other. Raise acquisition alone — five times the runes, fourteen times the spec runes worn — and nothing happens, because at authored magnitudes four runes are worth about what one is worth: nothing. Raise power alone and nothing happens, because a multiplier on a rune nobody is wearing multiplies zero. Any experiment that varies one factor at a time is guaranteed to report "no effect" whichever explanation is true — which is exactly what Batch AA and Batch AB did, and they were not careless. The pool is not weak and it is not diluted. It is both, and either fix alone is worthless.
  • "At any reachable magnitude" — closed, not assumed. The power arm was pushed until it ran out of road, acquisition untouched:
    ×1 → depth 16.97 · ×3 → 16.49 · ×6 → 17.07 · ×10 → 17.93
    A tenfold increase in every authored magnitude in the game moves the primary metric by 0.96 tiers, against a resolvable difference of ±3.9. Power alone is not a live lever at any multiplier a designer would ever ship, and now that is measured rather than supposed.
  • And the dose-response once acquisition is fixed — the number the designer actually needs. With rich held on and the multiplier swept:
    ×1 → depth 16.12 · ×1.5 → 20.50 · ×2 → 23.75 · ×3 → 27.13
    Smooth, monotone, roughly +3.5 tiers per half-step. The effect becomes detectable at about ×1.5 and is unambiguous by ×2 — so if acquisition is fixed, the magnitude pass this project has been contemplating for three batches needs to be far smaller than ×3. The response is threshold-like rather than linear because the run economy compounds (Batch T: "the attrition→income loop unwinding IS the mechanism of every gain") — a small edge in zone 1 buys the gold and talent points that buy the next edge.
  • Stage 2 — the batch ends with NO CONTENT CHANGE, because that is what the data says. The brief allowed this and it is the correct outcome, not a shortfall. A runes.json magnitude pass is precisely the change measured to be inert on its own at ×3, ×6 and ×10. Shipping one now would also over-tune: the dose-response says ~×1.5–2 is enough once the runes arrive, so a pass authored against today's numbers would be roughly double what is wanted, and the error would surface in a human playtest rather than here — the exact failure the brief predicted. Acquisition is an economy decision and belongs to the designer. So: instrumentation and the finding ship; the levers are laid out below and not chosen.
  • The levers, with what the data says about each — designer's call, not this batch's. Measured at the control row: gold earned 582 / spent 151 / unspent 431 per run, slots 2.80 available per hero and 0.58 filled (21%), shops 0.6 taken of 5.4 offered, elite caches 0.82 per run, rests 2.3 taken of 5.6 offered.
    • Open the third slot earlier — DEAD LEVER, remove it from the list. The party already leaves 79% of the slots it owns empty. It cannot fill the ladder it has; lengthening the ladder changes nothing. (This is the brief's explicit ask, answered: slots are not the binding constraint.)
    • Rarity re-pricing (50/100/160) — cheap, safe, small. Two facts that look contradictory and are not: across a run price is not the constraint (the party dies holding 431 gold, enough for four rare runes), yet at the counter it binds (1.04 of 2.56 offers per run refused as unaffordable). Both are true because the party reaches a shop only 0.6 times per run, so most gold is earned after the last shop it will ever see. Re-pricing converts at most about one extra offer per run and leaves the pile untouched.
    • A rune offer at rest nodes — the largest sim-measurable lever. Rests are entered 2.3 times per run against the shop's 0.6 — nearly four times the touch rate — and the party has 431 unspent gold to convert. Cost: it dilutes what a rest node is.
    • A second elite cache — small. Elites are taken 0.8 per run and each cache dresses one hero, so doubling it adds roughly 0.2 runes per hero per run. It is also gated on surviving deep enough to meet elites.
    • A starting rune at the draft — the biggest single jump, and front-loaded. +1.00 per hero immediately, landing in zone 1 where the runs actually die (control wipe median: the zone-1 boss), and visible to a player in their first ten minutes.
    • Change nothing yet, and measure a human first. A real option, not a cop-out: the 0.6-of-5.4 shop rate is the bot's routing floor, and a human who deliberately routes through shops may already acquire several times what the sim does. Difficulty is closed pending playtesters; acquisition may deserve the same treatment.
  • A bug the arm's own positive control caught. The power multiplier decides what is a benefit and what is a cost by sign — but blood_pact runs backwards. The Rune of Exsanguination's -15 is its whole promise (enemy veins open at 85 buildup instead of 100), and read by sign alone it looked like a drawback and was held, leaving that rune inert in every arm. It surfaced because the test asserts that every entry not on a named UNSCALABLE list must actually respond to the arm — an assertion that exists precisely so a silently-unmoved entry is a failure rather than a shrug. Fixed with a named INVERTED_STAT_FIELDS list beside the existing PENALTY_FIELDS, and the rows that had already started were thrown away and re-run rather than caveated.
  • Verification.
    • Purity, asserted rather than intended: with both variables exported and sim_run false, rune_econ() stays normal, rune_power() stays 1.0, the slot ladder stays 2/3/4 and an authored payload is untouched — then the same env under sim_run arms both, because a gate that is always closed proves nothing.
    • The control IS the regression test. Pre-batch HEAD (7ec7f97) re-run from a clean git worktree at the same n=100 and the same flags: completions 6%, wipe median z1 t11.0, ratio@z1t8 1.00, choice 79%. This batch's control arm: completions 8%, wipe median z1 t11.0 (identical), ratio 0.92, choice 78%. Everything inside its band. Worth noting for its own sake: four measurements of the unchanged build span 0.92–1.00 on ratio and 4–10% on completions.
    • Save files byte-identical across a real DOD_SIM_RUN invocation with both arms armed — profile.json, run_save.bin and relics.json hashed before and after.
    • test_runes.gd extended, not replaced — 1,961 checks → 3,085, zero failures. New: the purity gate; a drift alarm asserting the shipped cost lists match the scarred audit's; every entry's cost held and every benefit scaled at ×3 with sign and int/float type preserved; ×1.0 proven to be the identity (a control row that was not a control would void the batch); and the whole scaled pool re-run through the existing schema and scarred checks, because stage 2 could have raised magnitudes for real.
    • test_rune_battle.gd — 84 → 91 checks. A dictionary being right is not the same as a payload reaching a hero, and the live-value gotcha has bitten twice. A Berserker is spawned three times (no runes / ×1 / ×3) and the three shapes are read straight off the unit as deltas: blood_pact −15 → −45 (the inverted benefit, live), crit_bonus +0.08 → +0.24 (ordinary benefit), dmg_taken_bonus +0.15 → +0.15 (a cost, held).
    • Headless parse: all ten scenes, --quit-after 90, zero SCRIPT ERROR.
  • Reported, not acted on.
    • The Pyromancer and the Sharpshooter are the only specs that move more than 5 points in the twelve-spec contribution table, and only under the combined arm — Pyromancer 35%→42%, Sharpshooter 25%→19%. The Pyromancer's direction is exactly the AoE-scales-with-field-size signature Batch W measured and deliberately left alone; deeper runs mean bigger fields. Per-spec sampling is only ~33 runs of 100 under rotation, so the band is wide. A balance finding, reported.
    • The Rune of Exsanguination's "cost" is not in its payload at all. Its scarred audit passes on the -15 blood_pact term — which this batch established is its benefit. The actual cost (the bleedout tears 15% of max HP instead of 20%) is a constant in battle.gd that no audit can see. The check passes for the wrong reason; whether that matters is an authoring call.
    • A pre-existing flaky assertion in test_rune_battle.gd: the White Flame check needs the bot to actually land a fire hit during its battle and fails roughly one run in three. Untouched by this batch, and the same fact is covered by a spawn-time assertion, but a future batch should not read it as a regression.
    • The treasure/Loot node type is still outstanding from Batch AC — delete it or deal it again. Still not called.

2026-08-04 — Batch AC: the tester's workbench — reach any node, on demand

  • Why. From the designer: "I need some way to access any node on the map for testing — often I want to test the shop or the ???, but I have to fight through the other nodes to get to them." The DEBUG section only had tools that move the party forward past everything (Jump to Boss Tier, Advance to Next Zone). A shop was guaranteed only in tiers 7–10, so testing shop behaviour cost six-plus fights; a specific event could not be tested at all, because events are drawn at the door, filtered by requirement, and never repeat within a run — three visits and the zone has none left to give. This batch is debug/testing affordances only: no balance change, no content, no difficulty. Difficulty stays closed pending human playtesters.
  • Stage 0 — what already existed and was left alone. The map burger already had a DEBUG section on ids 10–16 (+200 Gold, +200 Talent Points, Full Heal Party, Jump to Boss Tier, Advance to Next Zone, Reroll Specs, and the "All Spec Abilities Unlocked" check item), built behind Run.debug_enabled() and dispatched in _on_burger; the battle scene already had its DEBUG ▾ menu (Full Restore, Cooldowns OFF, Enemy attacks OFF, per-hero turn lock). All of it is untouched and none of it was re-implemented. New map ids start at 20 so nothing collides with 10–16, and the event picker's per-event ids start at 100. No "Summon Boss" was added — Jump to Boss Tier already goes there — and Loot is listed nowhere: the treasure node type is dead code no generator has dealt since the Batch 38 deck, and its existing click handler was left exactly as Batch Z recorded it.
  • The honesty flag ships in the same batch as the capability. Run.debug_used, saved with the run (save v5, tolerant load — a v4 save loads with it false). Written in exactly two places, both at the top of a debug dispatch — the map burger's and the battle menu's — so every debug item, the pre-AC ones included, trips it without seven separate write sites. Check items trip it when toggled on; unchecking never clears it, because a run that was debug-touched stays debug-touched. The run summary then opens with DEBUG TOOLS WERE USED IN THIS RUN — not a clean data point., in the panel and in the Copy summary clipboard text, which is the half that actually reaches feedback.
  • Free Travel — a check item, session-scoped, never saved, default OFF. While it is on, every node on the board is clickable: every tier, ignoring links and ignoring the party's position, backwards as well as forwards. Clicking one advances the run honestly — it goes through the existing _on_node_pressed path with no parallel branch, so floor_idx/node_idx/visited are all true afterwards, the tier readout reads the real tier, the save is a real save, and a free-travelled fight is scaled by the tier it actually sits on. It therefore does book its tally and its Profile entry, which is correct: the party really is there and really did the thing. debug_used is what marks the run.
  • Legibility of free travel, which matters more than it sounds: a tester must never confuse a debug-reachable node with a genuinely reachable one, or they will report their own toggle as a bug. Debug-only-reachable nodes are a fourth read, not a third with an ambiguity — the node's own colour pulled toward grey and dimmed, inside a magenta outline no node type or state uses — with a [DEBUG] tooltip prefix and a DEBUG: FREE TRAVEL marker in the corner while the toggle is on. Judgement call made and worth stating: a visited node keeps its dim-green history fill and gains the outline, rather than being repainted, so "where I have been" survives the toggle.
  • Test a node ▸ — a DEBUG submenu with Shop, Rest, ??? Event, Fight, Elite, entered in place. A summoned node does not move the party, does not touch floor_idx/node_idx, and does not mark anything visited; the board is exactly as it was when the tester returns. Everything else runs for real: a summoned shop is a real shop screen with real offers and real gold, a summoned rest heals for real, a summoned fight is a real battle at the current tier's scaling with real rewards.
  • "Debug visits don't count" is one guard, not four. A single session-scoped Run.debug_summon is set for the duration of a summoned node and cleared when it resolves (in map_screen._ready, the return point of every summon that leaves the map; the rest resolves in place and clears it itself). It is checked at exactly three sites: Run.tally_add and Run.tally_damage — which between them cover battles, gold earned and spent, rests, elites and per-hero damage — and the battle scene's wipe branch, which turned out to be the only Profile booking a summoned node can reach at all (no boss is summonable, and the event picker never calls Profile.note_event or appends to Run.seen_events in the first place). Judgement call, stated rather than buried: dying in a summoned fight really does end the run, but it does not book a wipe — "cycles lost to the Decay" should not count a fight conjured out of a menu.
  • The summoned warband uses the generator, not a copy of it. Run.compose(node_type) is the same call _generate_map makes to roll each node's warband, and it already defaults to the party's current tier — so no extraction or refactor was needed and there is still exactly one warband generator in the project. An elite summons through the elite path, keeping the budget floor of 6 that makes an elite an elite.
  • The event picker — the highest-value item in the batch, because today a specific event cannot be tested at all. "??? Event" does not call Events.pick(): it lists every event in data/events.json, alphabetically by title, with its id, and states per row whether its requirements currently pass or fail — and on failure, which condition failed and by how much ("Needs 120 gold (party has 40)"). Requirement-failing events are still selectable, because testing the greyed-out branches is the point; the event runs through the normal event screen so its own guards behave exactly as they would in play. Already-seen events are repeatable, and picking one does not append to Run.seen_events and does not call Profile.note_event.
  • One implementation of the requirement check, reused rather than copied.
