The event screen's private greyed-out-choice explainer became Events.failed_reason(run, req) — returns "" when met, otherwise a sentence naming the first failing condition — and Events.requires_met is now that function read as a boolean. The picker's pass/fail column, the event screen's tooltip and the gate that filters the real draw are therefore the same code and can never disagree. The old explainer only knew two of the six conditions and said "The party cannot do this" for the rest; all six now speak.
  • Not in this batch, and deliberately: no fight/warband picker (choosing theme and budget from the UI touches the encounter generator); no resurrection of the Loot node type; no Summon Boss; and none of it exists in exported builds — everything sits behind the existing Run.debug_enabled() gate.
  • Hardening that came free. The debug block is now gated twice: the section is not built unless debug_enabled(), and firing a debug id straight into the dispatch is refused as well (previously the pre-AC ids 10–16 were protected only by not being drawn). The battle menu gained the matching _debug_allowed() — debug_enabled() and not sim_run and not sim and not autoplay, mirroring _forfeit_allowed() — checked when the menu is built and again when an item fires. DOD_DEBUG=0 now forces the gate shut in a dev build, which is the only way a headless test can stand where an exported build stands and prove the items are unreachable.
  • The all-events smoke test — real coverage the project has never had. The events vocabulary is data-driven (12 verbs aimed by 4 target selectors, gated by 6 requirement conditions), so a typo in a JSON edit was silent until a tester met it. Now that the picker can address any event by id, test_events_all.gd walks the lot: 16 events × 32 choices, 142 dispatch applications, 2,613 checks, 0 failures. Every choice is applied twice — once against a healthy party, once against a stressed one (a hero down, no gold, no items) — and the vocabulary itself is asserted: no unknown verb, no unknown target selector, no unknown requirement key, no unreachable item/spec/class id, and the invariants the verbs promise (gold never negative, max HP never under 10, and events never kill). No event failed.
  • Verified (scratchpad, dies with the session): test_debug_access.gd 378 checks, 0 failures — the surface itself (ids, submenu contents, no Loot, no Boss, and the submenu parent's id); the picker listing every event with a pass/fail row, sorted by title, every one of them selectable including the failing ones, refusing to stack a second overlay, and arming an event without burning the draw pool or writing the Profile; every debug id unreachable with the gate shut and fired directly; the same under each of sim / autoplay / sim_run on both menus; the honesty flag set by every item, surviving save v5, loading false from a v4 save, and reaching both the summary panel and the clipboard text; a summoned rest, shop and fight leaving the ledger and the Profile untouched and the same three via a real node path moving both (a guard that is always on proves nothing); a summoned node leaving the board untouched; free travel to an arbitrary (f, i) forwards and backwards; and the profile and run-save bytes identical across a sim_run pass. Headless parse: all 10 scenes, 0 SCRIPT ERROR.
  • Sim purity, proven rather than asserted. This batch touches exactly one thing a sim reads — Events.requires_met, which RunSim calls when it resolves an event node — so a 50-run row was measured on this build against the same flags at HEAD, not assumed. Pre-batch (dc7ce03): route=default map=new diff=standard completions=2% wipe median z1 t11.0 ratio@z1t8=0.92 choice=79%. This build: completions=4% wipe median z1 t11.0 ratio@z1t8=0.98 choice=77%. The wipe median is identical, and the rest is a one-to-two-run spread at n=50 — the noise band Batches X, AA and AB each measured independently. The row reproduces. Separately, profile.json, run_save.bin and relics.json were hashed before and after a real DOD_SIM_RUN invocation and are byte-identical.
  • Reported, not acted on (all three are the designer's call):
    1. The treasure/Loot dead node type is now the only node type a debug menu cannot reach, which makes it slightly more conspicuous. Whether to delete it or deal it again is a decision this batch does not make.
    2. A summoned fight is excluded from the run ledger but still increments Run.combat_wins, which is real progression (+2% Attack and HP per win) and was left real deliberately, since a summoned fight is supposed to run for real. The visible consequence is cosmetic: in a debug-touched run the summary's "Battles won: X of Y" can read X greater than Y. Sims cannot see any of it — they never reach a summoned node.
    3. gold_pct is an authored effect verb that no event in the game uses. The sweep now fires it directly so it cannot rot unnoticed, but a verb with no data driving it is either a gap in the content or a line to delete.
  • Gotchas worth recording. (1) PopupMenu.add_submenu_node_item auto-assigns the submenu parent's id from its item index — which landed on 13, i.e. "Jump to Boss Tier". The parent now carries an explicit id (9, deliberately below the debug block, because opening a submenu is not yet using a debug tool). (2) Whether Godot also delivers a submenu press to the parent popup could not be determined headlessly: PopupMenu.activate_item hangs without a display. Both popups feed the one dispatch, and a doubled "Test a node ▸ Rest" would heal the party twice, so the dispatch drops a repeat of the same id inside one frame — correct whichever way the engine behaves, and no human clicks twice in a frame. Tested both ways round: two presses in one frame rest once, and the guard does not leak into the next frame. (3) An autoplay battle paces itself on real timers (only sim mode skips them) and takes ~40 wall-clock seconds / ~5,700 frames, so a frame budget does not bound it; the headless tests set Engine.time_scale, which scales the timers the battle waits on and nothing else — the same battle in ~1.5s with identical combat decisions. (4) macOS has no timeout binary, so a timeout … | grep -c "SCRIPT ERROR" parse check reports a clean zero for every scene while running nothing at all. Use --quit-after and no wrapper.

2026-08-03 — Batch AB: Hunter rune sets, a Shieldwall rework, two corrections

  • Correction — Resurrection costs 1 Mercy. The Holy tree's Resurrection node text said "spend 3 Mercy"; the ability has always cost 1. Batch AA flagged it rather than picking a side because it was a design call. The call: 1 is correct, the node text was wrong. Fixed. The ability description, the Last Rites rune, the glossary's Mercy and Empower entries and the Master Document all already said 1 — the node was the only liar.
  • Correction — the Shieldwall v1 fossil is deleted. There have been two Shieldwalls. Version 1 was a party-wide percentage ward ("−25% damage, 2 turns"); Batch G replaced it with the charge-based version, and Batch Z found the raw *= 0.75 branch still sitting in the damage path. It was v1's corpse, not a mistuned live ability. Gone: the damage-path branch, the status's percentage blurb, the vaulted "shieldwall" special that applied it, its entry in the self-cast list, and the Master Document's "Shieldwall −25%" target modifier. Nothing live applied it — the two remaining callers were vaulted match branches (the v1 ability itself and the vaulted Sanctuary) that no ability, enemy or rune references, which is why this is hygiene and not a change. The glossary was checked and never carried the fossil: its drift alarm covers DEBUFF_IDS, and Shieldwall is a buff.
  • Shieldwall is now a Block STANCE. Shieldwall and Interpose were the same verb pointed in two directions — both granted charges, both fully negated attacks, one at the Warden and one at everyone else — and the self-directed half was the stronger. Interpose keeps the guaranteed charges. Shieldwall stops granting them and raises his Block chance instead: +25% for 2 turns (Perfect: 3). 25 Rage, 2.0 initiative, 2cd, self-cast — unchanged. A status chip reads "+25% Block" and his Heavy Plating passive chip folds the bonus into its live total, so the readout never lies while the stance holds.
  • The trade, which is the point: the bonus rides the Heavy Plating slice of the block roll, so every block Shieldwall buys is a Heavy Plating block — and Tenacity and Rally feed on Heavy Plating blocks specifically, which Shieldwall's charges never triggered. He trades hard denial for an engine. It is stated in the ability description, because a trade a player cannot see is not a trade.
  • Shield Mastery re-specced in place (wd_shieldwall, same id, so saved ranks migrate and nobody is refunded): "+1 Shieldwall charge per rank" became "+1 turn of stance per rank", 2 ranks.
  • Block-source labels follow the charges. Interpose is now the only source of guaranteed blocks, so a charge block logs "(Interpose)", not "(Shieldwall)", and the charge chip reads "IP3" instead of "SW3". The log stays honest about what produced a block, which matters because two talents key off that distinction.
  • Prevented-damage instrumentation: Shieldwall is no longer credited as its own mitigation site — the credit now belongs to the block roll, which was already instrumented and credits the blocker. Counted once, and not lost.
  • Twelve Hunter spec runes (data/runes.json, 53 → 65 entries), four each for the Beastmaster, Sharpshooter and Survivalist, under the Batch X/AA rules: one rune per talent lane read out of LANE_TREES plus one splash, exactly one scarred per set and it sits on a lane, one payload branch per entry. This completes spec coverage: all twelve specs now have a set.
    Beastmaster — Deep Bond (Loyalty ceiling +1, +1.5% companion damage per stack), Turning Pack (swap cooldown −1, +8% per different beast fielded), Scarred Loosened Straps (Quick Shot +12% of Attack, −8% armor), Shared Wild (splash).
    Sharpshooter — Deep Sight (Focus cap 150, crits grant +20 Focus), Narrow Gap (+8% armor ignored, +12% vs Broken), Scarred Long Draw (opens on 60 Focus, +10 Focus per attack, −10 Speed), Level Aim (splash).
    Survivalist — Weeping Wound (basic attacks Poison, +2 damage per stack), Quick Spring (Snare Trap −5 Mana, −1 cooldown), Scarred Carrion Wake (+30% vs 3+ afflictions, 16% max Mana per death, −12% max health), Long Hunt (splash).
  • Zero new cfg fields — the budget was four and Batch AA spent one for twenty-four runes; this batch spends none. Every entry rides a talent counter that already has a read site.
  • The Hunter-specific traps, handled: the runes write wild_communion_ranks, never the Devout's communion_ranks; companions inherit the hunter's armor at summon and per-beast terms double under The Pack, so the Beastmaster's scarred rune puts its cost on armor and says so in its own text ("every beast he calls wears his plate") and its upside on Quick Shot; no Hunter rune carries a healing term, because Ancient Pact's no_heals would make one a dead rune; the Survivalist's runes write enemy-side (Poison) effects, since his meter lives on the enemy. Two Sharpshooter runes deliberately write Focus ceilings — the first runes to exercise Batch AA's call-site ordering fix for real.
  • THE ARTEFACT WARNING IS RETIRED. Batch AA's standing caveat — "any share/contribution shift disfavouring Beastmaster/Sharpshooter/Survivalist is pool depth, not balance" — expires with this batch and must not be carried forward. All twelve specs now have four spec runes each, so the twelve-spec contribution table is internally comparable for the first time since Batch X. AA's reading of the Sharpshooter (25 → 20 contribution) expires with it.
  • Measured — Shieldwall, its own row, before stage 3 existed (standalone sim, Batch G's Warden party: warden/cryomancer/inquisitor/beastmaster, 200 battles).
    Fixed party: deaths/battle 0.02 → 0.03, survivors' avg HP 86% → 84%, enemy damage dealt/battle 126 → 148, Warden damage prevented 65 → 47/battle, Warden contribution 23% → 21%, damage share flat at 17%.
    Rotated (DOD_SIM_ROTATE=1, 200 battles): deaths/battle 0.21 → 0.20, Warden prevented 70 → 49, contribution 20% → 19%.
    Read honestly: prevented falls by about a third, which is exactly what trading three guaranteed blocks for a 25% chance should do, and deaths/battle does not move. What these rows CANNOT show is the other half of the trade — a standalone sim spends no talent points, so Tenacity and Rally are absent from the very measurement used to price an ability whose whole purpose is now to feed them. That half is measured directly in test_shieldwall.gd instead.
    Not a stalemate fix and not recorded as one. Reducing the Warden's self-denial makes the "cannot die, cannot kill" state marginally harder to reach; that is a side effect. The balance question stays deferred and open.
  • Measured — the rune rows (--run, route=default, map=new, diff=standard).
    Control first, runes=off, n=50: completions 6%, ratio@z1t8 1.00, choice 78% — reproducing Batch AA's off row (6% / 0.99 / 78%) exactly.
    Fixed party, n=50: off 6% → full 2% (ratio 1.00 → 0.91).
    Rotated pair, n=60 each, its own control: off 8% → full 13% (ratio 0.94 → 1.00).
    Read honestly: the order FLIPPED between the two pairs inside this one batch — the fixed pair says runes cost 2 completions, the rotated pair says they gain 3, and the whole spread is a handful of runs at n=50–60. That is Batch AA's finding reproduced without needing two batches to see it, and it says the same thing: the authored pool at its current power does not move completions beyond noise. The open lever is still authored rune POWER — a runes.json data edit, not machinery.
  • A sharper calibration of the noise, offered because it was free. The default sim party is berserker/cryomancer/inquisitor/beastmaster — no Warden — and the control row runs with runes off, so that row is a genuine null-change control: nothing in this batch can reach it. Its wipe median still read z2 t5 against AA's z1 t11. The distributions are near-identical (23 vs 25 zone-1 wipes out of 47); the median simply sits on the z1/z2 boundary, so a two-run difference flips which zone it reports. The wipe median is a knife-edge statistic at this sample size and should not be quoted as a trend on its own.
  • The twelve-spec contribution table, internally comparable at last (rotated, off → full): Pyromancer 34 → 33, Survivalist 29 → 32, Holy 29 → 29, Warden 25 → 25, Sharpshooter 24 → 25, Arcanist 23 → 25, Cryomancer 27 → 24, Swordmaster 24 → 23, Beastmaster 20 → 21, Occultist 19 → 19, Berserker 18 → 19, Devout 14 → 15. The three Hunter specs all read slightly UP — the direction AA's artefact warning predicted would be down while their pool was thin. Everything here is within a couple of points, i.e. noise-sized; the Pyromancer remains the top contributor, as Batch W measured and deliberately left alone.
  • Rune economy, reported not changed (the dilution question stays open and is not this batch's job): the party acquires 2.2 runes per run at the fixed party (1.4 shop + 0.8 elite) and 2.8 rotated (1.6 + 1.2), across four heroes — roughly 0.55–0.7 runes per hero per run. The pool just grew by twelve entries while per-hero acquisition did not move, so a given Hunter still finishes a run carrying one or two of his four. Note the brief's "~2.4 shop runes/run" is a pre-Batch-Y figure: since Y the bot's fight-first preference binds and it takes only 0.7 of 5.1 shops offered.
  • Difficulty stays closed pending human playtesters — nothing here reopens it.
  • Verified: test_runes.gd 1961 checks (coverage now spans all twelve specs read out of Classes.SPEC_IDS, plus "exactly one scarred, on a lane" and a positive control proving the exclusive-pair alarm still watches fifteen named fields); test_shieldwall.gd 53 checks; test_rune_battle.gd 84 checks (three live Hunter battles, every rune of a spec equipped at once, the Beastmaster checked at TWO beasts); headless parse across all 10 scenes, 0 SCRIPT ERROR.
  • Measured — Tenacity, the number asked for. In a held-open Warden battle (Shield Mastery 2, Tenacity and Rally learned) his max HP went 200 → 240 over 9 blocks. More Heavy Plating blocks do mean faster Tenacity growth and its +5 max HP is still unbounded, but this is nowhere near the old runaway (~127,000 max HP, Batch W, fixed by cutting the Unkillable compounding link on 08-02).

2026-08-03 — Batch AA: Mage and Cleric rune sets, and a forfeit

  • Why: Batch X shipped the rune system with the Warrior as its pilot — universal runes, class runes for all four classes, and four spec runes each for the Berserker, Swordmaster and Warden. Nine of twelve specs still drew nothing but stat sticks and class runes, which means three quarters of the roster was still testing as the pre-Batch-X game. With Y (the map) and Z (the alpha shell) landed, this was the last content gap standing between the build and a fair playtest. This batch does Mage and Cleric; the Hunter follows in his own batch, because the companion machinery is the most idiosyncratic in the game and deserves a pass rather than being the tired third of three.
  • 24 spec runes (data/runes.json, 29 → 53 entries), four per spec: one per talent lane, read out of Talents.LANE_TREES rather than any written list, plus one splash that pays for reaching out of a lane. Exactly one scarred rune per spec, in its lane; splashes are always clean. Three of the twenty-four are Epic, the rest Rare.
    • Pyromancer — Cinder Trail (Kindling: Fireball spreads 1 turn of Burn, Flamewave burns 1 turn longer), Scarred White Flame (Inferno, Epic: enemy resistance to his damage is 30% thinner and Inferno Master gains +1% per burning enemy — for −12% max health), Blast Radius (Detonation: consumes 25% more remaining Burn, +3% crit), Long Burn (splash).
    • Cryomancer — Bitter Grip (Deep Freeze: Chilled slows 3% harder, Frozen enemies 2% likelier to be crit — party-wide), Honed Lance (Shatterpoint: Ice Lance −5 Mana, +5% of Attack), Scarred Killing Cold (Winter: +2% damage per Chilled stack and Chilled enemies miss 5% more, but heals he receives are 25% weaker), Long Winter (splash).
    • Arcanist — Resonant Core (Resonance: max Resonance +1, +2% damage per stack), Seventh Bolt (Overload: Arcane Barrage fires a seventh bolt), Scarred Unquiet Mind (Control: cooldowns tick an extra turn every sixth, and a fifth of Cannon's recoil is paid in Mana instead of blood — for 8 less Mana regenerated each turn), Wide Current (splash).
    • Holy — Triage Ward (Radiance: instant heals can crit, +3% healing, 5% of overhealing banked), Last Rites (Mercy, Epic: grants Resurrection), Scarred Sleepless Vigil (Sanctuary: passive party healing at both ends of her turn, for −10 Speed), Open Hand (splash — she opens each battle holding 1 Mercy).
    • Devout — Warded Robes (Bulwark: Divine Shield heals 4% of what it absorbs, +10% armor while it stands), Binding Oath (Faith: a release leaves 1 stack standing and heals 5% more), Scarred Burning Censer (Zeal: Consecrated Ground reflects 10% more and the reflection mends the party, for −12% max health), Standing Vow (splash).
    • Occultist — Deepening Ruin (Ruin: +1% damage taken per stack, 5 Break damage per turn to anything Ruined), Flayed Mind (Madness, Epic: grants Mind Flay), Scarred Hollow Chalice (Leech: the party drinks 5% more from Ruined targets and the Ruin lifesteal takes 3% more, but heals he receives are 30% weaker), Whispering Dark (splash).
  • The cost of the batch in machinery: ONE new cfg field (the cap was four). rune_resist_pierce (float, BattleUnit) — a target's positive resistance to the attacker's damage type is thinned by this fraction; weaknesses are untouched, so the rune can never shrink a vulnerability. Its only read site is battle.gd's resist block, immediately after Avatar of Flame's ignore-fire-resistance branch, and it logs "Rune: the flame bites through resistance". Everything else in the batch rides counters the talent trees already read — which is the point of the design, not a shortcut.
  • Fixed — a whole class of silent dud: the second-resource ceilings (the Arcanist's Resonance max, Holy's Mercy max and starting Mercy, the Sharpshooter's Focus cap and opening Focus) were derived from cfg before the rune-apply block ran. Any rune writing resonant_core_ranks, mercy_cap_bonus or zealous_mercy applied cleanly and did nothing. The three derivation blocks now sit immediately after runes apply. Talent behaviour is unchanged (talents already applied further up) and no shipped rune touched those fields, so this changes nothing that existed — but the very next Hunter batch would have hit it on the Sharpshooter's Focus flags. test_runes.gd now asserts the call-site order directly so it cannot silently move back.
  • One-line guard, no behaviour change: heal_amount now floors the healing multiplier at zero. healing_received_mult is a running sum of rune terms, and a negative one would quietly turn every heal into damage that bypasses death handling. No reachable loadout gets there — the worst case is −0.6 against a 1.0 or 1.15 base — but this batch adds two more negative sources, and "silently" is the failure mode the whole batch exists to close.
  • Rune roll call in the log: most spec runes ride an existing talent counter, so their effect surfaces on that talent's proc line rather than one of their own (the Batch X precedent — Grudges and The Standard behave the same way). One grep-stable Rune: <hero>: <rune name> line per equipped rune is now written when the battle opens, so every rune a hero carries is visible in a log a tester pastes back.
  • Forfeit Run — the alpha escape hatch (NOT a stalemate fix). The endless-battle state diagnosed on 08-02 remains a deferred, open balance question. What changed is that the guard which handles it is sims only: real play had no resolution mechanism at all, so at the measured ~0.9% of battles a tester doing five runs had roughly a one-in-three chance of landing in a fight that cannot end, with force-quitting the application as the only exit — which costs the run and comes back as "it froze" rather than as an opinion.
    • Forfeit Run in the battle burger, alongside Restart Run and Exit to Main Menu, behind a confirmation. It ends the run exactly as a wipe does, so there is nothing to exploit and no balance thought required: forfeiting is strictly worse than winning.
    • It routes into Batch Z's run summary, whose outcome line reads "Forfeited — Zone N …", never "Wiped", with its own title colour. The final battle's lineup is still reported (the fight the tester walked away from is the most useful line in the summary), and the summary text goes to the clipboard like any other.
    • A reason, chosen on the confirm dialog: this fight will not end / too hard / not enjoying it / something broke / just stopping. It lands in the summary and in the Copy-summary text — every forfeit becomes labelled feedback, which is most of the value of shipping the action during an alpha.
    • Its own Profile bucket (forfeits, plus Profile.note_forfeit / forfeits_total()) — not note_wipe. A forfeit is not a defeat, and folding them together corrupts the chronicle the moment testers start using the exit. The summary's chronicle line grows a "Cycles abandoned" figure, shown only once there are any.
    • Never reachable in sims or autoplay. The menu entry is not even built when sim, autoplay or Run.sim_run is armed, and the forfeit itself re-checks — same guard discipline Batch Z used on the orientation cards, and verified the same way (profile and save bytes identical before and after).
    • A nudge, not a force-end: in real play only, past the same turn count the sim guard uses (FORFEIT_NUDGE_TURNS = STALEMATE_TURNS), a one-line non-blocking hint appears saying the fight is not resolving and that Forfeit is in the menu. It does not end the battle, touch HP, or alter any outcome, it fires once, and it never fires in a sim — a tester who does not know the burger holds an exit is still trapped, which is the whole reason it exists.
  • Measured — control first, at route=default, n=50 per row, map=new, difficulty=standard:
    rune poolcompletionswipe medianratio @ z1 t8choice
    off (control)6%z1 t11.00.9978%
    stats8%z1 t10.00.9375%
    full4%z1 t11.01.0578%
    The control reproduces the current build (Batch X's off row was 4%, Batch Y's new-map row 4% at choice 80%) — nothing else in the batch was readable until it did. The whole off/stats/full spread is four points at n=50, which is two runs: the authored pool at current power still does not move completions beyond noise, exactly Batch X's finding, and the ordering of the three rows flipped between batches, which is itself evidence that the differences are noise and not signal. Damage shares are flat (Cryomancer 39% off → 38% full, Devout 4% → 4%). Reported, not tuned.
  • Measured — a rotated pair, so all six new sets are actually exercised (DOD_SIM_ROTATE=1, n=60 each, its own control because the fixed test party only contains two of the six specs):
    rotatedcompletionswipe medianratio @ z1 t8choice
    off (control)15%z1 t9.01.0179%
    full17%z1 t11.01.0278%
    Two points of completions at n=60 is one run. Contribution share, off → full: Pyromancer 36→32, Warden 29→36, Holy 29→28, Survivalist 29→30, Cryomancer 26→27, Sharpshooter 25→20, Swordmaster 23→25, Arcanist 22→21, Occultist 19→15, Beastmaster 19→18, Devout 15→13, Berserker 15→16.
    • Holy is the spec this batch was told to read on contribution, not damage — and she does not move: 29% → 28% contribution off a 5–6% damage share, ~339 → ~329 healing per battle. That reproduces Batch W's "28% contribution off 5% damage share, 337 heal/battle" almost exactly. Her runes did not measurably change her at this sampling.
    • The most likely reason is dilution, and it applies to the whole batch: the party buys about 2.4 runes per run at the shop plus its elite-cache picks, spread across four heroes. The mage and cleric spec pools just tripled, so a given Holy is carrying at most one or two of her four — the pool got deeper without any single hero getting more runes. That is a property of the rune economy, not of these entries, and it is the thing to look at before concluding the entries are underpowered.
    • Do not read the cleric support numbers as a direction. The Devout's healing per battle went 10 → 14 in the fixed-party pair and 25 → 13 in the rotated pair. Opposite signs on the same change is noise, and reporting only the flattering half would have been the mistake.
    • The flipped artefact warning is already visible in this table. The Sharpshooter — a Hunter, with no spec runes — falls 25% → 20%, the largest drop of any spec. That is pool depth. Do not tune on it.
  • The artefact warning FLIPS, and Batch X's wording must not be carried forward. Batch X warned that only the Warrior had spec runes, so any Warrior-favouring share shift was pool depth. After this batch, Warrior, Mage and Cleric have spec rune sets and the Hunter does not. Any contribution or damage-share shift that disfavours the Beastmaster, Sharpshooter or Survivalist is pool depth, not balance. Do not tune on it. Difficulty remains closed pending human playtesters.
  • Verified. test_runes.gd extended, 1523 checks: schema (one payload branch per entry — apply_payload is an if/elif chain, so a second branch is silently dropped), requires_ability present, self-consistent and reachable in some eligible hero's derivable kit, scarred-carries-a-real-cost, scarred-priced-under-its-clean-peer, scope leak-proofing in both directions, int restoration, exhaustion. Four new drift alarms: every stat field a rune writes must be a real BattleUnit property or a cfg field battle.gd consumes (a typo is a dud, not a crash — set() silently drops unknown names); something must actually read it; no rune may write one half of an exclusive talent pair (a rune must not hand back a road the tree made the player decline); and the call-site ordering assertion above. The coverage assertion the batch was asked for: every Warrior, Mage and Cleric spec has exactly four spec runes, one per lane in its own LANE_TREES entry plus exactly one splash — this is what catches a lane being renamed later. New test_forfeit.gd, 57 checks (booked as a forfeit and not a wipe, run over, save gone, summary survives clear_save and says Forfeited with its reason, every reason string reaches the summary, cancel is harmless; unreachable under each of sim / autoplay / sim_run including firing the burger id directly, with profile and save bytes identical; the nudge fires once, ends nothing, moves no HP, and never fires in a sim). New test_rune_battle.gd, 46 checks across three live autoplay battles — every rune of a spec equipped at once, the ordering fix proven live (Holy opens with Mercy in hand, the Arcanist's ceiling reads 6), counters proven to sum when two runes feed one, the Ice Lance and Arcane Barrage ability payloads landing on the real Ability objects, Resurrection and Mind Flay granted, and the live-value chip gotcha checked directly: the Pyromancer's Inferno Master readout shows the rune-boosted step, not the talent-only one. The White Flame's read site is proven against a deliberately fire-resistant warband, because a raider lineup would have left it unexercised. Headless parse: all 10 scenes, 0 SCRIPT ERROR.
  • Reported, not changed (two doc-in-code drifts found while authoring). (1) The Holy tree's Resurrection node text says "spend 3 Mercy"; the ability's faith_cost is 1, and the code is what runs. This is in-game UI text, so it is worse than a doc drift — but which number is wrong is a design call, not a transcription one, so it is flagged rather than silently reconciled. (2) Batch X's rule that "every scarred payload carries a negative term" is not literally true of the shipped Glass Rune, whose cost is +0.15 dmg_taken_bonus — a positive number that is the penalty. The schema check now knows about penalty-signed fields rather than rejecting a rune that already ships.

2026-08-03 — Batch Z: the alpha shell — glossary, orientation, run summary

  • Why: Batch Y made the map a decision surface; this batch makes the run legible — the other half of a rough playable alpha. A first-time tester had to absorb the initiative timeline, skill checks, Pressure/Break, seven damage schools, bleed buildup, five spec resources, lane-gated talents, runes, trophies, and relics with no explanation anywhere in the build — every tooltip explains a number, nothing explained a system. And a wipe reported two lines and a New Run button: twenty minutes of play, no record. A confused tester answers "what confused me," not "was it fun."
  • Stage 0 — Batch Y reconciliation: checked before writing any map code. All of Y's stage-4 legibility items were present and live: rest/shop/event tooltips, the "Tier N of 10 — the <boss> waits" ladder readout, gold roads out of the current node, and the three-state visited/reachable/unreachable reads. Nothing was re-implemented; this batch only ADDED a Glossary entry to the existing map burger and the framing card. No duplicate tier readouts or tooltip paths exist.
  • The glossary (stage 1): data/glossary.json + scripts/glossary.gd (the Enemies/Events/Relics/Runes pattern — adding an entry is a JSON edit). 66 entries across seven categories: combat flow (initiative, skill check, Pressure & Break, crit/miss/parry, fallen heroes), all 25 debuffs the battle code can apply plus Quick Draw, the seven damage schools + resists, all nine resources (Rage, Mana, Resonance, Mercy, Empower, Faith, Focus, Loyalty, Ruin), progression (lanes, the ceil(N/3) curve, points, trophies, node scaling), gear (runes, scarred runes, relics-vs-runes — its own entry, the most confusable distinction in the game), and the run itself. Every entry was written from the code, with numbers verified at the read sites, not from the master doc (see the drift list below). The panel (scripts/glossary_panel.gd) opens from the map burger, the Party screen, and the battle burger; a category tree on the left, entry text with cross-links on the right. In battle it is pure Control UI — no awaits, nothing that touches the timeline — and the battle scene gates its raw _input while the panel is open so a click cannot grade a live skill check through the overlay. Contextual hooks: status chips now append their glossary one-liner to their tooltip when it adds information. Ability-tooltip and resource-bar hooks were considered and deferred (the chip hook is the high-value one; the stage was already large).
  • First-run orientation (stage 2): two things, neither a tutorial. (1) A skippable framing card on the first map of a first run — what a run is, boss → descend → three zones, death ends the run, bosses leave permanent relics. (2) A one-time pointer at the skill check when the first battle's first timing bar would appear — the marker, the gold/green windows, and the multipliers (+15%/+25% Break on Perfect, 60%/ half Break on Sloppy), because a tester who never notices the timing window is playing a materially worse game and will report it as shallow. Both are gated on Profile flags (new flags bucket + flag/set_flag), set on dismissal so a returning tester is never re-taught. Sims and autoplay cannot trip either: the card lives in the map scene (RunSim never loads scenes) behind a sim_run guard, and the pointer requires not sim and not autoplay and Run.active — verified by test.
  • The run summary (stage 3): both end panels — defeat and completion — replaced by a real summary screen: outcome and depth ("Wiped — Zone 2 (The Scarlands), Tier 6 of 10"), the final battle's type/theme/lineup and who fell, each hero's spec with talent count, lane spread, runes, and trophies, damage share across the whole run, the run economy (battles won, elites taken, gold earned/spent/unspent, rests, events), relics carried, and a chronicle line (cycles survived / lost) so a session of runs accumulates into something visible. A Copy summary button puts a plain-text version on the clipboard — every wipe becomes a report a tester can paste straight back, feedback data with no telemetry built. Plumbing, both the easy-to-get-wrong pieces done as specified: the summary snapshots the run state BEFORE clear_save() (the save logic was NOT reordered — a dead-but-resumable run is a worse bug than a missing summary), and the new run ledger lives on Run (Run.tally, save v4, tolerant load), banked from a battle-local slice at battle end because the battle scene reloads between fights. _stat writes the real-play slice only when sim is false, so RunSim's stats path is untouched and nothing double-counts. Ledger bump sites: battles/elites and damage (battle end, real runs only), combat gold (award_gold), shop spend, rests.
  • Doc drift found by writing the glossary from code — the audit list. Every system glossed was re-derived from its read sites and then checked against the master doc. The load-bearing machinery held: the timeline formula, the skill-check windows and multipliers, Break, crit/miss/parry, the seven-school resist pipeline and its 85%/1-damage clamps, all nine resources, the talent gates (both models, correctly distinguished), and the run structure all matched the code exactly. Batch Y's §3 episode did not repeat. Eight corrections landed:
    1. Shieldwall was documented at −50%; the code does −25% (raw *= 0.75) — wrong in two places (the target-modifier list and the status roster). The in-game status tooltip already said 25%, so the doc and the tooltip had been contradicting each other. The combat log was lying too ("party takes half damage") and was fixed as part of this batch — a legibility batch does not ship a string that misstates a number to the player's face.
    2. Waystone Shard: §7 said +1 talent point at run start, the relic grants +3 — and §8 of the same document already said +3. Self-contradiction resolved to the code.
    3. The run-flow line advertised "Battles / Rest / Loot". There are no Loot nodes (see below); corrected to Battles / Rest / Shop / ???.
    4. The status table gave Poison as "3 nature damage per stack" (flat). It is 3% of the applier's Attack — the same document's §4.5 said so correctly. The two agree only at exactly 100 Attack, which is why it survived this long.
    5. The relic line said the elemental trio "covers all six damage schools" — the game has seven; the trio covers the six non-physical ones.
    6. The Party-screen description said hovering Resistances "lists all six elements" — it lists seven, physical included.
    7. The Peddler was reached via "the 6 shop nodes"; the deck holds 5.
    8. CLAUDE.md: the rune line headlined "26 authored" while its own components (5 universal + 3×4 class + 12 warrior-spec) add to 29, which is what data/runes.json holds; and a stale Batch-28 line still gave talent income as 2/4/4, superseded by 1/2/3 since Batch 30. Both corrected.
    Note the shape of these: none is a system working differently than designed. They are numbers that moved once and left a copy behind — which is precisely the drift a glossary written from the doc would have taught to every tester as fact.
  • Dead code recorded, not removed: the treasure/"Loot" node type still has a label, a colour, and a live click handler in map_screen.gd, but neither map generator has dealt one since the Batch 38 deck (17 fight / 5 rest / 5 shop / 3 event) — elites are in-place upgrades of fights, and the boss is appended, so the four dealt types are all a player ever sees. Left in place rather than removed mid-batch; the glossary does not mention Loot nodes, because no tester will ever meet one.
  • Verification (stage 4): scratchpad test_glossary.gd — 859 checks: schema (id/term/category/short/long, see_also resolution, no duplicate ids or terms), coverage alarms (every BattleUnit.DEBUFF_IDS status, every damage school incl. a sweep of every resist key in enemies.json, and a spec→resource map asserted to cover Classes.SPEC_INFO exactly — a new spec or status fails the test until the glossary learns it). test_run_summary.gd — 23 checks: the ledger accumulates across simulated scene reloads and matches per-battle totals, sim-mode _stat leaves the run slice alone, the snapshot survives clear_save and is a deep copy, summary text formats from the snapshot alone, save v4 round-trips the ledger, pre-Z saves self-heal (the real save file is backed up and restored around the test). test_glossary_battle.gd — an autoplay battle opens the panel mid-fight: the timeline keeps advancing under the open panel, closes clean, battle carries on, and no profile file is written. Headless parse: all ten scenes, zero script errors. Sim purity: a DOD_SIM_RUN run leaves profile.json and run_save.bin byte-identical (hash-checked).

2026-08-03 — Batch Y: the map node economy (alpha-scoped)

  • Why: the map was documented as a route-planning surface, but two generation rules made that aspirational. The 70% adjacent-link roll left 53% of nodes reaching exactly one node (measured — worse than the 30% the rule implies, because an edge column could roll its adjacent out of range and get nothing), and the 30-card deal was blind to position: nothing guaranteed a rest in the back half, a shop before the boss, or an elite near your path. Measured before any change (route=default, economy on, n=50): 1.58 reachable nodes per step, a real choice on 34% of steps (≥2 reachable AND not all one type), rests ever-reachable 4.3 of 9.0 dealt. The player was walking, not routing. This batch targets a rough playable alpha — legibility and agency are the deliverables; difficulty stays deliberately unsettled (Batch T owns the numbers, and the designer closed difficulty pending human playtesters).
  • Instrumentation first (stage 0): the run report gained a route agency block — reachable nodes per step, % of steps offering a real choice, taken-vs-offered per node type, and dealt-vs-ever-reachable per type — and the pre-change baseline above was recorded before a single generation rule moved. The Matrix row line now carries map=, diff= and choice= fields.
  • Links (stage 1): every node now links to its own column plus at least one adjacent column, guaranteed; a middle column links to both neighbours 25% of the time. The inbound-path guarantee is unchanged, and the pre-boss funnel to a single boss node stays by design. The old generator is preserved verbatim behind DOD_SIM_MAP=old — every pre-Y baseline row (including DOD_SIM_ROUTE=greedy as the Batch S floor) only reproduces at map=old.
  • The structured deal (stage 2): the deck stays 17/5/5/3, but the deal is constrained — no tier deals three of a kind; at least one rest in tiers 2–5 AND one in tiers 6–10; at least one shop in tiers 7–10; and at least one elite per zone (the 25% roll on tiers 6+ gets a floor, applied after the roll). Every map is also guaranteed a single walkable route touching 2 rests + a shop + an elite, checked by a graph walk at generation. Dealing is rejection sampling (bounded at 40 reshuffles) with a deterministic swap repair and an authored last-resort arrangement — generation can never fail. Measured: ~7% of maps use the swap repair; repaired maps satisfy every constraint.
  • Validator (stage 3): scratchpad test_map_economy.gd — 500 new maps + 50 old-gate maps, 37,710/37,710 checks: composition conserved, no homogeneous tier, no stranded node, boss reachable from every gate node, the 2-rest/shop/elite route exists (independent DP, never trusting the generator's own check), zero single-link nodes (mean 2.09 links/node, 80% of nodes offer a typed choice), and the old gate really is the old corridor (53% single-link nodes reappear at map=old).
  • Legibility (stage 4): non-combat nodes answer on hover — the rest quotes its actual heal percentage including relic bonuses, the shop states its stock, and the "???" event says outright that what waits is unknown (mystery by design, not a blank). A ladder readout ("Tier 7 of 10 — the Withered Warden waits") replaces the static subtitle. The roads out of the current node glow gold; visited / reachable / unreachable nodes sit in three clearly separate reads.
  • The Road (stage 5, ALPHA TESTING AFFORDANCE — not a balance decision): the draft offers Standard (ZONE_BASE_MULTS exactly as they are) or Wanderer (enemies at 70% strength, one multiplier folded into the single zone_base_mult read site — trivially removable when difficulty is actually decided). Saved with the run (save v3, tolerant load). Sims arm it via DOD_SIM_DIFFICULTY (default standard) so no baseline row can be contaminated. It exists because a tester who never reaches zone 3 cannot say whether the game is fun; it does NOT supersede Batch T, and no other number was adjusted around it.
  • Measured (route=default, economy on, n=50/row): control at map=old reproduces the baseline within noise (0% completions, ratio@z1t8 0.97, choice 36%) — the batch is readable. New map: choice rate 34% → 80%, reachable/step 1.58 → 2.16, elites ever-reachable 1.2 → 2.2 per run, completions 0% → 4% (said in advance: completions were expected to rise as a consequence of agency, and the effect at standard is small). New map + Wanderer: 58% completions, wipe median z3 t5 — a competent tester now sees all three zones and the finale, which is the affordance doing exactly its job (z1 ratio 1.44 = z1 is easy on Wanderer, accepted for the alpha).
  • Honest artifact, not tuned away: with real choice, the fixed bot's fight-first preference now binds — shops taken fell 1.6 → 0.6/run (of 5.7 offered) and unspent gold doubled, because the old corridor was force-feeding the bot the economy (shops used to be the only reachable node). The agency numbers are the deliverable; the bot's spending habits are a policy artifact a human player will not share.
  • Saves: a pre-Y save keeps its old map and old deal — correct, never migrated; nothing at runtime assumes the new guarantees hold on a loaded map.

2026-08-02 — Batch X: runes become a build system

  • Why: every prior batch measured the same wall — zone 1 roughly right, runs die in zones 2–3 — and the party had no in-run power source that keeps pace with depth (nodes +2% linear, talents bounded, relics flat). Runs also needed to differ from each other: relics and events got authored vocabularies; runes had six stat templates and a multiplier. Runes were already plumbed end to end, so they got both jobs. No new payload machinery — everything routes through the existing Talents.apply_payload branches.
  • Slot growth (stage 1, landed and measured ALONE): rune slots are now Run.rune_slots() = 2/3/4 by zone slot, no cap (a 4th zone gives 5). Every hardcoded 2 routed through the accessor (party-screen equip cap + header, run-sim elite auto-equip and shop free-slot check); the zone-victory screen announces the new slot. No save migration — the count derives from zone_idx. Latent bug fixed: the party screen drew at most 4 rune rows, so a pouch of 5+ silently hid runes; the rows now live in a scroller and every owned rune renders. Measured alone against the OLD generator: 8% completions, wipe median z1 t11, ratio@z1t8 0.97 — noise-range vs the old-generator baseline, as expected with only ~2.6 runes bought per run (the 2-slot cap was rarely binding). The slot lever waits on a pool worth filling slots with.
  • Authored pool (stages 2–3): scripts/runes.gd wrapping data/runes.json (the Enemies/Events/Relics pattern) — 26 authored runes: 5 universal, 9 class (3 per class, all four classes ON PURPOSE — a Warrior-only pool would make any Warrior share shift a pool-depth artefact, the Batch W denominator lesson), and 12 Warrior spec runes as the pilot (Berserker/Swordmaster/Warden). Spec sets follow the one-rune-per-talent-lane rule (one deepens each lane, one pays for splashing out), so build variety is structural. Rarity means kind (Common = stat, the six old templates surviving as the filler family and exhaustion floor; Rare = alters an ability or grants a talent-like effect, counter fields stacking with the talent by design; Epic = grants an ability / changes a damage type / inverts a rule), weights shifting by zone slot 60/30/10 → 40/40/20 → 25/45/30, formulaic past 3. Scarred is a boolean flag on Rare/Epic (own prefix + colour, every payload carries a negative term, priced BELOW the clean peer). Eligibility: scope universal / class:<key> / spec:<id>, and ability-payload runes carry requires_ability checked against the derivable kit (core + spec + kit-override renames + boss trophies) — the apply_payload ability branch matches display names and a rune naming an unowned ability is a silent dud, so eligibility is load-bearing. Exhausted pools widen, then fall back to the Common family — offers never come back empty. JSON payload numbers are int-restored on load (the Enemies AB_INT_KEYS lesson).
  • New cfg fields — 4 of the sanctioned 4, each read at exactly ONE battle.gd site (the relics hook-audit precedent), each logging a grep-stable "Rune:" line: blood_pact (Exsanguination: enemy bleedouts pop at 85 for 15% max HP — read in _add_bleed_with_burst), rune_lifesteal (Vampiric Rune — read beside the ability-lifesteal block in the strike loop), rune_execute_bonus (Reaper: +damage below 35% HP — read in the raw-damage block by Grudge), and rune_bd_bonus (Breaker runes: Break-damage multiplier — read in the pressure block after Broken Will). Everything else in the pool rides existing fields and existing ability names.
  • Elite pick-of-3 (stage 4): the elite drop is no longer a random rune onto a random hero — three candidates roll at drop time, are stored on the member (rune_candidates queue + rune_picks_owed mirroring bm_picks_owed, saved with the run, never rerolled on a screen open), and the pick resolves on the Party screen via the boss-trophy picker pattern; the chosen rune auto-equips while a slot is free. Sim bot policy, printed in the report header: build-lane match → spec-scoped → first candidate.
  • Measured (route=default, economy on, 50 runs per row; DOD_SIM_RUNES flag: off/stats/full):
    — control first: --sweep 200 fixed party at off reproduces Batch W within noise (win 100/100/98/96, deaths 0.01→0.96, Cryo 49/42/40/37) — the baseline is controlled, no read-site regression.
    — off = 4% completions, wipe median z2 t2.5, ratio@z1t8 0.98, 0 runes/run.
    — stats (≈ pre-batch generator) = 2%, z1 t11, 0.94, 1.7 runes bought/run.
    — full (authored pool) = 6%, z1 t11, 0.98, 2.9 runes bought/run.
    — stage-1-alone row (slots grown, old generator): 8%, z1 t11, 0.97.
    Reading (report, not tune — Batch T owns the difficulty numbers): at n=50 the three rows sit 2–6% — the authored pool at current authored power does NOT move completions beyond noise; the z2 mid-band ratio nudges up under full (z2 t7 0.73→0.85; z3 tail 0.44–0.73 → 0.45–0.73) but the z2/z3 collapse survives. The depth question stays open, and the next lever is authored rune POWER (a data edit), not machinery. Damage shares are flat across rows (Berserker 28/28/28, Cryo 38/39/39) — no Warrior-favouring shift materialised at current power.
    Artefact warning 1: only the Warrior has spec runes this batch — any future damage-share or contribution shift favouring Warrior specs is POOL DEPTH, not balance; do not tune on it.
    Artefact warning 2: the rotated sweep's twelve-spec table is only comparable to Batch W at DOD_SIM_RUNES=off — say so wherever that table is reproduced.
    Comparator honesty: the Batch V fixed-party row (14%, z2 t6) does NOT reproduce on the current build even at off — Batch W's stalemate guard (stuck battles now scored as losses) sits between. Matrix rows are only comparable within one batch's flags, per the standing rule.
  • Verification: scratchpad test_runes.gd (920 checks: schema, ability-existence + requires_ability, stat-field existence, scarred negative terms + underpricing, scope/trophy eligibility incl. the bm_abilities case, exhaustion floor, int restoration through Ability.make, payload application, rarity-weight sums, rune_slots 2/3/4 + no-cap, save round-trip incl. stored candidates, sim purity) — all pass. Live proof test_rune_battle.gd: a headless autoplay battle with the Warpath and Exsanguination runes equipped shows the altered Hack and Slash (+30 Bleed) and the scarred early bleedout ("bleedout at 85") in the combat log. Parse checks clean across all ten scenes.

2026-08-01 — Batch W: the outlier pass (measure first)

  • The gap that mattered more than the outlier: every sim in this project had run the same four specs (berserker, cryomancer, inquisitor, beastmaster — the default in sim.sh, the sweep and the run harness), so eight of the twelve specs had never appeared in a damage-share report, and the Sharpshooter's 38% — cited as the game's worst outlier since Batch 31 — predated half the roster's rework. This batch measures all twelve on the current build.
  • DOD_SIM_ROTATE=1 cycles the spec in each class slot across successive battles (sweep/standalone) or runs (--run, never mid-run — that would be a different game). Schedule lives in Classes.rotated_specs(n): any 3 consecutive counters sample each class's three specs exactly once, and drift terms reshuffle which specs land TOGETHER (27 distinct parties, 66-67 samples per spec per 200 battles) so no spec is only ever measured beside the same trio. Shares now print a sample count, and the denominator is the spec's OWN pool — the party damage of the battles it was in — so a fixed party reproduces pre-W numbers exactly while a rotated one stays on the same 4-hero basis. The fixed default party is unchanged: rotation is opt-in, A/B against every prior batch still works.
  • Support contribution metrics (both reports, per hero): healing done, damage prevented, Break damage applied, statuses applied, plus a normalised contribution share (damage + healing + prevented). Prevented damage is instrumented at the mitigation sites that already compute a delta (parry, Block incl. Interpose-granted charges credited to the Warden who granted them, barrier absorbs credited to the caster, Shieldwall, stance, Faith, Iron Will, Shared Vigil, Consecrated Ground, Blessed Vestments, Savage Presence, Frost Ward, Molten Core, Flame Shield, the Chilled swing malus), never derived; anything unattributable reports as its own unattributed bucket rather than being dropped (it measured 0-1/battle). Base armor and resists are deliberately NOT counted — a stat block is not a contribution, and counting them would make every frontliner read as a guardian. Sanity checks from the batch brief all pass: Warden and Devout high on prevented (165 / 136 per battle at budget 12), Holy high on healing (431), Berserker near zero on both, no tank reading zero.
  • THE TWELVE-SPEC TABLE (rotated sweep, 200 battles per budget, zone-1 roster, n = 66-67 per spec per budget; damage share against that spec's own party pool):
    specb3b6b9b12heal/b (b12) prev/b (b12)BD/b (b12)contrib% (b12)

    Rotated sweep difficulty curve: 100 / 98 / 92 / 82% wins at budgets 3/6/9/12 (deaths/battle 0.00/0.33/0.82/1.34) — a rotating field is markedly harder than the fixed party's 100/100/100/96, which is itself a finding: the standard test party is one of the stronger compositions, not a neutral one.
  • The Sharpshooter's 38% is retired. Measured on the current build he is 32-36% — mid-pack among damage specs, and NOT the game's worst outlier. Tuning him against the stale figure would have been exactly the mistake Batch R was written to stop.
  • Three specs were invisible, not weak. The Devout's 3-5% damage was an artefact of asking a Warder how much damage he does: he prevents 136/battle at budget 12 and reads 15% contribution. The Holy is the clearest case — 6-13% damage share, but 431 healing/battle and a 33% contribution share, the highest of any spec at budget 12. On the old instrument he looked like the weakest spec in the game; he is one of the strongest. The Occultist's healing (102/battle) is likewise a side effect of debuffing, and now visible.
  • Cryomancer — the diagnosis did not survive measurement. Ice Lance's Batch O clause was walked back +10% → +5% of Attack per Chilled stack (Crystal Edge still adds +5%/rank on top; Permafrost's permanence and Razor Ice's three shards untouched, as instructed — they fixed a real structural fault). It barely moved the number. Fixed-party control, 200/budget: 49/42/39/39% at budgets 3/6/9/12 versus Batch V's 48→40 — inside noise, despite Ice Lance being cast a real 1.1x/battle. Per the brief the second lever was then measured ALONE: freeze remainder 1 stack → 0 gave 47/42/40/38% — also noise, so it was reverted and the ember stays (Batch O's declared knob is spent, and now known not to be the lever). Under rotation he reads 38/39/40/37%. Both levers reported as required; only the Ice Lance halving is kept, on the grounds the brief gave for it (+10% on a permanent-stack passive plus an auto-crit did more than "a reason to exist outside the Frozen window"), not on grounds it solved anything.
  • Why his 40-48% was partly an instrument artefact: the fixed test party contains the Devout, the lowest-damage spec in the game (3-5%). With one member contributing ~4%, the other three split ~96% — every share in that party is inflated by construction. Measured against a rotating field the Cryomancer is 37-40%, and his own classmates read 34-47%: he is typical for a mage, not an outlier among mages. The mage class as a whole out-damages the rest of the roster, which is a class-balance question and not a Cryomancer question.
  • The real outlier is the Pyromancer — 42/46/46/47%, higher than the Cryomancer at every budget, and it CLIMBS with field size, which by this project's own diagnostic is the AoE signature (a share that falls with field size is baseline overtuning; one that climbs is the AoE). Per the brief's rule — "if the rotation turns up something worse than the Cryomancer, that's the next conversation, not this one" — he is deliberately untouched. No changes were made to the Sharpshooter, the Devout, or any of the eight newly-measured specs.
  • Harness bug found and fixed (pre-existing, exposed by rotation): _log appended to the battle history RichTextLabel even in headless sims, where nothing can read it. Every line allocates TextServer RIDs; a long verbose battle exhausts the pool, after which each _log call emits an 8-line engine backtrace. A 13-minute sweep ran 3+ hours and wrote a 500 MB log while still making forward progress — a hang that is really I/O thrash. _log now skips the panel when sim; DOD_SIM_DEBUG=1 still prints. It surfaced only under rotation because the never-simulated specs (Holy, Occultist, Survivalist) log several times more per battle. Documented in CLAUDE.md as a third sim-hang failure mode, distinguished from the other two by %CPU (30-50%, not 1-2%) and log size.
  • Rotated run harness, 50 runs (route=default, economy on): completions 12%, wipe median z1 t9.0, ratio@z1t8 0.97 — versus Batch V's fixed-party 14% / z2 t6. A rotating roster is harder than the standard party in the run harness too, the same direction the sweep showed. Runs sampled per spec 16-17 each; battle counts per spec vary (n=129 Swordmaster to n=273 Warden) because durable parties survive deeper and therefore fight more battles — that spread is itself a signal. The contribution metric pays off hardest here: the Warden posts 206 damage prevented per battle and 33% contribution — the highest of any spec in the run harness — off 27% damage share, and the Holy reads 28% contribution off 5% damage (337 healing/battle). On damage share alone, the game's two most valuable specs looked unremarkable and near-worthless respectively.
  • WARDEN SOFTLOCK FOUND (real play, not a sim artefact) — diagnosis corrected on 08-02. The symptom was Endurance reporting +97,521% armor, and that is what this entry first blamed. It was wrong: effective_armor() has always ended in minf(a, 0.85), so the runaway number never reached the damage path — the chip was lying, not driving. The real loop is Tenacity feeding Unkillable: Tenacity adds +5 max HP per Heavy Plating block with no bound (battle-long), and Unkillable mends 2%/rank of max HP per block — so every block enlarges the pool that the next block heals from. Measured: max HP ~127,000, mends of 7,607 per block. Sitting at the 0.85 armor clamp he takes 15% of incoming damage and out-heals it indefinitely, so the battle can never end. In real play that is a softlock, not just a slow sim. It had never surfaced because the fixed test party contains no Warden and the sweep grants no talents; only the run harness buys them, so rotation put a talent-invested Warden into a long fight for the first time.
  • Endurance capped at +75% armor (08-02, at the designer's direction). This stops the chip and combat log advertising a bonus the damage path can never grant, and bounds the term for any future armor source that reads it before the clamp. It does not close the softlock, and was not expected to: with base armor 0.32 the Warden reaches the 0.85 clamp after roughly 18 unhealed turns with or without the cap.
  • SOFTLOCK FIXED (08-02, designer chose the Unkillable side): Unkillable now mends 2%/rank of base max HP — max_hp - tenacity_hp_gained, the same "true max" idiom the end-of-battle save sync already uses — instead of the live pool. Tenacity is untouched and still grants +5 max HP per Heavy Plating block; the two simply no longer feed each other off their shared trigger. Verified with a talent-invested Warden (DOD_SIM_TALENTS="wd_unkillable:3,wd_tenacity:1"): the mend is a flat 12 across 56 blocks (6% of base 200) where it previously climbed to 7,607. The rule this teaches, worth applying to future kits: a percentage effect must never read off a value that its own trigger inflates.
  • ...but it did NOT close the endless battle, and the cause is now established by evidence. Re-running the rotated 50-run after the fix left the stalemate count unchanged (5 → 6 of ~700 battles), which retired the second diagnosis as well. The debug log of an actual stalled battle shows what is really happening: the party is down to a single surviving Warden — 169 of 169 hero actions in the final window are his — facing a five-strong warband holding a Shaman, a Totemist and a Shieldmaster, with 92 heal/shield events in that same window. He cannot die (0.85 armor clamp, Block, Unkillable) and his ~17-damage single-target strike cannot out-pace five enemies' healing. Neither side can finish. This is structural, not a talent bug: any last-hero-standing durable-but-low-damage survivor against a healing warband reproduces it, and rotation exposed it because rotated parties form low-damage compositions the fixed test party never did. Both earlier fixes are kept on their own merits — an absurd 7,607 mend and a lying armor chip were each worth removing — but neither was the cause, and this entry previously said otherwise. Open design question for real play: a human who reaches this state is stuck in an unwinnable, unloseable fight; the game currently has no resolution mechanism. Options are a turn limit, escalating pressure on both sides, or a flee/forfeit action — a design call left with the designer. Sims are covered by the counted stalemate guard.
  • Stalemate guard (SIMS ONLY, real play untouched): a battle exceeding 600 unit turns (~60 rounds; a long real battle is 4-12) is force-ended. Scored as a loss, never a win — a free victory would inflate completions — and no HP is touched, so death stats stay honest. Every trip is counted and printed in all three reports, so the cap can never pass silently. Measured: 5 stalemates across the 50-run invocation (~700 battles). Validated both directions: forced to 25 turns it fires on 8/8 battles and reports them; at 600 a normal 40-battle sim is 40/40 with zero.
  • Run harness now prints per-run progress ("run 12/50 — wiped z2 t7 (3 completed so far)"), mirroring the sweep's per-stage marker. The run report only printed at the very end, so a long invocation was indistinguishable from a hung one for its entire life — which cost this batch a 48-minute run that turned out to be healthy and was then lost to session teardown with nothing to show for it.
  • Known reporting limitation (stated, not silent): the "Hero ability uses/battle" line divides by ALL battles, not battles-that-spec-was-present, so under rotation it reads ~3x low. The contribution table divides by n correctly.

2026-08-01 — Batch V: enemies that ask questions

  • Four new enemy kinds, each posing a question a specific hero spec answers — the roster's sixteen kinds shared three special behaviours between them (enemy_shield, healing_wave, wild_growth), so enemies never engaged the heroes' status engines. No existing enemy's stats changed (Batch T just tuned the curve against that roster). The new kinds: Orc Ritual Chanter (power 2, mender, all rosters — fast, fragile, physically soft), Ash Hurler (power 3, brute·sniper, rosters 2–3 — slow ranged siege), Orc Bloodcaller (power 2, hexer, all rosters), and Grave Totem (power 2, mender·hexer, rosters 1–3 forest slots — Con 60 but physical resist 35%, fire/holy vulnerable 50%).
  • Four new special behaviours (the batch's working title said three; the data design needed four). cleanse_allies — Cleansing Rite strips the longest-remaining debuff from each living ally, with guardrails: Chilled thaws ONE stack (a single cast must not erase four turns of Cryomancer work), stripping Ruin also clears its primer, sticky poisons (Slow Acting/Epidemic) refuse it, and Broken/Bleed are meter states it never touches. windup — Siege Stone applies a visible Charging chip on cast and resolves the following turn as a party-wide attack through the normal pipeline; being Broken, Frozen, or Stunned while charging cancels it entirely, loudly logged — the first telegraphed enemy mechanic. blood_tribute — +25% damage and Break damage per enemy fallen this battle, resolved as a scaled attack copy so armor/resists/crits apply. totem_pulse — Dark Vigil heals every living ally 6% of its own max HP. All four ride the condition-gated support AI (the Chanter casts when any ally carries a strippable debuff, the Totem when two or more allies are wounded), and a psychotic Chanter or Totem turns its magic on the heroes, like every other enemy support.
  • Mender cap: the theme generator could roll three and four healer warbands (2,007 distinct ones existed — Ritual and Lightning Storm pools go to 3–4); with a cleanse joining the roster, that is a wall, not a question. _combo_ok now rejects any warband with more than two mender-TAGGED kinds, counted by role tag no matter which pool claimed them (a Grave Totem drawn through a hexer pool still heals). Theme-safety audit (recreated test_zone_rosters.gd): satisfiability matrices before/after show no theme lost a single budget in any roster — the new kinds only added rolls (Swarm r2 now reaches 10–12, Poison Volley r2 reaches 12) — and the Rot Behemoth still appears in roster-2 brute slots alongside the Hurler. Distinct warbands: 11,344 → 23,561.
  • Verified headless (forced-lineup autoplay battles): a Cleansing Rite stripped Pyromancer Burns; a Siege Stone charge resolved the following turn party-wide; Blood Tribute logged +25% after one death; Dark Vigil pulsed 53 HP across a five-body warband; and the load-bearing one — a Swordmaster Break cancelled a mid-charge Siege Stone ("slips from its grasp — CANCELLED"), with a Pommel-Strike stun cancelling another. Zero script errors; map-screen resist preview reads the new kinds from the same JSON as everything else.
  • Doc repair while in §9: the tier-scaling paragraph still described the pre-Batch-36 game-long ladder at the pre-Batch-T rates (+4%/+5%); corrected to zone-local +2%/+2.5% atop the ×1.0/1.5/2.2 slot multipliers.
  • Re-measure (report only, nothing tuned — Batch T owns the numbers): sweep 200, roster 1: 100 / 100 / 98.5 / 97.5% wins at budgets 3/6/9/12 vs Batch R's 100/100/98/93.5 — mid budgets flat, top band EASIER (+4pts; deaths/battle at 12 fell ~1.3–1.5 → 1.00), inverting the batch's own prediction. Two causes: the Hurler is rosters 2–3 only, so the roster-1 sweep never sees the one kind meant to spike the top; and the mender cap deleted the 3–4-healer warbands that lived exactly in the top budgets. Run 50 (route=default, economy on, vs Batch U's matching row): completions 6% → 14%, wipe median z1 t9-10 → z2 t6 — the wipe median moved MORE than one tier, so per this batch's own rule that is a tuning conversation to have separately, not a silent adjustment. The z1 curve itself is unchanged (t8 ratio 0.99 in band, t9-10 at 0.86/0.84, boss 0.72 vs T's 0.70; 33/50 runs reach the z1 boss, 94% win) — the easing is concentrated where heal-wall warbands used to grind runs down, which points at the cap, not the kinds. Caveats stated: n=50 completion counts are noisy (7 vs 3 runs); the corroborating signals are the median shift and the sweep's top-band death drop. z2/z3 tails sit where U left them (z2 t10 0.65, z3 t9-10 0.50/0.51, z3 boss 0.41). Damage share: Cryomancer 40–48% falling with field size, Devout 3–5% — the outlier pass's targets are untouched by this batch.

2026-08-01 — Batch U: an honest floor

  • Harness batch, zero tuning — Batch T owns the numbers; this batch establishes what they mean. Batch S's floor was measured by a bot that walked past every rest node into fights at 45% health, bought nothing, and never drank a potion it carried — so an unknown share of the tiers-4–7 wipe cluster was routing artefact, not difficulty fact. The harness now plays three route policies (DOD_SIM_ROUTE): greedy keeps the old ladder byte for byte (fight > elite always; rest only when no combat is offered and someone is under 60%), default rests when average party HP falls below 65% and a rest is reachable, cautious rests below 80%. Shops are played (heal-first: any hero under 50% buys a Health Potion each; then the priciest affordable offer not already carried — runes included, only onto a member with a free slot, equipped at purchase; no buy dips below the 40g reserve; DOD_SIM_SHOPS=off restores the buy-nothing floor). In battle a hero opening a turn below 35% HP drinks a carried Health Potion before acting (DOD_SIM_ITEMS=off restores the never-drinks floor; sweep/standalone battles always stay dry). The report adds the run economy — gold earned/spent/unspent, items used and carried unused, rests taken versus offered — and a machine-comparable matrix row per invocation.
  • Shop schema, reported before the policy was set: five consumables at flat prices (Health/Mana Potion 30g, Defense 40g, Bomb 45g, Revive 80g), unlimited stock, no tier scaling, only the relic discount moves prices; plus one rune offer per party member per visit (Common 50g / Rare 100g / Epic 160g — permanent run-scoped stat boosts, one copy each). A run STARTS carrying all five consumables, so "the priciest item not already carried" buys almost nothing on its own — runes are the shop's only real gold sink, which is why the purchase rule covers them.
  • The four-row matrix (50 runs each; the control row reproduces the Batch T floor before anything else is read — 2/50 completions, 22 z1-boss entries at 82%, Cryomancer 40%, boss ratio 0.69: the baseline is controlled): 
    Route policy          Completions   Wipe median      Ratio @ z1 t8
greedy (floor ctrl*)      4%          z1 t10             0.90
greedy                    0%          z1 t9              0.98
default                   6%          z1 boss            0.89
cautious                  8%          z1 boss            0.97
* shops+items OFF — the Batch S/T floor. Ratio@t8 wobble (0.89–0.98)
  is sampling noise: ~20 fights land on that tier per row.
  • Verdict — routing was NOT the story. The spread between greedy and cautious is 0–4% → 8% completions: real, roughly a doubling of the old floor, and far too small to reframe the difficulty question. Even resting at 80%, buying runes and drinking potions, 46/50 runs still wipe. The z1 t4–7 cluster thins (15 → 10 wipes) and the survivors die later (wipe median moves t9–10 → the z1 boss; z1-boss wipes 4 → 7), but the curve's shape survives every policy. Batch T's calibration was NOT made against a misleadingly depressed floor — its stage-3 numbers stand, and the deferred levers stay what they were: the z1 tail (t9–boss 0.86/0.82/0.68), the z2–z3 collapse (0.66 by z2 t7, 0.37–0.43 at the z3 tail — no 40 HP potion closes a 2× effective-power gap), and the outlier pass (Cryomancer 38–40% and Devout 4% at the honest floor too — the overtune is policy-independent, ready to measure).
  • Rests offered versus taken — the diagnostic line: taken went 1.6/run (floor control) → 2.6/run (cautious) against 3.3–3.9 offered. Five rest nodes exist per zone map, but one-step reachability means the walk is only ever OFFERED about four, and the most cautious policy uses two and a half. The recovery the old harness left on the table was ~one extra rest per run — party HP entering z1 t7 rose 55% → 62% — not the five-rests-a-zone the node deck implies. Route planning toward rests (map lookahead) is the one lever this batch deliberately did not model.
  • The gold pile was real: the floor accrued 422g unspent per run. The policy converts about half (spent 222–225/run: ~2.2 runes, ~1.5 heal potions, the rest reserve-blocked or unaffordable at death) and the bot drinks 3.8–4.3 potions per run — worth roughly +2–4 completion points and one wipe-band of depth, no more. The exclusions were costing measurement honesty, not hiding a solved game.
  • Deeper reads now on record: the honest floor puts 60% of cautious runs at the z1 boss (77% win there, ≈46% of runs clear zone 1 vs 28% under Batch T's floor) — zone 1 is roughly right; runs die in zones 2–3 where the measured ratio collapses regardless of policy. Talent income at the honest floor: 11.4–12.2 pts/hero/run (economy assumes ~35 — the gap is depth-of-run, not award rates).
  • Verification: the three correctness gates re-run green (recovered from the Batch T scratchpad); headless parse clean; the greedy control reproduced Batch T within noise BEFORE the policy rows were read, per the batch's stop-rule. Simulated runs still never touch the save/relics/profile — the shop policy spends Run.gold in memory only.

2026-08-01 — Batch T: close the gap

  • The first tuning batch of the difficulty-convergence arc, staged and measured: one change, then ./sim.sh --run 50, then decide whether the next stage was needed. Baseline (Batch S): 0/50 completions, 40 of 50 wipes in zone 1 tiers 4–7, the measured power ratio crossing under 1.0 exactly where the budget ladder stepped 3–6 → 6–9. Target: ratio 1.0–1.3 at every zone-1 tier; the bot floor reaching the zone-1 boss in most runs. No spec's damage numbers were touched (the Cryomancer/Sharpshooter/Devout outlier pass is deliberately deferred until it can be measured over a stable curve — their shares held at 39/27-ish/4% through all three stages, so the overtune is tier-independent and will still be there to measure).
  • Stage 1 — the awakening HP sync (correctness, not tuning): spec stat blocks raise max HP at the awakening, but current HP stayed at the class base until the first victory sync — the party entered its first specced fight at ~75% health. New Run.sync_spec_hp raises current HP by the same amount as the max-HP raise (never to full: damage already taken is preserved), called from BOTH awakening paths — the spec-choice screen and the run sim's direct assignment. Measured alone: still 0/50 completions and the ratio table barely moved (it reads max stats, as predicted) — but tier-4 win rate jumped 44% → 79%, tier-8 20% → 56%, and a run crossed into zone 2 for the first time. Ratios z1: t1 1.21 / t3 1.15 / t4 0.77 / t6 0.80 / t8 0.57 / t10 0.72.
  • Stage 2 — the budget ladder became a ramp: three bands over eleven tiers meant two discontinuities with the wipes sitting on the first; now a tier's fight budget rolls 3 + floor((tier−1)/2) to that +2 (t1 3–5 ... t10 7–9). Two guards preserved authored content: elite budgets floor at 6 (the cheapest elite theme, Rage Company, costs 6 in every roster — below that an elite node silently degraded to the plain-mob fallback while still paying elite rewards) and the boss node keeps its 10–12 band (the Escort composition is authored, so the tier-10 → boss step is now the zone's sharpest — accepted, see stage 3's table). Measured: the tier-4 cliff is gone (ratio 0.77 → 1.16, win 44% → 97%); 9 runs reached the z1 boss, 5 entered zone 2, 1 reached zone 3; talent income rose 3.8 → 5.7 points/hero/run as the attrition loop started unwinding. The wall moved to t7–t10 (0.73–0.78). Ratios z1: t1 1.42 / t2 1.28 / t3 1.13 / t4 1.16 / t5 0.92 / t6 0.94 / t7 0.78 / t8 0.73 / t9 0.75 / t10 0.75 / boss 0.57.
  • Stage 3 — enemy tier scaling halved (its condition — tiers 7–11 still below 1.0 — was met): +4% Attack / +5% HP per tier → +2% / +2.5%. The zone slot multiplier (×1.0 / ×1.5 / ×2.2) is untouched, so between-zone escalation still carries the run's progression arc; what fell is the second multiplicative source compounding on the budget ramp within a zone. Measured: the first completed runs in the game's history — 2/50 full three-zone clears; 20/50 reached the z1 boss (70% win there), 14 cleared zone 1. Talent income 9.3 points/hero/run (2.4× baseline). Ratios z1: t1 1.37 / t2 1.42 / t3 1.28 / t4 1.20 / t5 1.01 / t6 1.03 / t7 0.95 / t8 0.95 / t9 0.87 / t10 0.78 / boss 0.70.
  • Stage 4 — talent income: SKIPPED, and the skip is the finding. Its trigger was "only if stages 1–3 leave the mid-band short," and the mid-band (t4–7) closed at 0.95–1.20 with 78–100% win rates. What remains short is the zone-1 tail (t9 0.87, t10 0.78) and the deliberately-kept boss step (0.70) — one extra point per zone moves party power ~1–2% and cannot close that; spending an economy change there would buy nothing measurable. The 3×11-tier economy invariant stands untouched.
  • Where the target landed: tiers 3–8 sit in the 1.0–1.3 band; t1–t2 overshoot it (1.37/1.42 — the ramp's 3–5 floor makes the opening gentler than the band's letter; designer's call whether gentle onboarding outranks the metric); t9–t10 and the boss remain below. The difficulty line itself — 40% of floor runs reaching the z1 boss, 28% clearing zone 1, 4% full clears, remembering the floor bot never rests while a fight is reachable, never buys, never drinks — is the designer's taste question, flagged not assumed. If the tail wants closing later, the levers are the ramp's slope or the boss band, not spec numbers.
  • Sweep comparability note: the end-of-batch ./sim.sh --sweep 200 still probes fixed budgets 3/6/9/12 unscaled — those rows remain directly comparable to the Batch R and Batch S baselines even though the ladder that FEEDS budgets to real runs changed. The sweep measures kit-vs-budget; the run harness measures the ladder. Do not read one as contradicting the other. The end-of-batch sweep proved the point by replicating Batch S within noise: win% 100/100/100/99 at budgets 3/6/9/12, Cryomancer share 49/43/40/38% — the standalone rows didn't feel Batch T at all, which is exactly what "the run ladder changed, the kits didn't" should look like.
  • Verification: the three correctness gates (hero win scaling, talent spend conservation, enemy tier×slot scaling) were recreated as test_run_harness.gd and re-run green after every stage; a budget-ramp probe pinned the observed rolls per tier (t1 3–5 ... t10 7–9, boss 10–12); a theme satisfiability probe mapped every theme × budget 3–12 × roster before the ramp shipped (that map is what surfaced the elite floor and confirmed plain fights satisfiable at every budget ≥3 in all rosters).

2026-08-01 — Batch S: measure a real run

  • No tuning ships in this batch — like Batch R, it produces numbers. Two new instruments: the sweep now reports damage share and field size per budget, and ./sim.sh --run N plays N complete runs with progression on both sides and reports where they end.
  • Sweep, part 1 — damage share and field size per budget: every budget row now carries average enemy count, average enemies still alive entering round 3 (the field-size proxy AoE actually feeds on), and each hero's damage share at that budget — the previous report pooled share across the whole sweep, so an AoE spec's share was one number smeared across every field size. Because DOD_SIM_THEME narrows the sweep to one theme, a themed sweep now reads out per-theme damage share for free — the follow-up tool for whichever answer the field-size breakdown gives. The full report: 
    ===== DAWN OF DECAY — DIFFICULTY SWEEP =====
